It consists of the [electromagnetic stress-energy tensor](electromagnetism.md#electromagnetic-stress-energy-tensor) plus the quadratic mass contribution $m^2(A_aA_b-g_{ab}A^2/2)$.

#### Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton theory

↑ **Parent:** [Einstein-Hilbert action](#einstein-hilbert-action)

Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton theory couples the metric to a scalar field and a Maxwell field with scalar-dependent gauge coupling, typically through $e^{\alpha\Phi}F_{ab}F^{ab}$. Its Maxwell equation is $\nabla_a(e^{\alpha\Phi}F^{ab})=0$.

##### Magnetically charged dilaton black hole

↑ **Parent:** [Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton theory](#einstein-maxwell-dilaton-theory)

The unit-coupling magnetic solution of [Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton theory](#einstein-maxwell-dilaton-theory) has $ds^2=-Vdt^2+V^{-1}dr^2+R^2d\Omega_2^2$, magnetic potential $A=P\cos\theta\,d\phi$, and dilaton $\phi=-\tfrac12\log(1-r_-/r)$, where $r_-=2P^2/r_+$ and $r_+>0$. Its physical asymptotic component has $r>r_-$. For $0\le r_-<r_+$ it has a regular [Killing horizon](#killing-horizon) at $r_+$, area $4\pi r_+(r_+-r_-)$ and [surface gravity](#surface-gravity) $1/(2r_+)$. Its asymptotic mass is $r_+/2$. For $r_->r_+$ the zero-area singularity is naked. The limiting case $r_-=r_+$ is singular and must not be treated as a regular nondegenerate horizon.

###### Causal structure of the magnetic dilaton black hole

↑ **Parent:** [Magnetically charged dilaton black hole](#magnetically-charged-dilaton-black-hole)

For $r_-<r_+$, an ingoing chart with $v=t+r_*$ has metric $-Vdv^2+2dv\,dr+R^2d\Omega_2^2$. In its future interior, $\nabla r=\partial_v+V\partial_r$ is future timelike, so every future causal curve decreases $r$ and cannot reach [future null infinity](#future-null-infinity). The singularity $r=r_-$ is spacelike. A [Kruskal–Szekeres coordinates](#kruskal-szekeres-coordinates) extension has the same qualitative [Penrose diagram](#penrose-diagram) as [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime), with two exterior regions, a future black-hole interior and a past [white hole](#white-hole) interior. The past interior has the same radial interval but is not a [black hole](#black-hole) region; specifying the radial interval alone does not identify the future component. For $r_->r_+$, the physical exterior $r>r_-$ has $V>0$ everywhere and outgoing null rays escape from arbitrarily near a timelike [naked singularity](#naked-singularity).

###### Geodesic potential of the magnetic dilaton black hole

↑ **Parent:** [Magnetically charged dilaton black hole](#magnetically-charged-dilaton-black-hole)

Rotate any [geodesic](riemannian-geometry.md#geodesic) into an equatorial plane of the [magnetically charged dilaton black hole](#magnetically-charged-dilaton-black-hole). Conserved energy and angular momentum are $E=V\dot t$ and $L=R^2\dot\phi$. Normalizing its tangent to $g(\dot x,\dot x)=-\epsilon$, with $\epsilon=1,0,-1$ for timelike, null and spacelike geodesics, gives $\dot r^2=E^2-V(\epsilon+L^2/R^2)$. Thus its radial equation is $\dot r^2/2+\widetilde V=0$. Radial null rays have $\dot r=\pm E$ and use the same [Schwarzschild tortoise coordinate](#schwarzschild-tortoise-coordinate) with $2M$ replaced by $r_+$.

###### Extremal dilaton metric photon capture

↑ **Parent:** [Geodesic potential of the magnetic dilaton black hole](#geodesic-potential-of-the-magnetic-dilaton-black-hole)

For $f=1-2M/r$ and squared areal radius $C=r(r-b)$, an equatorial [null geodesic](special-relativity.md#null-geodesic) obeys $\dot r^2=E^2[1-B^2f/C]$, with [impact parameter](classical-mechanics.md#impact-parameter) $B=L/E$. At $b=2M$, $f/C=1/r^2$ in the exterior. Its supremum is $1/(4M^2)$ at the null singular boundary, rather than at a regular exterior [photon sphere](#photon-sphere). Rays with $B>2M$ turn at $r=B$; rays with $B<2M$ reach the boundary. Assuming those rays are absorbed gives the displayed geometric-optics cross-section despite zero limiting horizon area.

###### Komar mass of the magnetic dilaton black hole

↑ **Parent:** [Magnetically charged dilaton black hole](#magnetically-charged-dilaton-black-hole)

For the [magnetically charged dilaton black hole](#magnetically-charged-dilaton-black-hole), the metric-dual one-form of $k=\partial_t$ is $k^\flat=-Vdt$. With orientation $dt\wedge dr\wedge d\theta\wedge d\phi$, its [Hodge star operator](differential-form.md#hodge-star-operator) gives $\star dk^\flat=-V'R^2\sin\theta\,d\theta\wedge d\phi$. Therefore the [Komar mass](#komar-mass) is $R^2V'/2=r_+/2-P^2/r$. Its radial dependence is allowed because the [Ricci tensor](#ricci-tensor) is nonzero; the divergence of $dk^\flat$ is sourced by $R_{ab}k^b$. At the horizon $M_K(r_+)=(r_+-r_-)/2=\kappa A_H/(4\pi)$.

### Trace-reversed Einstein field equations in D dimensions

↑ **Parent:** [Einstein field equations](#einstein-field-equations)

For $D>2$, tracing and substituting back gives

$$
R_{\mu\nu}
=\kappa\left(T_{\mu\nu}-\frac1{D-2}g_{\mu\nu}T\right).
$$

#### Ricci curvature sourced by a Klein-Gordon scalar field

↑ **Parent:** [Trace-reversed Einstein field equations in D dimensions](#trace-reversed-einstein-field-equations-in-d-dimensions)

For the conserved scalar stress tensor above,

$$
R_{\mu\nu}
=\frac\kappa2\left[
\nabla_\mu\phi\nabla_\nu\phi
+\frac{m^2}{D-2}g_{\mu\nu}\phi^2
\right].
$$

### Metric variation

↑ **Parent:** [Einstein field equations](#einstein-field-equations)

For $h_{ab}=\delta g_{ab}$, variation of the inverse metric and metric volume form gives

$$
\delta g^{ab}=-h^{ab},
\qquad
\delta(d\operatorname{vol}_g)=\frac12g^{ab}h_{ab}\,d\operatorname{vol}_g.
$$

The [Levi-Civita connection](#levi-civita-connection) varies by

$$
\delta\Gamma^a{}_{bc}=\frac12g^{ad}
(\nabla_bh_{cd}+\nabla_ch_{bd}-\nabla_dh_{bc}).
$$

#### Palatini identity

↑ **Parent:** [Metric variation](#metric-variation)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palatini_identity)

For the [Levi-Civita connection](#levi-civita-connection), the difference of two connections is a tensor. Varying the coordinate expression for the [Ricci tensor](#ricci-tensor) then gives the displayed covariant identity. It is the starting point for the [metric variation of scalar curvature](#metric-variation-of-scalar-curvature).

#### Metric variation of scalar curvature

↑ **Parent:** [Metric variation](#metric-variation)

For $h_{ab}=\delta g_{ab}$, the [Ricci scalar](#ricci-scalar) varies as

$$
\delta R=-R^{ab}h_{ab}+\nabla_a\nabla_bh^{ab}-\nabla^a\nabla_ah,
\qquad h=g^{ab}h_{ab}.
$$

The last two terms form a [covariant divergence](#covariant-divergence) and hence contribute only a boundary term to a compactly supported variation of the [Einstein-Hilbert action](#einstein-hilbert-action).

### Modified gravity

↑ **Parent:** [Einstein field equations](#einstein-field-equations)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modified_gravity)

Modified gravity changes the Einstein-Hilbert action or its field equations in order to produce gravitational dynamics different from general relativity.

#### Scalar-tensor theory

↑ **Parent:** [Modified gravity](#modified-gravity)

A [scalar-tensor theory](#scalar-tensor-theory) uses a [metric tensor](#metric-tensor) and an additional [scalar field](quantum-field-theory.md#scalar-field) to mediate gravity. A [scalar](vector-space.md#scalar) multiplier of the [Ricci scalar](#ricci-scalar) makes the [curvature](differential-geometry.md#curvature) coupling depend on that field. The [Brans-Dicke theory](#brans-dicke-theory) is a standard example; the choice of [scalar](vector-space.md#scalar) potential and kinetic coupling distinguishes different members of the class.

##### Metric variation of a scalar-curvature coupling

↑ **Parent:** [Scalar-tensor theory](#scalar-tensor-theory)

With the [curvature sign convention](#curvature-sign-convention) in which $\delta R_{bc}=\nabla_c\delta\Gamma^a{}_{ba}-\nabla_a\delta\Gamma^a{}_{bc}$, the inverse-metric [variation](calculus-of-variations.md#variation) of $\int\sqrt{-g}\,\Phi R$ is $\int\sqrt{-g}\,E_{ab}\delta g^{ab}$ plus a [boundary term](calculus.md#boundary-term), for a [scalar](vector-space.md#scalar) multiplier held fixed in that variation. The [Palatini identity](#palatini-identity) expresses the [curvature](differential-geometry.md#curvature) variation as divergences of $\delta\Gamma$. Two [integrations by parts](calculus.md#integration-by-parts) move the [derivatives](calculus.md#derivative) onto $\Phi$, yielding the displayed terms. For constant $\Phi$ they vanish. The opposite [curvature](differential-geometry.md#curvature) convention instead has $E_{ab}=\Phi G_{ab}+g_{ab}\Box\Phi-\nabla_a\nabla_b\Phi$.

##### Brans-Dicke theory

↑ **Parent:** [Scalar-tensor theory](#scalar-tensor-theory)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brans–Dicke_theory)

The [Brans-Dicke theory](#brans-dicke-theory) adds a dynamical [scalar field](quantum-field-theory.md#scalar-field) $\Phi$ to a [metric tensor](#metric-tensor) theory of gravity, with constant dimensionless coupling $\omega$. In a $(+---)$ signature and the reversed-curvature convention used here, the displayed Lagrangian is supplemented by matter and integrated against the [metric tensor](#metric-tensor) volume density with prefactor $1/(16\pi G)$. Its [scalar](vector-space.md#scalar) multiplier of the [Ricci scalar](#ricci-scalar) lets the [curvature](differential-geometry.md#curvature) coupling vary in spacetime. Both the [metric tensor](#metric-tensor) and [scalar](vector-space.md#scalar) are varied; the [scalar equation of Brans-Dicke theory](#scalar-equation-of-brans-dicke-theory) follows by eliminating [curvature](differential-geometry.md#curvature) between the [scalar](vector-space.md#scalar) equation and the [trace](linear-algebra.md#matrix-trace) of the [metric tensor](#metric-tensor) equation. The original construction is described in [Brans and Dicke's 1961 article](https://doi.org/10.1103/PhysRev.124.925).

###### Scalar equation of Brans-Dicke theory

↑ **Parent:** [Brans-Dicke theory](#brans-dicke-theory)

Assume four spacetime dimensions, $\Phi\ne0$, constant $\omega$, no [scalar](vector-space.md#scalar) potential and matter independent of $\Phi$. In the stated action convention, [scalar](vector-space.md#scalar) [variation](calculus-of-variations.md#variation) gives $\Phi R+\omega\Phi^{-1}(\nabla\Phi)^2-2\omega\Box\Phi=0$. The [trace](linear-algebra.md#matrix-trace) of the [metric tensor](#metric-tensor) equation gives $\Phi R+\omega\Phi^{-1}(\nabla\Phi)^2+3\Box\Phi=8\pi G T$. Their subtraction proves the displayed [wave equation](wave-equation.md) with $T=g^{ab}T_{ab}$. The [degenerate coupling of Brans-Dicke theory](#degenerate-coupling-of-brans-dicke-theory) needs separate treatment before dividing by its coefficient.

###### Degenerate coupling of Brans-Dicke theory

↑ **Parent:** [Scalar equation of Brans-Dicke theory](#scalar-equation-of-brans-dicke-theory)

At $\omega=-3/2$, the combined [scalar equation of Brans-Dicke theory](#scalar-equation-of-brans-dicke-theory) becomes the matter consistency condition $T=0$. It does not determine $\Box\Phi$ by division, since the wave coefficient has vanished. The original [metric tensor](#metric-tensor) and [scalar](vector-space.md#scalar) [Euler-Lagrange equations](analysis.md#euler-lagrange-equation) must be retained. Introducing scalar-dependent matter or a [scalar](vector-space.md#scalar) potential changes the consistency condition.

<h4 id="f-r-gravity">f(R) gravity</h4>

↑ **Parent:** [Modified gravity](#modified-gravity)

An f(R) theory replaces the Ricci scalar in the gravitational action by a smooth function $f(R)$. Its metric field equation is

$$
f'(R)R_{ab}-\frac12f(R)g_{ab}
+(g_{ab}\Box-\nabla_a\nabla_b)f'(R)=8\pi T_{ab}.
$$

<h5 id="einstein-frame-representation-of-metric-f-r-gravity">Einstein-frame representation of metric f(R) gravity</h5>

↑ **Parent:** [f(R) gravity](#f-r-gravity)

In the mostly-minus convention with vacuum gravitational action proportional to $\int\sqrt{-g}f(R)$ and $f'<0$, the displayed [conformal transformation](geometry-and-topology.md#conformal-map) turns its vacuum equation into $\widetilde G_{\mu\nu}=T_{\mu\nu}(\phi)$, with potential $V=(Rf'-f)/(2f'^2)$ and the [stress-energy tensor of a canonical scalar field](#stress-energy-tensor-of-a-canonical-scalar-field). The conformal Ricci formula cancels the second derivatives of $f'$ against the original field equation. Its remaining derivative tensor is $\partial_\mu\phi\partial_\nu\phi-\widetilde g_{\mu\nu}(\widetilde\nabla\phi)^2/2$. Expressing $V$ as a function of $\phi$ requires a locally invertible $R\mapsto f'(R)$. When $f''=0$, this scalar-tensor parametrization degenerates and introduces no new propagating scalar degree of freedom.

<h5 id="einstein-metric-condition-in-f-r-gravity">Einstein metric condition in f(R) gravity</h5>

↑ **Parent:** [f(R) gravity](#f-r-gravity)

Every four-dimensional Einstein metric with $R_{ab}=\Lambda g_{ab}$ has constant [Ricci scalar](#ricci-scalar) $R=4\Lambda$. The vacuum metric [f(R) gravity](#f-r-gravity) tensor reduces to $(\Lambda f'(4\Lambda)-f(4\Lambda)/2)g_{ab}$, giving the displayed necessary and sufficient condition for a fixed [cosmological constant](cosmology.md#cosmological-constant). Both function and derivative may vanish, so division by $f'$ is unnecessary. Requiring this for all real $\Lambda$ and a smooth $f$ instead yields $f(R)=CR^2$.

<h5 id="normalization-of-the-metric-f-r-field-equation">Normalization of the metric f(R) field equation</h5>

↑ **Parent:** [f(R) gravity](#f-r-gravity)

For a metric action $S_g=A\int\sqrt{-g}f(R)\,d^4x$ and the usual [stress-energy tensor](#stress-energy-tensor) convention, covariant metric variation gives $A E_{ab}=T_{ab}/2$, where $E_{ab}=f'R_{ab}-fg_{ab}/2+(g_{ab}\Box-\nabla_a\nabla_b)f'$. With $A=1/(16\pi G)$ this becomes $E_{ab}=8\pi G T_{ab}$; with $A=1$ it becomes $E_{ab}=T_{ab}/2$. The chosen normalization does not alter the vacuum equation.

<h5 id="linearized-f-r-gravity-about-minkowski-spacetime">Linearized f(R) gravity about Minkowski spacetime</h5>

↑ **Parent:** [f(R) gravity](#f-r-gravity)

If $f(0)=0$, $f'(0)=1$, and $f''(0)=0$, then $f(R)=R+O(R^3)$ near zero. The field equations linearized about [Minkowski spacetime](special-relativity.md#minkowski-spacetime) are therefore the [Linearized Einstein equations](#linearized-einstein-equations); deviations first appear beyond linear order.

## Linearized gravity

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linearized_gravity)

Linearized gravity writes $g_{\mu\nu}=\eta_{\mu\nu}+h_{\mu\nu}$ with $|h_{\mu\nu}|\ll1$ and discards terms quadratic in $h$ and its derivatives.

### Gauge-invariant scalar-vector-tensor perturbations of Minkowski spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Linearized gravity](#linearized-gravity)

For signature $(+---)$, decompose a [metric perturbation](#linearized-gravity) into $h_{00}=2A$, $h_{0i}=\partial_iB+B_i$ and $h_{ij}=2C\delta_{ij}+2(\partial_i\partial_j-\delta_{ij}\Delta/3)E+\partial_iE_j+\partial_jE_i+h_{ij}^{\rm TT}$, where the spatial vectors are divergence-free and $h^{\rm TT}$ is a [transverse-traceless tensor](#transverse-traceless-tensor). Under a passive coordinate shift $\xi^0=\tau$, $\xi^i=\partial_iL+L_i$, with $\partial_iL_i=0$, the changes are $\delta A=-\dot\tau$, $\delta B=\dot L-\tau$, $\delta C=\Delta L/3$, $\delta E=L$, $\delta B_i=\dot L_i$, and $\delta E_i=L_i$. The displayed combinations and $h^{\rm TT}$ are therefore gauge invariant. The decomposition is unique after appropriate spatial decay or mode restrictions; homogeneous and boundary modes require separate conventions.

#### Vacuum constraints for gauge-invariant Minkowski perturbations

↑ **Parent:** [Gauge-invariant scalar-vector-tensor perturbations of Minkowski spacetime](#gauge-invariant-scalar-vector-tensor-perturbations-of-minkowski-spacetime)

With the reversed convention in [curvature-sign convention in Killing derivative identities](#curvature-sign-convention-in-killing-derivative-identities), choose the representative $h_{00}=2\Phi$, $h_{0i}=V_i$, $h_{ij}=2\Psi\delta_{ij}+h_{ij}^{\rm TT}$. The linearized Einstein components are $G_{00}^{(1)}=-2\Delta\Psi$, $G_{0i}^{(1)}=-2\partial_i\dot\Psi-\Delta V_i/2$ and $G_{ij}^{(1)}=(\partial_i\partial_j-\delta_{ij}\Delta)(\Phi-\Psi)-2\delta_{ij}\ddot\Psi-(\partial_i\dot V_j+\partial_j\dot V_i)/2+\Box h_{ij}^{\rm TT}/2$. On all space with regular decaying fields, or for nonzero spatial Fourier modes, the vacuum constraints remove the scalar and vector parts, leaving two radiative [gravitational wave polarizations](#gravitational-wave-polarization). Singular Coulomb fields, spatial boundaries and homogeneous modes are not covered by that removal argument.

### Static scalar perturbation of Minkowski spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Linearized gravity](#linearized-gravity)

For static scalar perturbations with no mixed time-space or spatial shear terms, the displayed [metric tensor](#metric-tensor) uses signature $(+---)$. With the reversed convention in [curvature-sign convention in Killing derivative identities](#curvature-sign-convention-in-killing-derivative-identities), its first-order [Einstein tensor](#einstein-tensor) is $\delta G_{00}=2\Delta C$, $\delta G_{0i}=0$, and $\delta G_{ij}=\partial_i\partial_j(A+C)-\delta_{ij}\Delta(A+C)$. These follow by substituting the metric into the first-order [Christoffel symbols](riemannian-geometry.md#christoffel-symbol) and [Ricci tensor](#ricci-tensor), discarding products of perturbations. A static source with the [dust stress-energy tensor](#dust-stress-energy-tensor) obeys $\Delta C=-4\pi G\rho$ and the decaying spatial equation makes $A=-C$. For a point mass the [Newtonian potential of a point mass](classical-mechanics.md#newtonian-potential-of-a-point-mass) gives $A=-GM/r$, $C=GM/r$. The spatial potential is necessary to determine light propagation; a time potential alone is insufficient.

### Linearized Schwarzschild metric

↑ **Parent:** [Linearized gravity](#linearized-gravity)

For a static spherical point source, the weak-field solution in isotropic radius $\rho$ has $g_{tt}=1-2GM/\rho$ and spatial metric $-(1+2GM/\rho)(d\rho^2+\rho^2d\Omega^2)$. The [isotropic-to-areal weak-field radius change](#isotropic-to-areal-weak-field-radius-change) gives the displayed metric with $r_S=2GM$. Its validity requires $r_S/r\ll1$. Treating it as an exact metric near $r\sim r_S$ can turn omitted terms into large corrections; it does not establish horizons or strong-field photon dynamics.

#### Weak-field photon-orbit validity

↑ **Parent:** [Linearized Schwarzschild metric](#linearized-schwarzschild-metric)

The first-order null-orbit equation $(u')^2=r_S^2E^2/h^2-u^2(1-u)$ formally has a circular solution at $u=2/3$, with $|h/E|=3\sqrt3r_S/2$. Its second-order equation gives a perturbation $\delta u''=\delta u$, hence instability. However, $u=2/3$ is outside the weak-field expansion. The exact [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime) has a corresponding unstable [photon sphere](#photon-sphere), but that physical result needs the exact geometry; extrapolating the linearized metric is not a controlled proof.

#### Isotropic-to-areal weak-field radius change

↑ **Parent:** [Linearized Schwarzschild metric](#linearized-schwarzschild-metric)

The area of a symmetry sphere in the weak-field isotropic metric is $4\pi\rho^2(1+2GM/\rho)$. Equating it to $4\pi r^2$ defines the [areal radius](#areal-radius) and gives the displayed change. To first order $dr=d\rho$, and replacing $1/\rho$ by $1/r$ changes the perturbation only at second order. This explains why the isotropic angular correction disappears in the [linearized Schwarzschild metric](#linearized-schwarzschild-metric) without discarding spatial curvature.

### Quadratic gravitational effective stress-energy tensor

↑ **Parent:** [Linearized gravity](#linearized-gravity)

Expand the [Einstein tensor](#einstein-tensor) about the [Minkowski metric](special-relativity.md#minkowski-metric). The quadratic term in the [metric perturbation](#linearized-gravity) defines $t_{\mu\nu}=-G^{(2)}_{\mu\nu}[h,h]/(8\pi)$, a symmetric tensor relative to the background. For a first-order vacuum solution, the expanded [contracted Bianchi identity](#contracted-bianchi-identity) gives $\partial^\mu t_{\mu\nu}=0$. Equivalently, a second-order correction $j$ obeys $G^{(1)}[j]=8\pi t$, whose flat divergence vanishes identically. This conservation is an on-shell statement. The local $t$ depends on perturbative gauge and the background split; conservation alone does not make it a gauge-independent local gravitational [stress-energy tensor](#stress-energy-tensor).

### Massless Fierz-Pauli action

↑ **Parent:** [Linearized gravity](#linearized-gravity)

A [massless Fierz-Pauli action](#massless-fierz-pauli-action) is the quadratic flat-background action for a symmetric metric perturbation that yields the vacuum [Linearized Einstein equations](#linearized-einstein-equations). One normalization, up to a total derivative, is

$$
\mathcal L=-\frac14\partial_\mu h^{\rho\sigma}\partial^\mu h_{\rho\sigma}
+\frac12A_\mu A^\mu-\frac12A^\mu\partial_\mu h
+\frac14\partial_\mu h\partial^\mu h,\qquad A_\mu=\partial_\nu h^\nu{}_\mu.
$$

Varying the symmetric field with compact support gives the displayed linearized [Einstein tensor](#einstein-tensor) coefficient. Its linearized coordinate-gauge symmetry is consistent with the contracted [Bianchi identity](fiber-bundle.md#bianchi-identity). It is the massless gravitational action, distinct from imposing the massive [Fierz-Pauli equations](relativistic-quantum-field.md#fierz-pauli-equations) with five polarizations.

### Linearized coordinate gauge transformation

↑ **Parent:** [Linearized gravity](#linearized-gravity)

An infinitesimal coordinate change generated by $\xi$ changes a metric perturbation about [Minkowski spacetime](special-relativity.md#minkowski-spacetime) by $h_{\mu\nu}\mapsto h_{\mu\nu}-\partial_\mu\xi_\nu-\partial_\nu\xi_\mu$, in the passive sign convention. For a passive coordinate change $x^\mu\mapsto x^\mu+\xi^\mu$, the first-order change is minus the [Lie derivative of a tensor field](fiber-bundle.md#lie-derivative-of-a-tensor-field) of the background metric, equivalently the pullback by the inverse flow. The [gauge invariance of the linearized Riemann tensor](#gauge-invariance-of-the-linearized-riemann-tensor) follows because the added terms have commuting third derivatives, which cancel in the curvature antisymmetrizations. Gauge choices alter the metric components while preserving linearized tidal effects.

#### Affine transverse trace in a diagonal linearized plane wave is pure gauge

↑ **Parent:** [Linearized coordinate gauge transformation](#linearized-coordinate-gauge-transformation)

For a perturbation $h_{xx}=2a(t-z)$, $h_{yy}=2b(t-z)$ with all other components zero, the linearized [Vacuum Einstein equations](#vacuum-einstein-equations) give $(a+b)''=0$. Put $s=a+b$ and choose $\xi_x=sx/2$, $\xi_y=sy/2$, $\xi_t=-s'(x^2+y^2)/4$, $\xi_z=s'(x^2+y^2)/4$. Under the [linearized coordinate gauge transformation](#linearized-coordinate-gauge-transformation), the mixed time/transverse and longitudinal/transverse components cancel, while the other time/longitudinal changes are proportional to $s''=0$. The remaining components are $h_{xx}=a-b$, $h_{yy}=-(a-b)$, $h_{xy}=0$, a [transverse-traceless gauge](#transverse-traceless-gauge) perturbation of [plus polarization](#plus-polarization). This explicitly removes a nonzero affine trace without assuming it vanishes by boundary conditions. If $(a-b)''=0$ too, all linearized curvature vanishes.

### Weak-field approximation

↑ **Parent:** [Linearized gravity](#linearized-gravity)

The weak-field approximation requires every dimensionless component of the [metric perturbation](#linearized-gravity) to have magnitude much smaller than one, so terms quadratic in the perturbation may be neglected.

### Linearized Einstein equations

↑ **Parent:** [Linearized gravity](#linearized-gravity)

In terms of the [trace-reversed metric perturbation](#trace-reversed-metric-perturbation) and in the [Lorenz gauge in linearized gravity](#lorenz-gauge-in-linearized-gravity), the linearized Einstein equations on Minkowski spacetime are

$$
\Box\bar h_{\mu\nu}=-16\pi T_{\mu\nu}.
$$

#### Parallel photon beams in linearized gravity

↑ **Parent:** [Linearized Einstein equations](#linearized-einstein-equations)

For signature $(+---)$ and the curvature convention giving $\Box\bar h_{ik}=-16\pi G\epsilon^{-1}T_{ik}$, take $u=t-x$, $\ell_i=(1,-1,0,0)$ and $T_{ik}=\epsilon\rho(u)\ell_i\ell_k$. A trace-free Lorenz-gauge particular solution has the displayed form. The vector $\ell^i=(1,1,0,0)$ is null, and $\Gamma^i{}_{jk}\ell^j\ell^k=0$, so co-propagating test photons are undeflected by this beam field. For a transversely uniform source, $H=4\pi G\rho(u)(y^2+z^2)$ is one particular solution. A function of $u$ alone has vanishing wave operator and cannot source nonzero $\rho$; homogeneous gravitational perturbations and boundary conditions remain separate data.

#### Radiation boundary condition for linearized gravity

↑ **Parent:** [Linearized Einstein equations](#linearized-einstein-equations)

The retarded source solution of the [Linearized Einstein equations](#linearized-einstein-equations) is selected by excluding added incoming or freely specified homogeneous [gravitational waves](#gravitational-wave). A homogeneous wave can satisfy [Lorenz gauge in linearized gravity](#lorenz-gauge-in-linearized-gravity) while having no source [mass quadrupole moment](#mass-quadrupole-moment). Thus a formula for the retarded source field is not an identity for every solution of the sourced wave equation.

#### Retarded quadrupole field

↑ **Parent:** [Linearized Einstein equations](#linearized-einstein-equations)

For a compact slowly moving source in the [weak-field approximation](#weak-field-approximation), with no incoming homogeneous wave and $G=c=1$, the retarded [Linearized Einstein equations](#linearized-einstein-equations) give this leading far-zone [trace-reversed metric perturbation](#trace-reversed-metric-perturbation). The key consequence of [stress-energy conservation](#stress-energy-conservation) is $\ddot I_{ij}=2\int T_{ij}\,d^3x$. The physical [gravitational wave](#gravitational-wave) is obtained by the [transverse-traceless projector](#transverse-traceless-projector).

##### Long-wavelength source approximation

↑ **Parent:** [Retarded quadrupole field](#retarded-quadrupole-field)

Dropping the position-dependent source delay in the leading far-zone retarded field requires source size $d$ to be small compared with its variation time $\tau$, in units $c=1$. The [retarded quadrupole field](#retarded-quadrupole-field) also requires $d/r\ll1$. For a typical nonrelativistic bound source, $\tau\sim d/v$ implies this long-wavelength condition; an arbitrarily rapidly oscillating small displacement is not controlled merely by its instantaneous speed.

### Linearized inverse metric

↑ **Parent:** [Linearized gravity](#linearized-gravity)

If $g_{\mu\nu}=\eta_{\mu\nu}+h_{\mu\nu}$, then $g^{\mu\nu}=\eta^{\mu\nu}-h^{\mu\nu}+O(h^2)$, with indices on $h$ raised using the background metric.

### Linearized Levi-Civita connection

↑ **Parent:** [Linearized gravity](#linearized-gravity)

About a Cartesian flat background,

$$
\Gamma^\alpha_{\beta\gamma}
=\frac12\eta^{\alpha\rho}
(\partial_\beta h_{\gamma\rho}+\partial_\gamma h_{\beta\rho}-\partial_\rho h_{\beta\gamma})+O(h^2).
$$

### Linearized Riemann curvature operator

↑ **Parent:** [Linearized gravity](#linearized-gravity)

The linearized Riemann tensor defines an operator on two-forms by

$$
(\mathcal R\omega)_{ab}=\frac12R^{(1)}_{ab}{}^{cd}\omega_{cd}.
$$

Unlike the metric perturbation itself, this operator is invariant under linearized gauge transformations.

#### Gauge invariance of the linearized Riemann tensor

↑ **Parent:** [Linearized Riemann curvature operator](#linearized-riemann-curvature-operator)

Under $h_{\mu\nu}\mapsto h_{\mu\nu}+\partial_\mu\xi_\nu+\partial_\nu\xi_\mu$, every contribution to the linearized Riemann tensor contains three commuting derivatives of $\xi$ and cancels pairwise. Linearized curvature is therefore gauge invariant about flat spacetime.

##### Flat linearized metric perturbations are locally pure gauge

↑ **Parent:** [Gauge invariance of the linearized Riemann tensor](#gauge-invariance-of-the-linearized-riemann-tensor)

On a [contractible](algebraic-topology.md#contractible-space) Cartesian patch of a flat background, zero [linearized Riemann curvature operator](#linearized-riemann-curvature-operator) says that the [connection 1-forms](connection-1-form.md) $\Gamma^{(1)a}{}_{bc}\,dx^c$ are closed. The [Poincaré lemma](differential-form.md#poincare-lemma) writes them as [derivatives](calculus.md#derivative) of a matrix-valued potential. Symmetry of the two lower [affine connection](fiber-bundle.md#affine-connection) indices then supplies a second potential, giving $\Gamma^{(1)a}{}_{bc}=\partial_b\partial_c\xi^a$. Linearized [metric compatibility](fiber-bundle.md#metric-compatibility) implies that $h_{ab}-2\partial_{(a}\xi_{b)}$ is constant. Its symmetric constant part is absorbed by an affine [vector field](calculus.md#vector-field). Thus the [metric perturbation](#linearized-gravity) is locally a coordinate change. Global [topology](topology.md) and imposed [boundary conditions](differential-equation.md#boundary-condition) can obstruct the corresponding global gauge choice.

### Trace-reversed metric perturbation

↑ **Parent:** [Linearized gravity](#linearized-gravity)

In four dimensions, the trace-reversed perturbation is

$$
\bar h_{\mu\nu}=h_{\mu\nu}-\frac12\eta_{\mu\nu}h,
\qquad h=\eta^{\rho\sigma}h_{\rho\sigma}.
$$

Trace reversal simplifies the linearized Einstein equations.

#### Lorenz gauge in linearized gravity

↑ **Parent:** [Trace-reversed metric perturbation](#trace-reversed-metric-perturbation)

The Lorenz condition $\partial^\mu\bar h_{\mu\nu}=0$ reduces the vacuum field equation to $\Box\bar h_{\mu\nu}=0$. A plane-wave amplitude therefore satisfies $k^\mu H_{\mu\nu}=0$ and $k^\mu k_\mu=0$.

##### Residual gauge symmetry of linearized gravity

↑ **Parent:** [Lorenz gauge in linearized gravity](#lorenz-gauge-in-linearized-gravity)

An infinitesimal coordinate change generated by $\xi_\mu$ changes

$$
\bar h_{\mu\nu}\mapsto
\bar h_{\mu\nu}-\partial_\mu\xi_\nu-\partial_\nu\xi_\mu
+\eta_{\mu\nu}\partial_\rho\xi^\rho.
$$

It preserves Lorenz gauge exactly when $\Box\xi_\mu=0$.

##### Plane gravitational wave in linearized gravity

↑ **Parent:** [Lorenz gauge in linearized gravity](#lorenz-gauge-in-linearized-gravity)

A vacuum mode $\bar h_{\mu\nu}=H_{\mu\nu}e^{ik\cdot x}$ has a null wavevector and a transverse symmetric amplitude. Residual gauge freedom can further reduce it to two physical transverse-traceless polarizations.

###### Transverse-traceless gauge

↑ **Parent:** [Plane gravitational wave in linearized gravity](#plane-gravitational-wave-in-linearized-gravity)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transverse-traceless_gauge)

Transverse-traceless gauge sets the time and longitudinal components and the spatial trace of a vacuum gravitational-wave perturbation to zero.

###### Explicit plane-wave reduction to transverse-traceless gauge

↑ **Parent:** [Transverse-traceless gauge](#transverse-traceless-gauge)

For a [plane gravitational wave in linearized gravity](#plane-gravitational-wave-in-linearized-gravity) with $k_\mu=(-\omega,0,0,\omega)$, [Lorenz gauge in linearized gravity](#lorenz-gauge-in-linearized-gravity) gives $H_{3\nu}=-H_{0\nu}$. Under $\delta H_{\mu\nu}=i(k_\mu X_\nu+k_\nu X_\mu-\eta_{\mu\nu}k^\rho X_\rho)$, choose $X_i=H_{0i}/(i\omega)$ for $i=1,2$, $X_3-X_0=iH_{00}/\omega$, and $X_0+X_3=(H_{11}+H_{22})/(2i\omega)$. This [residual gauge symmetry of linearized gravity](#residual-gauge-symmetry-of-linearized-gravity) removes all time and longitudinal entries and the transverse trace. The remaining amplitudes $H_+,H_\times$ are the two [gravitational wave polarizations](#gravitational-wave-polarization). The formula uses the plus-sign convention for $\delta h=2\partial_{(\mu}\xi_{\nu)}$; reversing the definition of $\xi$ reverses the gauge change.

###### TT coordinate separation and measured separation

↑ **Parent:** [Transverse-traceless gauge](#transverse-traceless-gauge)

In [transverse-traceless gauge](#transverse-traceless-gauge), particles initially at rest have fixed spatial coordinates because $\Gamma^\mu{}_{00}=0$. Their separation measured in a [parallel-propagated orthonormal frame](#parallel-propagated-orthonormal-frame) changes: to first order $S^{\hat A}=S^A+h_{AB}S^B/2$. Hence the physical [geodesic deviation](#geodesic-deviation) is $\ddot\xi^{\hat A}=\ddot h_{AB}L^B/2$, whereas the coordinate displacement obeys $\ddot\xi^A_{\rm coord}=0$. The extra $\partial_0\Gamma^A{}_{0B}L^B$ term in the coordinate expansion of the second [covariant derivative](#covariant-derivative) reconciles the two results. This applies to infinitesimal separations and linear [metric perturbations](#linearized-gravity).

###### Sliding-bead gravitational wave detector

↑ **Parent:** [TT coordinate separation and measured separation](#tt-coordinate-separation-and-measured-separation)

A short rigid nonrotating guide constrains two freely sliding beads to one axis $n$. In its [parallel-propagated orthonormal frame](#parallel-propagated-orthonormal-frame), the axial [geodesic deviation](#geodesic-deviation) is $\ddot L=-R_{n0n0}L$. For a transverse [gravitational wave](#gravitational-wave), $R_{n0n0}=-\epsilon\ddot h_{nn}/2$, so a wave incident after the beads are at rest changes the measured separation by $\epsilon h_{nn}L_0/2$ to first order. Transverse reaction forces do not supply an axial restoring force. Alignment with the propagation direction gives no first-order length change.

###### Gravitational wave polarization

↑ **Parent:** [Transverse-traceless gauge](#transverse-traceless-gauge)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gravitational_wave_polarization)

A gravitational wave in four-dimensional general relativity has two independent linear polarizations, conventionally called plus and cross. A rotation by angle $\vartheta$ in the transverse plane rotates their pair by $2\vartheta$.

###### Transverse-traceless tensor

↑ **Parent:** [Gravitational wave polarization](#gravitational-wave-polarization)

A transverse-traceless spatial tensor is orthogonal to its [Fourier mode](fourier-analysis.md#fourier-mode) wavevector in each index and has zero trace. The [transverse-traceless projector](#transverse-traceless-projector) extracts this part. Trace terms and symmetrized coordinate-displacement gradients have no such part, explaining the linear gauge invariance of [tensor cosmological perturbations](linear-cosmological-perturbation-theory.md#tensor-cosmological-perturbation).

###### Plus polarization

↑ **Parent:** [Gravitational wave polarization](#gravitational-wave-polarization)

A [gravitational wave](#gravitational-wave) of plus polarization has opposite transverse strain or [geodesic deviation](#geodesic-deviation) [eigenvalues](linear-operator-theory.md#eigenvalue) along two fixed perpendicular axes. For a sinusoidal [periodic function](function.md#periodic-function) profile, the stretching and compressing roles alternate every half-period.

###### Cross polarization

↑ **Parent:** [Gravitational wave polarization](#gravitational-wave-polarization)

The cross polarization is the plus pattern rotated through $45$ degrees in the transverse plane.

###### Transverse-traceless projector

↑ **Parent:** [Gravitational wave polarization](#gravitational-wave-polarization)

The transverse-traceless projector maps a symmetric tensor to the part orthogonal to a chosen wavevector and with zero trace. It is the identity on physical gravitational-wave polarizations.

###### Polarization completeness relation

↑ **Parent:** [Transverse-traceless projector](#transverse-traceless-projector)

A polarization completeness relation expresses a projector as a sum of outer products of an orthonormal polarization basis. For transverse-traceless tensors, $\sum_s\epsilon_{ij}^s\epsilon_{mn}^{s*}$ is the transverse-traceless projector up to the chosen normalization.

### Linearized Ricci tensor and scalar

↑ **Parent:** [Linearized gravity](#linearized-gravity)

To first order around flat spacetime,

$$
R_{\mu\nu}
=\frac12\left(
\partial_\rho\partial_\mu h^\rho{}_{\nu}
+\partial_\rho\partial_\nu h^\rho{}_{\mu}
-\mathop{\Box}h_{\mu\nu}
-\partial_\mu\partial_\nu h
\right),
$$

and

$$
R=\partial_\mu\partial_\nu h^{\mu\nu}-\mathop{\Box}h,
\qquad h=\eta^{\mu\nu}h_{\mu\nu}.
$$

### Newtonian limit

↑ **Parent:** [Linearized gravity](#linearized-gravity)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newtonian_limit)

For a weak, static field produced by nonrelativistic matter, $T_{00}=\rho$ is the dominant stress-energy component. The field equations and the slow-particle [geodesic equation](riemannian-geometry.md#geodesic-equation) imply

$$
h_{00}=-2\Phi,
\qquad
\kappa=8\pi G,
$$

where the [Poisson equation](partial-differential-equation.md#poisson-equation) is $\nabla^2\Phi=4\pi G\rho$.

#### Newtonian connection from an exponential lapse

↑ **Parent:** [Newtonian limit](#newtonian-limit)

For smooth spatial $\phi$ and $g(c)=-c^2e^{2\phi/c^2}dt^2+\delta_{ij}dx^idx^j$, the only nonzero [Christoffel symbols](riemannian-geometry.md#christoffel-symbol) are $\Gamma^t{}_{ti}=\Gamma^t{}_{it}=c^{-2}\partial_i\phi$ and $\Gamma^i{}_{tt}=e^{2\phi/c^2}\partial_i\phi$. Consequently the [Levi-Civita connection](#levi-civita-connection) converges smoothly on compact sets as $c\to\infty$ although the metric itself diverges in these coordinates. The limiting [torsion-free connection](fiber-bundle.md#torsion-free-connection) has only $\Gamma^i{}_{tt}=\partial_i\phi$ and gives $d^2x^i/dt^2=-\partial_i\phi$ when $t$ is an [affine parameter](riemannian-geometry.md#affine-parameter). With $R^a{}_{bcd}=\partial_c\Gamma^a{}_{db}-\partial_d\Gamma^a{}_{cb}+\cdots$, its [Ricci tensor](#ricci-tensor) is $(\Delta\phi)dt\otimes dt$: quadratic connection terms vanish and $R^i{}_{tjt}=\partial_j\partial_i\phi$. Thus zero Ricci tensor is the [Laplace equation](partial-differential-equation.md#laplace-equation), while full flatness would require the entire Hessian to vanish.

#### Static radius

↑ **Parent:** [Newtonian limit](#newtonian-limit)

For a point mass in a positive [cosmological constant](cosmology.md#cosmological-constant), the inward Newtonian acceleration $GM/r^2$ balances the outward cosmological acceleration $\Lambda r/3$ at the static radius $r_{\rm s}=(3GM/\Lambda)^{1/3}$.

#### Linearized point-mass metric in radial Cartesian form

↑ **Parent:** [Newtonian limit](#newtonian-limit)

For a point mass and $r>0$, write

$$
h_{00}=\frac{2GM}{r},
\qquad
h_{ij}=f(r)x_i x_j.
$$

The vacuum scalar-curvature equation gives $rf'+3f=0$, so $f=C/r^3$. The remaining vacuum equations fix $C=2GM$ in the corresponding Schwarzschild gauge.

### Slowly rotating weak-field metric

↑ **Parent:** [Linearized gravity](#linearized-gravity)

Far from a slowly rotating isolated body of mass $m$ and angular momentum $\mathbf J$, the scalar part of the metric is the linearized point-mass field while $g_{0i}$ is the dipole field of $\mathbf J$. For rotation about the z-axis, the cross term is $4Jr^{-3}(y\,dx-x\,dy)dt$ in a common convention.

## Curvature singularity

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)

A curvature singularity is an incomplete spacetime boundary approached by curves along which a scalar curvature invariant or a component of the [Riemann curvature tensor](#riemann-curvature-tensor) in a parallel-propagated frame becomes unbounded. Such divergence cannot be removed by changing coordinates.

## Schwarzschild spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)

In units $G=c=1$, the Schwarzschild exterior metric is

$$
ds^2=-\left(1-\frac{2M}{r}\right)dt^2
+\left(1-\frac{2M}{r}\right)^{-1}dr^2+r^2d\Omega^2.
$$

### Schwarzschild radial fall from rest at a finite radius

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime)

For radial timelike [geodesic](riemannian-geometry.md#geodesic) fall released from rest at $r_0>2M$, the [Killing energy](#killing-energy) is $E=\sqrt{1-2M/r_0}$ and the [proper time](special-relativity.md#proper-time) radial equation is $(dr/ds)^2=2M/r-2M/r_0$. The displayed parameterization integrates its inward branch, with $s=0$ at release. The [Schwarzschild event horizon](#schwarzschild-event-horizon) is reached at $\eta_H=\arccos(4M/r_0-1)$ in finite proper time, even though [Schwarzschild time](#schwarzschild-time) diverges.

// Target: general-relativity.bigb

#### Finite pulse count for a falling Schwarzschild beacon

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild radial fall from rest at a finite radius](#schwarzschild-radial-fall-from-rest-at-a-finite-radius)

A freely falling source emits [optical pulses](optics.md#optical-pulse) at proper times $\delta+nP$, for integers $n\ge0$. If its horizon-crossing [proper time](special-relativity.md#proper-time) is finite, only pulses beginning before $s_H$ can escape. Their number is the displayed ceiling, for $0\le\delta<P<s_H$. Infinite [Schwarzschild time](#schwarzschild-time) at crossing does not give infinitely many emitted pulses. The pulse phase $\delta$ fixes both the count and the redshift of the last pulse. If a finite pulse straddles the horizon, its exterior portion has an arbitrarily late, increasingly redshifted received tail.

### Proper-time bound inside a Schwarzschild black hole

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime)

Inside the future black-hole region, the radius decreases along every future-directed timelike path. Its unit tangent obeys $|dr/ds|\geq\sqrt{2M/r-1}$ from the metric normalization, independently of radial acceleration or angular motion. Integrating from $r_0<2M$ to zero gives the displayed finite upper bound. The statement concerns future continuation within the black-hole region; reversing time in the white-hole region reverses the radial direction.

### Horizon redshift of a Schwarzschild infaller

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime)

For smooth radial infall through a future [Schwarzschild event horizon](#schwarzschild-event-horizon), outgoing retarded time obeys $u\sim-4M\log(\tau_H-\tau)$. A distant observer sees the [redshift](optics.md#redshift) grow as $e^{t_{\rm rec}/(4M)}$. Its exponent in emission [Schwarzschild time](#schwarzschild-time) is instead $1/(2M)$, because $t_{\rm rec}\sim2t_{\rm em}$. At finite fixed observer radius the wavelength ratio includes $\sqrt{1-2M/r_B}$.

### Schwarzschild time

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime)

[Schwarzschild time](#schwarzschild-time) $t$ is the exterior time coordinate normalized to the [proper time](special-relativity.md#proper-time) of static observers at infinity. At fixed exterior radius, $d\tau=\sqrt{1-2M/r}\,dt$. A received signal and its emission occur at different values of this coordinate.

### Kruskal spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime)

The [Kruskal spacetime](#kruskal-spacetime) is the maximal analytic extension of the positive-mass vacuum [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime). It contains two exterior regions, a [black hole](#black-hole), and a [white hole](#white-hole). [Kruskal–Szekeres coordinates](#kruskal-szekeres-coordinates) remove both horizon coordinate singularities, while $r=0$ remains a [curvature singularity](#curvature-singularity).

#### Non-traversability of the Schwarzschild bridge

↑ **Parent:** [Kruskal spacetime](#kruskal-spacetime)

In [Kruskal–Szekeres coordinates](#kruskal-szekeres-coordinates), every future-directed radial causal curve has nondecreasing $U$ and $V$. The right exterior has $U<0,V>0$, while the left has $U>0,V<0$. No future-directed causal curve can travel between the exteriors because it would have to decrease one null coordinate. The spatial bridge on a time-symmetric slice is therefore not a traversable passage.

#### Kruskal diagram

↑ **Parent:** [Kruskal spacetime](#kruskal-spacetime)

This radial spacetime diagram uses [Kruskal–Szekeres coordinates](#kruskal-szekeres-coordinates) to display both exteriors, the black hole and the white hole of maximally extended [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime). Each point represents a symmetry two-sphere. Radial [null geodesics](special-relativity.md#null-geodesic) have slopes $\pm1$ in the displayed coordinates; the horizons are $T_K=\pm X_K$ and the singularities satisfy $T_K^2-X_K^2=1$ in the usual normalization. A further compactification gives the corresponding [Penrose diagram](#penrose-diagram).

#### Radial timelike incompleteness in Kruskal spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Kruskal spacetime](#kruskal-spacetime)

Every maximal radial [timelike geodesic](#timelike-geodesic) in positive-mass [Kruskal spacetime](#kruskal-spacetime) is incomplete in at least one direction. Its equations are $\dot r^2=E^2-1+2M/r$ and $\ddot r=-M/r^2$. A finite turning point is a maximum, and each trajectory reaches $r=0$ at one or both ends. The remaining [proper time](special-relativity.md#proper-time) is finite because $d\tau\sim\sqrt{r/(2M)}\,dr$. Complete circular timelike geodesics exist, but they have nonzero [angular momentum](classical-mechanics.md#angular-momentum).

### Negative heat capacity of a Schwarzschild black hole

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime)

In units $G=\hbar=c=k_B=1$, a [Schwarzschild black hole](#schwarzschild-spacetime) has [Hawking temperature](#hawking-temperature) $T=1/(8\pi M)$, so its [heat capacity](thermodynamics.md#heat-capacity) is $dM/dT=-8\pi M^2$. It heats up as it loses [mass](classical-mechanics.md#mass), preventing stable canonical equilibrium with an unlimited thermal reservoir.

### Ultrarelativistic limit of the boosted Schwarzschild metric away from the null plane

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime)

Boost a Schwarzschild mass with speed $v$ and hold $p=m\gamma$ fixed as $v\to1$. Away from the null plane $x=t$, the isotropic-coordinate expression tends to

$$
ds^2=ds_{\rm Minkowski}^2+\frac{4p}{|x-t|}(dt-dx)^2.
$$

The complete distributional limit on the null plane requires the additional coordinate treatment that produces the Aichelburg-Sexl shock wave.

### Schwarzschild-de Sitter spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schwarzschild-de_Sitter_spacetime)

The Schwarzschild-de Sitter spacetime is the spherically symmetric vacuum solution with positive [cosmological constant](cosmology.md#cosmological-constant). In static coordinates its metric function is $f(r)=1-2m/r-H^2r^2$, with a black-hole horizon and a cosmological horizon when $mH<1/\sqrt{27}$.

### Geodetic effect

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geodetic_effect)

Geodetic precession is the rotation of a parallel-transported gyroscope relative to the orbital radial direction in curved spacetime. For a circular Schwarzschild orbit its exact precession per revolution is $2\pi[1-\sqrt{1-3M/R}]$.

<h3 id="painleve-gullstrand-coordinates">Painlevé–Gullstrand coordinates</h3>

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Painlevé–Gullstrand_coordinates)

Painlevé–Gullstrand coordinates write the Schwarzschild metric as

$$
ds^2=-dt^2+\left(dr+\sqrt{\frac{2M}{r}}\,dt\right)^2+r^2d\Omega^2.
$$

The constant-$t$ spatial slices are flat, and the coordinates remain regular at the [Schwarzschild radius](#schwarzschild-radius).

#### Domain of the ingoing Painleve-Gullstrand chart

↑ **Parent:** [Painlevé–Gullstrand coordinates](#painleve-gullstrand-coordinates)

Choose [Kruskal–Szekeres coordinates](#kruskal-szekeres-coordinates) with the right exterior at $U<0,V>0$ and $UV=(1-r/(2M))e^{r/(2M)}$. The ingoing [Painlevé–Gullstrand coordinates](#painleve-gullstrand-coordinates) cover that exterior and the future black-hole region $U>0,V>0$, together with their common future horizon. They exclude the other exterior, the white hole, the bifurcation sphere and the singularity. Indeed $\tau=v+F(r)-r_*(r)$, with $v=4M\log V$ and $F'=\sqrt{2M/r}/f$; the difference $F-r_*$ is finite at $r=2M$, whereas $V=0$ requires infinite coordinate time.

#### Free-fall proper time in Schwarzschild spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Painlevé–Gullstrand coordinates](#painleve-gullstrand-coordinates)

The inward radial [geodesic congruence](geodesic-congruence.md) with unit [Killing energy](#killing-energy) has $U=f^{-1}\partial_t-\sqrt{2M/r}\,\partial_r$, where $f=1-2M/r$. Its metric dual is $U^\flat=-d\tau$, so $\tau$ is a synchronized [proper time](special-relativity.md#proper-time) for the congruence. Locally $\tau=t+2\sqrt{2Mr}+2M\log| (\sqrt r-\sqrt{2M})/(\sqrt r+\sqrt{2M}) |$. This yields [Painlevé–Gullstrand coordinates](#painleve-gullstrand-coordinates) with flat spatial slices and a regular future horizon.

##### Schwarzschild horizon-to-singularity proper time for fall from infinity

↑ **Parent:** [Free-fall proper time in Schwarzschild spacetime](#free-fall-proper-time-in-schwarzschild-spacetime)

A radial observer released from rest at infinity has $dr/d\tau=-\sqrt{2M/r}$. Integration from $r=2M$ to $r=0$ gives $\Delta\tau=\int_0^{2M}\sqrt{r/(2M)}dr=4M/3$ in geometric units. In ordinary units it is $4GM/(3c^3)$. The endpoint is the curvature singularity, not a regular spacetime centre; this value belongs to that specified energy, not every possible infalling worldline.

<h4 id="orthonormal-coframe-in-painleve-gullstrand-coordinates">Orthonormal coframe in Painlevé–Gullstrand coordinates</h4>

↑ **Parent:** [Painlevé–Gullstrand coordinates](#painleve-gullstrand-coordinates)

An orthonormal coframe is

$$
e^0=dt,
\qquad e^1=dr+\sqrt{\frac{2M}{r}}\,dt,
\qquad e^2=r\,d\theta,
\qquad e^3=r\sin\theta\,d\phi.
$$

### Radial Weyl curvature of Schwarzschild spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime)

With the curvature convention

$$
R^\alpha{}_{\beta\gamma\delta}
=\partial_\gamma\Gamma^\alpha_{\beta\delta}
-\partial_\delta\Gamma^\alpha_{\beta\gamma}+\cdots,
$$

the vacuum Schwarzschild metric satisfies

$$
C_{trtr}=R_{trtr}=-\frac{2M}{r^3}.
$$

The opposite Riemann-sign convention reverses this sign.

#### Schwarzschild tidal force

↑ **Parent:** [Radial Weyl curvature of Schwarzschild spacetime](#radial-weyl-curvature-of-schwarzschild-spacetime)

The orthonormal-frame components of Schwarzschild curvature scale as $M/r^3$. The [geodesic deviation](#geodesic-deviation) equation therefore gives radial stretching and transverse compression whose magnitude diverges as $r\to0$.

#### Tidal gravity and the equivalence principle

↑ **Parent:** [Radial Weyl curvature of Schwarzschild spacetime](#radial-weyl-curvature-of-schwarzschild-spacetime)

Weyl curvature produces relative acceleration between neighbouring freely falling trajectories and therefore tidal deformation. A freely falling frame removes gravity at one event, as required by the equivalence principle, but cannot remove curvature over an extended region.

### Equatorial null orbit in Schwarzschild spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime)

For inverse radius $y=1/r$ and azimuth $\phi$, a Schwarzschild null geodesic obeys

$$
(y')^2+y^2-2My^3=\frac1{b^2},
\qquad
y''+y=3My^2,
$$

where $b=L/E$ is the [impact parameter](classical-mechanics.md#impact-parameter).

#### Critical photon orbits of Schwarzschild spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Equatorial null orbit in Schwarzschild spacetime](#equatorial-null-orbit-in-schwarzschild-spacetime)

At the critical [impact parameter](classical-mechanics.md#impact-parameter) $b=3\sqrt3M$, the null orbit equation factors as $(u')^2=(u-1/3)^2(2u+1/3)$ for $u=M/r$. One oriented family has the displayed solution. As $\phi\to-\infty$, positive $A$ approaches the [photon sphere](#photon-sphere) from larger radii and negative $A$ from smaller radii; $A=0$ is the circular orbit. The physical domain must retain $r>0$ and the positive square root in its implicit parametrization.

#### Circular null geodesic

↑ **Parent:** [Equatorial null orbit in Schwarzschild spacetime](#equatorial-null-orbit-in-schwarzschild-spacetime)

A circular null geodesic remains at constant areal radius. It lies at a stationary point of the null radial effective potential and may be stable or unstable according to the sign of the second derivative there.

##### Photon sphere

↑ **Parent:** [Circular null geodesic](#circular-null-geodesic)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Photon_sphere)

A photon sphere is a spherical set of circular [null geodesics](special-relativity.md#null-geodesic). In [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime) it occurs at $r=3M$ and is unstable.

###### Photon capture cross-section of a Reissner-Nordstrom black hole

↑ **Parent:** [Photon sphere](#photon-sphere)

For a [Reissner-Nordstrom black hole](#reissner-nordstrom-spacetime) with $|Q|\le M$, the outer unstable [circular null geodesic](#circular-null-geodesic) has radius $r_{\mathrm{ph}}=(3M+\sqrt{9M^2-8Q^2})/2$. Maximizing $f/r^2$ for $f=1-2M/r+Q^2/r^2$ gives the critical [impact parameter](classical-mechanics.md#impact-parameter) $b_c^2=r_{\mathrm{ph}}^2/f(r_{\mathrm{ph}})=2r_{\mathrm{ph}}^3/(r_{\mathrm{ph}}-M)$. The [geometrical optics](optics.md#geometrical-optics) capture cross-section is $\pi b_c^2$, reducing to $27\pi M^2$ in [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime) and $16\pi M^2$ in the extremal charged case. Finite-wavelength absorption need not equal this geometric limit.

// Target: general-relativity.bigb

### Schwarzschild radius

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schwarzschild_radius)

The Schwarzschild radius is $r_s=2GM/c^2$, or $2M$ in geometrized units.

### Schwarzschild tortoise coordinate

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime)

The tortoise coordinate satisfies

$$
\frac{dr_*}{dr}=\left(1-\frac{2M}{r}\right)^{-1},
\qquad
r_*=r+2M\log\left|\frac{r}{2M}-1\right|.
$$

#### Outgoing Eddington-Finkelstein coordinates

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild tortoise coordinate](#schwarzschild-tortoise-coordinate)

The retarded coordinate $u=t-r_*$ puts the radial [Schwarzschild metric](#schwarzschild-spacetime) into $ds^2=-f\,du^2-2\,du\,dr+r^2d\Omega^2$, with $f=1-2M/r$. Outgoing radial [null geodesics](special-relativity.md#null-geodesic) have constant $u$. These coordinates are regular at the past horizon; an infaller crossing the future horizon has $u\to+\infty$.

#### Ingoing Eddington-Finkelstein coordinates

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild tortoise coordinate](#schwarzschild-tortoise-coordinate)

The advanced coordinate $v=t+r_*$ puts the radial Schwarzschild metric into the form

$$
ds^2=-\left(1-\frac{2M}{r}\right)dv^2+2\,dv\,dr+r^2d\Omega^2,
$$

which is regular at the future horizon.

These are the advanced, ingoing member of the [Eddington-Finkelstein coordinates](#eddington-finkelstein-coordinates).

##### Finkelstein diagram

↑ **Parent:** [Ingoing Eddington-Finkelstein coordinates](#ingoing-eddington-finkelstein-coordinates)

A [Finkelstein diagram](#finkelstein-diagram) plots [areal radius](#areal-radius) $r$ against $T=v-r$ in [Ingoing Eddington-Finkelstein coordinates](#ingoing-eddington-finkelstein-coordinates). Its radial [null directions](special-relativity.md#null-directions) show explicitly that both future light-ray families decrease $r$ inside a [Schwarzschild black hole](#schwarzschild-spacetime). It is not a compactified [Penrose diagram](#penrose-diagram).

##### Radial null trajectories in ingoing Eddington-Finkelstein coordinates

↑ **Parent:** [Ingoing Eddington-Finkelstein coordinates](#ingoing-eddington-finkelstein-coordinates)

Radial null curves comprise the ingoing family $v=\text{constant}$ and the outgoing family

$$
\frac{dr}{dv}=\frac12\left(1-\frac{2M}{r}\right).
$$

Outside the horizon the outgoing family moves to larger $r$; inside it both future-directed families move to smaller $r$.

### Schwarzschild event horizon

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime)

The surface $r=2M$ is the future event horizon of Schwarzschild spacetime. Its singularity in Schwarzschild coordinates is removable, whereas $r=0$ is a curvature singularity.

#### Schwarzschild horizon collision energy

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild event horizon](#schwarzschild-event-horizon)

For two equal-mass inward [timelike geodesics](#timelike-geodesic) of unit specific [Killing energy](#killing-energy), their invariant [center-of-mass energy](special-relativity.md#center-of-mass-energy) has this finite horizon limit. If both are captured from infinity, [Schwarzschild marginally bound capture](#schwarzschild-marginally-bound-capture) gives $E_{\rm com}<m\sqrt{20}$, with $m\sqrt{20}$ a supremum. No such universal bound follows for particles prepared inside the angular-momentum barrier.

### Schwarzschild singularity

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime)

The Schwarzschild singularity is the spacelike curvature singularity at $r=0$. Curvature invariants and orthonormal tidal components diverge there.

<h3 id="kruskal-szekeres-coordinates">Kruskal–Szekeres coordinates</h3>

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kruskal–Szekeres_coordinates)

Kruskal–Szekeres coordinates extend the Schwarzschild solution smoothly through its future and past horizons. Constant Schwarzschild radius curves are hyperbolae, while the horizons are null lines through the bifurcation sphere.

#### Kruskal extension across a simple static horizon

↑ **Parent:** [Kruskal–Szekeres coordinates](#kruskal-szekeres-coordinates)

For $ds^2=-fdt^2+dr^2/f+R^2d\Omega^2$, suppose $f$ and $R$ are analytic at $r_H$, $f(r_H)=0$, $f'(r_H)=2\kappa>0$ and $R(r_H)>0$. The [tortoise coordinate](#tortoise-coordinate) obeys the displayed expansion with analytic $h$. For $U=-e^{-\kappa(t-r_*)}$ and $V=e^{\kappa(t+r_*)}$ in the right exterior, $-UV=(r-r_H)e^{2\kappa h(r)}$ after fixing an additive constant. The inverse function theorem makes $r$ analytic in $UV$ through zero, while $ds^2=f\,dU\,dV/(\kappa^2UV)+R^2d\Omega^2$ has a finite nonzero radial coefficient there. This supplies the local analytic extension and its [bifurcate Killing horizon](#bifurcate-killing-horizon).

#### Schwarzschild geodesic from the bifurcation sphere to the singularity

↑ **Parent:** [Kruskal–Szekeres coordinates](#kruskal-szekeres-coordinates)

A radial geodesic orthogonal to the time-symmetric Kruskal slice starts at $r=2M$ with zero Schwarzschild energy and reaches $r=0$ after proper time $\pi M$.

### Gravitational redshift between static Schwarzschild observers

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime)

For static observers at radii $r_A$ and $r_B$, equal Schwarzschild-coordinate time intervals correspond to proper times $d\tau=\sqrt{1-2M/r}\,dt$. Hence

$$
\frac{\Delta\tau_B}{\Delta\tau_A}
=\sqrt{\frac{1-2M/r_B}{1-2M/r_A}}.
$$

This static-observer relation is a special case of [gravitational redshift](#gravitational-redshift), not a definition of every gravitational frequency shift.

### Proper acceleration of a static Schwarzschild observer

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime)

A static observer at Schwarzschild radius $r>2M$ must maintain the outward [proper acceleration](special-relativity.md#proper-acceleration)

$$
a=\frac{M}{r^2\sqrt{1-2M/r}}.
$$

It approaches the Newtonian value $M/r^2$ at large radius and diverges at the horizon.

<h3 id="birkhoff-s-theorem">Birkhoff's theorem</h3>

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birkhoff's_theorem_(relativity))

Every spherically symmetric vacuum solution of the Einstein field equation is locally a portion of Schwarzschild spacetime. In particular, a spherically symmetric body's exterior field depends only on its total mass.

#### Redshifted gravitational mass of a static thin shell

↑ **Parent:** [Birkhoff's theorem](#birkhoff-s-theorem)

A thin spherical shell of proper surface mass density $\rho$ held at Schwarzschild radius $r_{\rm st}$ around mass $M$ contributes, to first order in its mass,

$$
\Delta M_\infty
=4\pi r_{\rm st}^2\rho\sqrt{1-\frac{2M}{r_{\rm st}}}
$$

to the exterior Schwarzschild mass parameter.

### Schwarzschild light deflection

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime)

A null Schwarzschild geodesic with $b\gg r_s$ is deflected through $2r_s/b=4GM/(bc^2)$ to first order.

This is the leading spherical-mass deflection underlying a [gravitational lens](#gravitational-lens).

#### Perturbative derivation of Schwarzschild light deflection

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild light deflection](#schwarzschild-light-deflection)

Writing $y=b^{-1}\sin\phi+\Delta y$ in $y''+y=3My^2$ and retaining first order in $M$ gives

$$
\Delta y''+\Delta y=\frac{3M}{2b^2}(1-\cos2\phi).
$$

The displacement of the outgoing zero is $4M/b$.

<h2 id="nordstrom-s-theory-of-gravitation">Nordström's theory of gravitation</h2>

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordström's_theory_of_gravitation)

Nordstrom's scalar theory restricts the spacetime metric to the conformally flat form $g_{\mu\nu}=\varphi^2\eta_{\mu\nu}$. Its null geodesics have the same unparametrized paths as those of the [Minkowski metric](special-relativity.md#minkowski-metric), so the theory predicts no gravitational bending of light.

### Newtonian limit of Nordstrom gravity

↑ **Parent:** [Nordström's theory of gravitation](#nordstrom-s-theory-of-gravitation)

For a weak, nearly static [conformal factor](#conformal-factor) in [Nordstrom theory of gravitation](#nordstrom-s-theory-of-gravitation), the trace of the [dust stress-energy tensor](#dust-stress-energy-tensor) is negative in signature $(-+++)$, so its scalar field equation reduces to the [Poisson equation](partial-differential-equation.md#poisson-equation) for the [Newtonian gravitational potential](classical-mechanics.md#newtonian-gravitational-potential). The [geodesic equation for a conformally flat metric](#geodesic-equation-for-a-conformally-flat-metric) gives $\ddot{\boldsymbol x}=-\boldsymbol\nabla\Phi$ at small speeds. The constant one in $\phi$ has no effect on the force.

### Conformally flat metric

↑ **Parent:** [Nordström's theory of gravitation](#nordstrom-s-theory-of-gravitation)

A metric is conformally flat when it is locally a positive scalar multiple of a flat metric. Multiplication by the conformal factor preserves null cones and unparametrized null geodesics.

A conformally flat metric is locally a positive scalar multiple of a flat metric. It makes its domain a [conformally flat manifold](#conformally-flat-manifold).

#### Tolman wormhole

↑ **Parent:** [Conformally flat metric](#conformally-flat-metric)

For $\lambda>0$, this four-dimensional Euclidean metric has two asymptotically flat ends. Inversion $\rho=\lambda/r$ puts the metric near the $r=0$ end into the same form with $\rho\to\infty$. On $r>0$, its [Ricci scalar](#ricci-scalar) vanishes because $\Delta(r^{-2})=0$, but its [Ricci tensor](#ricci-tensor) is $R_{ij}=4\lambda(\delta_{ij}-4x_ix_j/r^2)/(r^2+\lambda)^2$. It therefore does not satisfy the [Vacuum Einstein equations](#vacuum-einstein-equations) unless $\lambda=0$. For negative $\lambda$ the conformal factor vanishes at a finite radius, so that metric is not a smooth wormhole across that sphere.

#### Curvature of a conformally Euclidean metric

↑ **Parent:** [Conformally flat metric](#conformally-flat-metric)

For a positive conformal factor in dimension $n\geq3$, set $S_{ij}=-u_{ij}+u_i u_j-\tfrac12|\nabla u|^2\delta_{ij}$. These are the coordinate components of the [Schouten tensor](#schouten-tensor). In the convention $R_{ijkl}=g(e_i,R(e_k,e_l)e_j)$, the [Riemann curvature tensor](#riemann-curvature-tensor) is $R_{ijkl}=e^{2u}(\delta_{ik}S_{jl}+\delta_{jl}S_{ik}-\delta_{il}S_{jk}-\delta_{jk}S_{il})$. The [orthonormal coframe](#orthonormal-coframe-in-spacetime) $e^u dx^i$ has [connection 1-forms](connection-1-form.md) $\omega^i{}_j=u_jdx^i-u_idx^j$; substituting into [Cartan's second structure equation](connection-1-form.md#cartan-s-second-structure-equation) proves this expression directly. Its contractions give $R_{ij}=(n-2)S_{ij}+\delta_{ij}\sum_kS_{kk}$ and $R=e^{-2u}[-2(n-1)\Delta u-(n-1)(n-2)|\nabla u|^2]$.

#### Conformal factor

↑ **Parent:** [Conformally flat metric](#conformally-flat-metric)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conformal_factor)

A conformal factor is the positive scalar field multiplying a metric under a conformal rescaling. In numerical relativity one often writes a spatial metric as $\gamma_{ij}=\psi^4\widetilde\gamma_{ij}$.

##### Conformal preservation of null geodesic paths

↑ **Parent:** [Conformal factor](#conformal-factor)

The difference of the two [Levi-Civita connections](#levi-civita-connection) is $\widetilde\Gamma^a{}_{bc}-\Gamma^a{}_{bc}=\delta^a_b\partial_c\log\Omega+\delta^a_c\partial_b\log\Omega-g_{bc}g^{ad}\partial_d\log\Omega$. Along a [null geodesic](special-relativity.md#null-geodesic) with tangent $k$, the last term vanishes and the remaining acceleration is $2k(\log\Omega)k$. It is parallel to the tangent, so changing the [affine parameter](riemannian-geometry.md#affine-parameter) removes it. Thus a positive [conformal factor](#conformal-factor) preserves unparametrized [null geodesics](special-relativity.md#null-geodesic), although it generally does not preserve timelike or spacelike geodesics.

### Geodesic equation for a conformally flat metric

↑ **Parent:** [Nordström's theory of gravitation](#nordstrom-s-theory-of-gravitation)

For $g_{\mu\nu}=\varphi^2\eta_{\mu\nu}$,

$$
\Gamma^\mu_{\alpha\beta}
=\delta^\mu_\alpha\partial_\beta\log\varphi
+\delta^\mu_\beta\partial_\alpha\log\varphi
-\eta_{\alpha\beta}\partial^\mu\log\varphi.
$$

For a null curve, the last term drops from the [geodesic equation](riemannian-geometry.md#geodesic-equation); the remaining acceleration is parallel to the tangent and can be removed by reparametrization.

## de Sitter spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/de_Sitter_spacetime)

De Sitter spacetime is the maximally symmetric Lorentzian spacetime of constant positive curvature. Its expanding flat slicing has scale factor $a(\tau)=-1/(H\tau)$ in conformal time $\tau<0$.

### Open slicing of de Sitter spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [de Sitter spacetime](#de-sitter-spacetime)

With no matter or radiation, $k<0$ and $H_\Lambda=\sqrt{\Lambda/3}>0$, the [Friedmann equation](cosmology.md#friedmann-equations) in units $c=1$ is $\dot a^2=H_\Lambda^2a^2-k$. Separating variables on the expanding branch and choosing its coordinate origin at $a=0$ gives the displayed [scale factor](cosmology.md#scale-factor-cosmology). Normalization $a(t_0)=1$ imposes $\sinh(H_\Lambda t_0)=H_\Lambda/\sqrt{-k}$ and $H_0^2=H_\Lambda^2-k$. The zero of the [scale factor](cosmology.md#scale-factor-cosmology) is the boundary of this open coordinate patch, rather than a curvature singularity of [de Sitter spacetime](#de-sitter-spacetime).

### Two-dimensional de Sitter spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [de Sitter spacetime](#de-sitter-spacetime)

Unit-radius [two-dimensional de Sitter spacetime](#two-dimensional-de-sitter-spacetime) is the [hyperboloid](differential-geometry.md#hyperboloid) $(X^0)^2-(X^1)^2-(X^2)^2=-1$ in three-dimensional [Minkowski spacetime](special-relativity.md#minkowski-spacetime). Its closed coordinates are $X^0=\sinh t$, $X^1=\cosh t\cos\chi$, $X^2=\cosh t\sin\chi$, with $t\in\mathbb R$ and $\chi$ periodic modulo $2\pi$. Unwrapping $\chi$ gives its universal covering spacetime instead.

#### Universal cover of two-dimensional de Sitter spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Two-dimensional de Sitter spacetime](#two-dimensional-de-sitter-spacetime)

The unrestricted spatial coordinate unwraps the spatial circle of [two-dimensional de Sitter spacetime](#two-dimensional-de-sitter-spacetime). The [conformal time](cosmology.md#conformal-time) $\eta=\arcsin(\tanh t)$ lies in $(-\pi/2,\pi/2)$, and [null geodesics](special-relativity.md#null-geodesic) have slopes $d\xi/d\eta=\pm1$. Constant-$\eta$ lines are [Cauchy hypersurfaces](#cauchy-surface): along any causal curve $|d\xi/d\eta|\leq1$, so a curve cannot escape to spatial infinity in a bounded conformal-time interval. The covering spacetime is therefore [globally hyperbolic](#globally-hyperbolic-spacetime), despite its observer-dependent cosmological horizons. Identifying $\xi$ modulo $2\pi$ gives the de Sitter cylinder, also globally hyperbolic.

#### Conformal cylinder of two-dimensional de Sitter spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Two-dimensional de Sitter spacetime](#two-dimensional-de-sitter-spacetime)

The [conformal time](cosmology.md#conformal-time) $T=2\arctan(e^t)-\pi/2$ satisfies $\sin T=\tanh t$ and $\cos T=\operatorname{sech}t$. Multiplying the physical metric by $\cos^2T$ gives a finite [conformal compactification](geometry-and-topology.md#conformal-compactification) with periodic spatial coordinate. Its past and future conformal boundaries are spacelike circles. [Null geodesics](special-relativity.md#null-geodesic) have slopes $d\chi/dT=\pm1$.

##### Observer horizons in two-dimensional de Sitter spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Conformal cylinder of two-dimensional de Sitter spacetime](#conformal-cylinder-of-two-dimensional-de-sitter-spacetime)

For the complete observer $\chi=0$ in [two-dimensional de Sitter spacetime](#two-dimensional-de-sitter-spacetime), let $d(\chi,0)\in[0,\pi]$ be shortest angular distance. An event can signal to the observer exactly when $d(\chi,0)<\pi/2-T$, and can receive its signals exactly when $d(\chi,0)<T+\pi/2$. Equality describes its future and past [observer event horizons](#observer-event-horizon). Their intersection is the [static patch of de Sitter spacetime](#static-patch-of-de-sitter-spacetime) containing the observer. These are observer-dependent cosmological horizons; constant-$T$ circles are [Cauchy hypersurfaces](#cauchy-surface), so there are no [Cauchy horizons](#cauchy-horizon) in the full spacetime.

#### Geodesics of two-dimensional de Sitter spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Two-dimensional de Sitter spacetime](#two-dimensional-de-sitter-spacetime)

A [geodesic](riemannian-geometry.md#geodesic) on the embedded [two-dimensional de Sitter spacetime](#two-dimensional-de-sitter-spacetime) is the intersection with a two-plane through the ambient origin. For initial point $P$ and tangent $V$ satisfying $P^2=-1$, $P\cdot V=0$, $V^2=\varepsilon$, its affine parametrization is $P\cosh s+V\sinh s$ when timelike, $P+sV$ when null and $P\cos s+V\sin s$ when spacelike. These solve $X''=\varepsilon X$. The [spacelike geodesics](riemannian-geometry.md#spacelike-geodesic) are closed; [timelike geodesics](#timelike-geodesic) and [null geodesics](special-relativity.md#null-geodesic) extend to both conformal infinities with infinite affine length.

### Closed slicing of de Sitter spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [de Sitter spacetime](#de-sitter-spacetime)

The vacuum [Einstein field equations](#einstein-field-equations) with positive [cosmological constant](cosmology.md#cosmological-constant) admit a closed spherical slicing with scale factor $a(t)=\ell\cosh[(t-t_0)/\ell]$. The time-symmetric slice is at $t=t_0$. The substitution $T=2\arctan(e^\tau)-\pi/2$ gives $dT/d\tau=\operatorname{sech}\tau$ and $\cos T=\operatorname{sech}\tau$, so the metric is $\ell^2\sec^2T[-dT^2+d\Omega_3^2]$. The conformal metric is a finite-time slab of the [Einstein static universe](#einstein-static-universe), with spacelike conformal infinities $T=\pm\pi/2$.

#### Cosmological horizons in global de Sitter spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Closed slicing of de Sitter spacetime](#closed-slicing-of-de-sitter-spacetime)

For a complete comoving observer at the north pole of the [closed slicing of de Sitter spacetime](#closed-slicing-of-de-sitter-spacetime), radial null rays have $d\chi=\pm dT$. The finite past conformal interval gives the displayed [particle horizon](cosmology.md#particle-horizon); the finite remaining future interval gives the displayed [observer event horizon](#observer-event-horizon). The angular distances range strictly between zero and $\pi$ at every finite proper time. Complete constant-$T$ three-spheres are [Cauchy hypersurfaces](#cauchy-surface), so their domains of dependence cover the global spacetime and they have no [Cauchy horizons](#cauchy-horizon). Cosmological horizons are observer-dependent and are not failures of global hyperbolicity.

### Static patch of de Sitter spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [de Sitter spacetime](#de-sitter-spacetime)

This region $0\leq r<R$ of [de Sitter spacetime](#de-sitter-spacetime) is static for the central observer, with a cosmological [Killing horizon](#killing-horizon) at $r=R$. The central [proper time](special-relativity.md#proper-time) fixes the normalization of $\partial_t$. Static observers have acceleration $r/(R^2\sqrt{1-r^2/R^2})$. Horizon-crossing null coordinates remove the coordinate singularity, while the global extension has compact spherical spatial slices and spacelike past/future conformal infinity.

#### Euclidean de Sitter action and entropy

↑ **Parent:** [Static patch of de Sitter spacetime](#static-patch-of-de-sitter-spacetime)

In units $G=\hbar=c=k_B=1$, a smooth [Wick rotation](perturbative-quantum-field-theory.md#wick-rotation) of the four-dimensional [static patch of de Sitter spacetime](#static-patch-of-de-sitter-spacetime) has Euclidean time period $2\pi\sqrt{3/\Lambda}$. Its compact geometry is a round four-sphere of radius $\sqrt{3/\Lambda}$. The [Einstein field equations](#einstein-field-equations) give $R=4\Lambda$, and direct integration gives volume $24\pi^2/\Lambda^2$, hence gravitational [Euclidean action](perturbative-quantum-field-theory.md#euclidean-action) $I_E=-3\pi/\Lambda$. With zero assigned internal energy, $I_E=\beta E-S$ gives [Bekenstein-Hawking entropy](#bekenstein-hawking-entropy) $S=3\pi/\Lambda=A/4$.

#### de Sitter horizon temperature

↑ **Parent:** [Static patch of de Sitter spacetime](#static-patch-of-de-sitter-spacetime)

In units $\hbar=c=k_B=1$, the central inertial observer has temperature $1/(2\pi R)$. The [Tolman temperature law](thermodynamics.md#tolman-ehrenfest-relation) redshifts it to the displayed static-observer value. Its acceleration satisfies $4\pi^2T^2=a^2+R^{-2}$. Near the horizon the temperature approaches $a/(2\pi)$, as predicted by the [Unruh effect](quantum-field-theory.md#unruh-effect); at the center temperature remains nonzero despite vanishing acceleration.

### de Sitter isometry

↑ **Parent:** [de Sitter spacetime](#de-sitter-spacetime)

Four-dimensional [de Sitter spacetime](#de-sitter-spacetime) has ten independent isometries. In the expanding flat slicing they act on the spatial boundary as three translations, three rotations, one dilation, and three special conformal transformations.

#### Spatial translation

↑ **Parent:** [de Sitter isometry](#de-sitter-isometry)

A spatial translation in de Sitter flat slicing sends $\mathbf x\mapsto\mathbf x+\mathbf a$ at fixed conformal time. Translation invariance of a momentum-space correlator produces its momentum-conserving [Dirac delta distribution](distribution-theory.md#dirac-delta-function).

#### Spatial rotation

↑ **Parent:** [de Sitter isometry](#de-sitter-isometry)

A spatial rotation acts by $\mathbf x\mapsto R\mathbf x$ with $R$ orthogonal. A rotationally invariant scalar two-point function depends only on the magnitude of its momentum.

#### de Sitter dilation

↑ **Parent:** [de Sitter isometry](#de-sitter-isometry)

In flat conformal coordinates, a de Sitter dilation acts as $(\tau,\mathbf x)\mapsto(s\tau,s\mathbf x)$. It is an isometry because the common coordinate rescaling cancels the inverse-square scaling of the conformal factor.

#### Special conformal transformation

↑ **Parent:** [de Sitter isometry](#de-sitter-isometry)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_conformal_transformation)

The three special conformal transformations complete translations, rotations, and dilations to the ten-dimensional de Sitter isometry group in flat slicing. A propagation speed different from the metric light speed generally breaks these transformations.

### Expanding flat slicing of de Sitter spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [de Sitter spacetime](#de-sitter-spacetime)

The expanding flat slicing has metric

$$
ds^2=-dt^2+e^{2Ht}d\mathbf x^2,
\qquad H>0.
$$

It covers one half of global [de Sitter spacetime](#de-sitter-spacetime) and has a past coordinate boundary at $t\to-\infty$.

#### Timelike geodesic in the expanding flat slicing of de Sitter spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Expanding flat slicing of de Sitter spacetime](#expanding-flat-slicing-of-de-sitter-spacetime)

For a timelike geodesic moving in the $x$ direction with $t(0)=x(0)=0$ and $\dot x(0)=v_0>0$, spatial translation symmetry gives $e^{2Ht}\dot x=v_0$. Proper-time normalization then gives

$$
\dot t=\sqrt{1+v_0^2e^{-2Ht}}.
$$

##### Past incompleteness of the expanding flat slicing of de Sitter spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Timelike geodesic in the expanding flat slicing of de Sitter spacetime](#timelike-geodesic-in-the-expanding-flat-slicing-of-de-sitter-spacetime)

The geodesic with nonzero conserved spatial momentum reaches $t=-\infty$ after the finite past proper time

$$
\tau_-=-\frac1{2H}\log\frac{\sqrt{1+v_0^2}+1}{\sqrt{1+v_0^2}-1}.
$$

This is incompleteness of the expanding coordinate patch, whose geodesics extend smoothly in global [de Sitter spacetime](#de-sitter-spacetime); it is not a curvature singularity.

## Einstein static universe

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Einstein_static_universe)

The Einstein static universe is a product of time with a round three-sphere supported by matter and a positive cosmological constant. For pressureless matter its cosmological repulsion balances the spatial curvature exactly.

## Anti-de Sitter spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-de_Sitter_spacetime)

Anti-de Sitter spacetime is the maximally symmetric Lorentzian spacetime of constant negative curvature.

### Anti-de Sitter timelike geodesic refocusing

↑ **Parent:** [Anti-de Sitter spacetime](#anti-de-sitter-spacetime)

Embed radius-$L$ [Anti-de Sitter spacetime](#anti-de-sitter-spacetime) as $X\cdot X=-L^2$ in signature with two timelike directions. Initial tangent $V_P$ satisfies $X_P\cdot V_P=0$ and $V_P\cdot V_P=-1$. The displayed curve remains on the hyperboloid and has unit timelike tangent; its ambient acceleration is normal, proving it is a [geodesic](riemannian-geometry.md#geodesic). Every such curve reaches $-X_P$ after proper time $\pi L$. On the [universal cover](algebraic-topology.md#universal-cover), this is a later antipodal event, not a closed causal loop.
