Global time advances by $2\pi$ over a complete ambient period.

### Matrix model of anti-de Sitter three-space

↑ **Parent:** [Anti-de Sitter spacetime](#anti-de-sitter-spacetime)

For ambient [quadratic form](linear-algebra.md#quadratic-form) $Q=-x_0^2-x_4^2+x_1^2+x_2^2$, associate $M=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}x_4+x_1&x_2+x_0\\x_2-x_0&x_4-x_1\end{smallmatrix}\right)$. Its [determinant](linear-algebra.md#determinant) is $-Q$, so the unit [Anti-de Sitter spacetime](#anti-de-sitter-spacetime) hyperboloid is the [special linear group](group-theory.md#special-linear-group) of real two-by-two matrices. On its tangent space at the identity, the induced [bilinear form](linear-algebra.md#bilinear-form) is $\tfrac12\operatorname{tr}(UV)$. The [Killing form](lie-algebra.md#killing-form) is $4\operatorname{tr}(UV)$, and left and right matrix multiplication preserve the ambient form. Consequently the induced [bi-invariant pseudo-Riemannian metric](lie-theory.md#bi-invariant-pseudo-riemannian-metric) is $B/8$. This model has periodic time; its causally unwrapped version is the [universal covering Lie group](lie-theory.md#universal-covering-lie-group).

<h4 id="left-right-double-cover-of-so0-2-2">Left-right double cover of SO0(2,2)</h4>

↑ **Parent:** [Matrix model of anti-de Sitter three-space](#matrix-model-of-anti-de-sitter-three-space)

The action $(A,B):M\mapsto AMB^{-1}$ preserves the [quadratic form](linear-algebra.md#quadratic-form) $-\det M$ on real two-by-two matrices. Its [kernel](linear-algebra.md#kernel-of-a-linear-map) consists of the simultaneous scalar pairs $(I,I)$ and $(-I,-I)$. The differential $(a,b):M\mapsto aM-Mb$ is injective on the two traceless [Lie algebras](lie-algebra.md), and both source and target have dimension six. Its image is therefore open and, since the source is connected, equals the [identity component of a Lie group](lie-theory.md#identity-component-of-a-lie-group) $SO_0(2,2)$. This gives a two-sheeted [covering map](algebraic-topology.md#covering-space) onto the identity component, not onto the disconnected full [special orthogonal group](linear-algebra.md#special-orthogonal-group) $SO(2,2)$.

### Poincare coordinates on anti-de Sitter spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Anti-de Sitter spacetime](#anti-de-sitter-spacetime)

For Lorentzian boundary metric $\eta$ and $z>0$, this [Anti-de Sitter spacetime](#anti-de-sitter-spacetime) patch has unit curvature radius. Its [orthonormal coframe](#orthonormal-coframe-in-spacetime) has connection $\omega^a{}_3=-\theta^a$ and curvature $\Omega^a{}_b=-\theta^a\wedge\theta_b$, giving [sectional curvature](second-fundamental-form.md#sectional-curvature) $-1$. The full local [isometry](riemannian-geometry.md#isometry) algebra is $\mathfrak{so}(d-1,2)$ in spacetime dimension $d$. A patch need not support every local Killing flow for all parameter values.

<h4 id="timelike-geodesics-in-a-warped-poincare-patch">Timelike geodesics in a warped Poincaré patch</h4>

↑ **Parent:** [Poincare coordinates on anti-de Sitter spacetime](#poincare-coordinates-on-anti-de-sitter-spacetime)

For unit-radius metric $dy^2+e^{-2y}\eta_{\mu\nu}dx^\mu dx^\nu$, translations conserve $p^\mu=e^{-2y}\dot x^\mu$. Timelike normalization gives $K^2=-\eta_{\mu\nu}p^\mu p^\nu>0$ and $\dot y^2=K^2e^{2y}-1$. Setting $w=e^{-y}$ yields $\dot w^2+w^2=K^2$, hence the displayed proper-time solution where the cosine is positive. Its two patch-horizon crossings are separated by proper time $\pi$, although the globally extended [geodesic](riemannian-geometry.md#geodesic) is complete.

<h4 id="poincare-horizon">Poincaré horizon</h4>

↑ **Parent:** [Poincare coordinates on anti-de Sitter spacetime](#poincare-coordinates-on-anti-de-sitter-spacetime)

The boundary of a chosen [Poincare coordinates on anti-de Sitter spacetime](#poincare-coordinates-on-anti-de-sitter-spacetime) patch inside the full spacetime is a null coordinate horizon. It is not the timelike [conformal boundary](geometry-and-topology.md#conformal-boundary), and not a curvature singularity. Timelike [geodesics](riemannian-geometry.md#geodesic) can cross it in finite proper time while the patch time diverges. In a signed-diameter conformal strip, the patch containing $T=\rho=0$ satisfies $|T|<\pi/2+\rho$.

### Two-dimensional anti-de Sitter spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Anti-de Sitter spacetime](#anti-de-sitter-spacetime)

The unit hyperboloid $(X^0)^2+(X^1)^2-(X^2)^2=1$ in ambient signature $(++-)$ has $X^0=\cosh r\cos t$, $X^1=\cosh r\sin t$, $X^2=\sinh r$. It gives the displayed metric with $r\in\mathbb R$ and periodic $t$ modulo $2\pi$. Its time circles are timelike, so the [universal cover](algebraic-topology.md#universal-cover), with $t\in\mathbb R$, removes these [closed timelike curves](#closed-timelike-curve). The metric alone does not prescribe the global identification. Both versions have constant negative curvature and two spatial ends.

#### Geodesics of two-dimensional anti-de Sitter spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Two-dimensional anti-de Sitter spacetime](#two-dimensional-anti-de-sitter-spacetime)

A geodesic of the unit embedded hyperboloid lies in the plane spanned by its initial point and tangent. The constraint $X^2=1$ forces the displayed ambient acceleration. Timelike geodesics are $X=P\cos s+V\sin s$, null ones are $X=P+sV$, and spacelike ones are $X=P\cosh s+V\sinh s$. Timelike geodesics are closed on the hyperboloid but lift to complete nonclosed curves on its [universal cover](algebraic-topology.md#universal-cover). In static coordinates, conserved $E=\cosh^2r\,dt/ds$ gives $(dr/ds)^2=E^2/\cosh^2r-1$ and $\sinh r=\sqrt{E^2-1}\sin(s-s_0)$. Null affine parameters obey $\lambda=\pm\sinh r/E+\text{constant}$, proving completeness at both spatial ends.

#### Conformal strip of two-dimensional anti-de Sitter spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Two-dimensional anti-de Sitter spacetime](#two-dimensional-anti-de-sitter-spacetime)

On the universal cover, $-\pi/2<\psi<\pi/2$ and $t\in\mathbb R$. The two [conformal boundaries](geometry-and-topology.md#conformal-boundary) are timelike. [Null geodesics](special-relativity.md#null-geodesic) are $t\pm\psi=\text{constant}$ and can travel from either boundary to the center in finite coordinate time, despite taking infinite physical affine parameter to reach the boundary. There is no event horizon for the complete central static observer. The spacetime is not [globally hyperbolic](#globally-hyperbolic-spacetime), unlike the global conformal cylinder of [two-dimensional de Sitter spacetime](#two-dimensional-de-sitter-spacetime); well-posed field evolution requires boundary conditions as well as initial data.

### Group model of three-dimensional anti-de Sitter spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Anti-de Sitter spacetime](#anti-de-sitter-spacetime)

Associate $(u,v,x,y)$ with $X=\begin{pmatrix}u+x&y+v\\y-v&u-x\end{pmatrix}$. The ambient quadratic form is $-\det X$; hence the radius-$\ell$ anti-de Sitter quadric is $X=\ell g$ with $g\in SL(2,\mathbb R)$. The action $X\mapsto AXB^{-1}$ gives $SO_0(2,2)\cong(SL(2,\mathbb R)\times SL(2,\mathbb R))/\{(I,I),(-I,-I)\}$. Its induced [bi-invariant pseudo-Riemannian metric](lie-theory.md#bi-invariant-pseudo-riemannian-metric) is $g=\ell^2K/8$, giving $\operatorname{Ric}=-2g/\ell^2$ and cosmological constant $-1/\ell^2$. The antipodal quotient is $PSL(2,\mathbb R)$; unwrapping global time gives the universal covering group instead. The connected component is essential in the displayed orthogonal-group isomorphism.

### Static coordinates on anti-de Sitter spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Anti-de Sitter spacetime](#anti-de-sitter-spacetime)

In four dimensions, global static coordinates have

$$
ds^2=-(1+r^2/a^2)dt^2+(1+r^2/a^2)^{-1}dr^2+r^2d\Omega^2.
$$

#### Radial timelike geodesic in anti-de Sitter spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Static coordinates on anti-de Sitter spacetime](#static-coordinates-on-anti-de-sitter-spacetime)

A radial timelike geodesic through the origin obeys a harmonic radial equation and returns to the origin after proper time $\pi a$, independently of its energy.

#### Radial null geodesic in anti-de Sitter spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Static coordinates on anti-de Sitter spacetime](#static-coordinates-on-anti-de-sitter-spacetime)

In global static coordinates, an outward radial null geodesic takes infinite affine parameter to approach the anti-de Sitter conformal boundary but only finite static coordinate time $\pi a/2$.

#### Stable circular timelike geodesic in anti-de Sitter spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Static coordinates on anti-de Sitter spacetime](#static-coordinates-on-anti-de-sitter-spacetime)

At every radius $r_0>0$, global anti-de Sitter spacetime has stable circular timelike geodesics with $|\dot\phi|=1/a$ and $\dot t=1$.

## Planarity of geodesics in spherical symmetry

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)

Rotational symmetry conserves the angular-momentum direction, so every nonradial geodesic lies in a plane through the symmetry centre; coordinates may place that plane at $\theta=\pi/2$.

## Timelike geodesic effective potential

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)

After using Killing constants and four-velocity normalization, radial timelike geodesic motion can often be written as kinetic energy plus an effective potential equal to a constant.

### Effective-potential stability of a circular orbit

↑ **Parent:** [Timelike geodesic effective potential](#timelike-geodesic-effective-potential)

A circular orbit at a strict local minimum of the radial effective potential is stable under small radial perturbations.

### Effective potential for timelike Schwarzschild geodesics

↑ **Parent:** [Timelike geodesic effective potential](#timelike-geodesic-effective-potential)

For equatorial timelike motion with specific angular momentum $h$,

$$
\frac12\dot r^2+V_{\rm eff}(r)=\frac{E^2-1}{2},
\qquad
V_{\rm eff}=-\frac mr+\frac{h^2}{2r^2}-\frac{mh^2}{r^3}.
$$

A circular orbit has $h^2=mr^2/(r-3m)$ and therefore requires $r>3m$.

#### Schwarzschild marginally bound capture

↑ **Parent:** [Effective potential for timelike Schwarzschild geodesics](#effective-potential-for-timelike-schwarzschild-geodesics)

A [timelike geodesic](#timelike-geodesic) with unit specific [Killing energy](#killing-energy) can plunge from infinity into a [Schwarzschild black hole](#schwarzschild-spacetime) only if its specific [angular momentum](classical-mechanics.md#angular-momentum) satisfies $|h|<4M$. The limiting case $|h|=4M$ asymptotes to the unstable orbit at $r=4M$. A geodesic already inside the angular-momentum barrier can cross the [event horizon](#event-horizon) with larger $|h|$, so the origin-at-infinity condition matters.

#### Angular velocity of a circular Schwarzschild geodesic

↑ **Parent:** [Effective potential for timelike Schwarzschild geodesics](#effective-potential-for-timelike-schwarzschild-geodesics)

A circular timelike geodesic of Schwarzschild radius $r>3M$ has coordinate angular velocity

$$
\frac{d\phi}{dt}=\sqrt{\frac{M}{r^3}}.
$$

Its coordinate period is $2\pi\sqrt{r^3/M}$.

##### Proper period of a circular Schwarzschild geodesic

↑ **Parent:** [Angular velocity of a circular Schwarzschild geodesic](#angular-velocity-of-a-circular-schwarzschild-geodesic)

Over one circular orbit at radius $r>3M$, the orbiting clock measures

$$
\Delta\tau=2\pi\sqrt{\frac{r^2(r-3M)}M}.
$$

The factor $r-3M$ combines gravitational and orbital kinematic time dilation.

#### Schwarzschild perihelion precession

↑ **Parent:** [Effective potential for timelike Schwarzschild geodesics](#effective-potential-for-timelike-schwarzschild-geodesics)

The nearly circular orbit equation

$$
u''+u=\frac m{h^2}+3mu^2
$$

has radial angular frequency $1-3m^2/h^2$ to leading order. The perihelion advances by $6\pi m^2/h^2$ per orbit.

## Metric tensor

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metric_tensor)

A metric tensor is a [nondegenerate](linear-algebra.md#nondegenerate-bilinear-form) symmetric [covariant tensor](linear-algebra.md#covariant-tensor) that defines squared line elements by $ds^2=g_{\mu\nu}dx^\mu dx^\nu$.

### Variation of metric volume density

↑ **Parent:** [Metric tensor](#metric-tensor)

For a [nondegenerate](linear-algebra.md#nondegenerate-bilinear-form) [metric tensor](#metric-tensor) whose signature is fixed during the [variation](calculus-of-variations.md#variation), the [Jacobi formula](linear-algebra.md#jacobi-s-formula) gives $\delta\det g=(\det g)g^{ab}\delta g_{ab}$. Differentiating the square root of the absolute [determinant](linear-algebra.md#determinant) proves the displayed identity. Expressed through the [variation of inverse metric](#variation-of-inverse-metric), the formula is $-\sqrt{|\det g|}\,g_{ab}\delta g^{ab}/2$. Absolute values accommodate either Riemannian or Lorentzian signature.

### Variation of inverse metric

↑ **Parent:** [Metric tensor](#metric-tensor)

Differentiating $g^{ac}g_{cb}=\delta^a_b$ and multiplying by the inverse gives the displayed [variation](calculus-of-variations.md#variation). It is the [matrix inverse](linear-algebra.md#matrix-inverse) [derivative](calculus.md#derivative), expressed with the covariant and contravariant components of a [metric tensor](#metric-tensor).

### Inverse metric volume variation

↑ **Parent:** [Metric tensor](#metric-tensor)

For a nondegenerate [metric tensor](#metric-tensor), differentiating $g_{ac}g^{cb}=\delta_a{}^b$ gives $\delta g_{ab}=-g_{ac}g_{bd}\delta g^{cd}$. Combining this with the [Jacobi determinant derivative formula](linear-algebra.md#jacobi-determinant-derivative-formula) proves the displayed volume-density variation. It is the determinant contribution when defining a [stress-energy tensor](#stress-energy-tensor) by varying the inverse [metric tensor](#metric-tensor).

### Prolate spheroidal coordinates

↑ **Parent:** [Metric tensor](#metric-tensor)

For $0<\alpha<\beta$, define $-\beta\leq\mu\leq-\alpha\leq\lambda$ by $R^2/(\tau+\alpha)+z^2/(\tau+\beta)=1$. Then $R^2=(\lambda+\alpha)(\mu+\alpha)/(\alpha-\beta)$ and $z^2=(\lambda+\beta)(\mu+\beta)/(\beta-\alpha)$. Constant $\lambda$ surfaces are confocal [prolate spheroids](geometry-and-topology.md#prolate-spheroid) and constant interior $\mu$ surfaces are two-sheeted hyperboloids, with foci $z=\pm\sqrt{\beta-\alpha}$. The coordinates are orthogonal away from their degeneracies. The squared scale factors are $h_\lambda^2=(\lambda-\mu)/[4(\lambda+\alpha)(\lambda+\beta)]$, $h_\mu^2=(\mu-\lambda)/[4(\mu+\alpha)(\mu+\beta)]$ and $h_\phi^2=R^2$. The two signs of $z$ are coordinate branches, joined at the equatorial fold $\mu=-\beta$.

### Lorentzian metric

↑ **Parent:** [Metric tensor](#metric-tensor)

A Lorentzian metric is a smooth nondegenerate symmetric [metric tensor](#metric-tensor) with one negative and all other positive directions, or its opposite-sign convention. At each point, a nonzero tangent is timelike, null or spacelike according as $g(v,v)$ is negative, zero or positive in the first convention. Unlike a [Riemannian metric](differential-geometry.md#riemannian-metric), it gives a light cone rather than a positive norm. [Minkowski spacetime](special-relativity.md#minkowski-spacetime) is its constant flat model, and [proper time](special-relativity.md#proper-time) along a timelike curve is $\int\sqrt{-g(\dot\gamma,\dot\gamma)}\,du$.

#### Spacetime metric

↑ **Parent:** [Lorentzian metric](#lorentzian-metric)

A spacetime metric is the [Lorentzian metric](#lorentzian-metric) on a [spacetime](special-relativity.md#spacetime). It determines [proper time](special-relativity.md#proper-time) along timelike paths and the null directions that describe local light propagation.

#### Null coordinate

↑ **Parent:** [Lorentzian metric](#lorentzian-metric)

A [null coordinate](#null-coordinate) is a coordinate function $u$ whose gradient is null, $g^{ab}\partial_au\partial_bu=0$. Its level sets are [null hypersurfaces](#null-hypersurface). Pairs of such coordinates straighten radial [null directions](special-relativity.md#null-directions) in two-dimensional orbit metrics; a monotone reparametrization of either function preserves its null level sets. Examples include the [retarded and advanced null coordinates](special-relativity.md#retarded-and-advanced-null-coordinates) and the radial [Kruskal–Szekeres coordinates](#kruskal-szekeres-coordinates).

##### Schwarzschild retarded null coordinate

↑ **Parent:** [Null coordinate](#null-coordinate)

In the exterior of [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime), $dr_*/dr=(1-2M/r)^{-1}$. Outgoing [radial null geodesics](special-relativity.md#radial-null-geodesic) therefore have constant $u=t-r_*$. At fixed receiver radius $R$, their arrival Schwarzschild time is $u+r_*(R)$, so differences in $u$ give arrival-time differences. A static receiver's [proper time](special-relativity.md#proper-time) includes the factor $\sqrt{1-2M/R}$.

// Target: optics.bigb

#### Spacelike vector

↑ **Parent:** [Lorentzian metric](#lorentzian-metric)

With [metric signature](topology.md#metric-signature) $(-,+,+,+)$, a nonzero [vector](vector-space.md#vector) $v$ is spacelike when its [Lorentzian inner product](#lorentzian-inner-product) with itself satisfies $g(v,v)>0$. In [Minkowski spacetime](special-relativity.md#minkowski-spacetime), this means its spatial components have larger squared Euclidean length than its time component. The classification is preserved by [Lorentz transformations](special-relativity.md#lorentz-transformation). A [timelike vector](#timelike-vector) has negative squared [Lorentzian inner product](#lorentzian-inner-product), and a [null vector](special-relativity.md#null-vector) has zero squared [Lorentzian inner product](#lorentzian-inner-product).

#### Lorentzian inner product

↑ **Parent:** [Lorentzian metric](#lorentzian-metric)

A [Lorentzian inner product](#lorentzian-inner-product) is a nondegenerate symmetric bilinear form with one negative and the remaining positive eigenvalues in the mostly-plus convention. Two nonzero future causal vectors have nonpositive inner product; if either is timelike, it is strictly negative.

##### Null covector

↑ **Parent:** [Lorentzian inner product](#lorentzian-inner-product)

A nonzero [covector](linear-algebra.md#covector) $\ell$ is null when its squared dual [Lorentzian inner product](#lorentzian-inner-product) is zero: $g^{\alpha\beta}\ell_\alpha\ell_\beta=0$. Raising its index gives a [null vector](special-relativity.md#null-vector). Null covectors describe normals to characteristic [light cones](special-relativity.md#light-cone) and are the test directions in the [classical null condition for wave equations](wave-equation.md#classical-null-condition-for-wave-equations).

#### Causal vector

↑ **Parent:** [Lorentzian metric](#lorentzian-metric)

A nonzero [causal vector](#causal-vector) is timelike or null: $g(v,v)\le0$ with [metric signature](topology.md#metric-signature) $(-,+,+,+)$. The zero vector is commonly included when stating the [dominant energy condition](#dominant-energy-condition).

#### Timelike vector

↑ **Parent:** [Lorentzian metric](#lorentzian-metric)

A [timelike vector](#timelike-vector) $v$ obeys $g(v,v)<0$ in [metric signature](topology.md#metric-signature) $(-,+,+,+)$. Its orthogonal complement has positive-definite metric; the two timelike cones give the local future and past choices.

##### Timelike vector field

↑ **Parent:** [Timelike vector](#timelike-vector)

A [timelike vector field](#timelike-vector-field) assigns a timelike vector continuously or smoothly to every event in its domain. A globally defined such field gives a [time orientation](#time-orientation), but it need not be a [Killing vector field](#killing-vector-field) or a [timelike geodesic vector field](geodesic-congruence.md#timelike-geodesic-vector-field).

### Oblate spheroidal coordinates

↑ **Parent:** [Metric tensor](#metric-tensor)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oblate_spheroidal_coordinates)

With parameter $a\geq0$, oblate spheroidal coordinates on Euclidean three-space are

$$
x=\sqrt{r^2+a^2}\sin\theta\cos\phi,
\quad
y=\sqrt{r^2+a^2}\sin\theta\sin\phi,
\quad
z=r\cos\theta.
$$

Writing $\Sigma=r^2+a^2\cos^2\theta$ and $\Delta=r^2+a^2$, their spatial line element is

$$
dl^2=\frac\Sigma\Delta dr^2+\Sigma d\theta^2
+\Delta\sin^2\theta\,d\phi^2.
$$

#### Outgoing null oblate spheroidal coordinates

↑ **Parent:** [Oblate spheroidal coordinates](#oblate-spheroidal-coordinates)

Starting from the [Minkowski metric](special-relativity.md#minkowski-metric) in [oblate spheroidal coordinates](#oblate-spheroidal-coordinates), set $u=t-r$, $R=r$, and choose an azimuth $\varphi$ satisfying

$$
d\varphi=d\phi-\frac{a}{R^2+a^2}dR.
$$

Then

$$
ds^2=-du^2-2\,du\,dR
+2a\sin^2\theta\,dR\,d\varphi
+(R^2+a^2\cos^2\theta)d\theta^2
+(R^2+a^2)\sin^2\theta\,d\varphi^2,
$$

so the coordinate vector $\partial_R$ is null.

### Inverse metric

↑ **Parent:** [Metric tensor](#metric-tensor)

The inverse metric satisfies $g^{\mu\rho}g_{\rho\nu}=\delta^\mu_\nu$ and raises covariant tensor indices.

## Two-dimensional hyperbolic metric in polar coordinates

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)

This metric is the [hyperbolic plane](geometry-and-topology.md#hyperbolic-plane) with curvature scale $a$. Its nonzero connection coefficients are

$$
\Gamma^r_{\phi\phi}=-\sinh r\cosh r,
\qquad
\Gamma^\phi_{r\phi}=\Gamma^\phi_{\phi r}=\coth r.
$$

### Coordinate geodesics of the hyperbolic polar metric

↑ **Parent:** [Two-dimensional hyperbolic metric in polar coordinates](#two-dimensional-hyperbolic-metric-in-polar-coordinates)

Every radial coordinate line $\phi=\text{constant}$ is a geodesic. No nonconstant coordinate circle $r=r_0>0$ is a geodesic.

### Curvature of the two-dimensional hyperbolic polar metric

↑ **Parent:** [Two-dimensional hyperbolic metric in polar coordinates](#two-dimensional-hyperbolic-metric-in-polar-coordinates)

For the curvature convention

$$
R^\alpha{}_{\beta\gamma\delta}
=\partial_\gamma\Gamma^\alpha_{\beta\delta}
-\partial_\delta\Gamma^\alpha_{\beta\gamma}
+\Gamma^\mu_{\beta\delta}\Gamma^\alpha_{\mu\gamma}
-\Gamma^\mu_{\beta\gamma}\Gamma^\alpha_{\mu\delta},
$$

the independent nonzero mixed components are

$$
R^r{}_{\phi r\phi}=-\sinh^2r,
\qquad
R^\phi{}_{r\phi r}=-1,
$$

and the Ricci scalar is $R=-2/a^2$.

## Ricci scalar

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)

The Ricci scalar is the full metric contraction of the [Ricci tensor](#ricci-tensor):

$$
R=g^{\mu\nu}R_{\mu\nu}.
$$

## Geodesic congruence

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)

[This section is present in another page, follow this link to view it.](geodesic-congruence.md)

## Null hypersurface

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Null_hypersurface)

A null hypersurface has a degenerate induced metric. Its normal covector raises to a null vector tangent to the hypersurface, whose integral curves are its null generators.

### Null hypersurface normal generates null geodesics

↑ **Parent:** [Null hypersurface](#null-hypersurface)

If $n_a=\nabla_a u$ is normal to a null level set, then on the hypersurface

$$
n^b\nabla_bn_a=\frac12\nabla_a(n^2)=\kappa n_a.
$$

Thus the normal is tangent to null pregeodesics and can be rescaled to give an affine null tangent.

### Killing horizon

↑ **Parent:** [Null hypersurface](#null-hypersurface)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killing_horizon)

A Killing horizon is a [null hypersurface](#null-hypersurface) whose null generators are orbits of a [Killing vector field](#killing-vector-field) $K$. On the horizon, $K^b\nabla_bK^a=\kappa K^a$ defines its [surface gravity](#surface-gravity).

#### Horizon angular velocity

↑ **Parent:** [Killing horizon](#killing-horizon)

In an axisymmetric stationary spacetime, the [horizon angular velocity](#horizon-angular-velocity) is the constant coefficient in the null horizon generator $\chi=k+\Omega_Hm$, where $k$ is the stationary Killing vector normalized at infinity and $m$ generates axial orbits with period $2\pi$. It measures rotation relative to the stationary frame at infinity. For a [Kerr black hole](#kerr-black-hole), $\Omega_H=a/(r_+^2+a^2)$. It enters the rotating physical-process first law through the horizon flux of $E-\Omega_HJ$.

#### Bifurcate Killing horizon

↑ **Parent:** [Killing horizon](#killing-horizon)

A bifurcate Killing horizon consists of two intersecting null [Killing horizons](#killing-horizon) of the same [Killing vector field](#killing-vector-field), which vanishes on their spacelike intersection, the [bifurcation surface](#bifurcation-surface). In regular Kruskal coordinates the horizons are $U=0$ and $V=0$, and their intersection is $U=V=0$. The displayed field is tangent and normal to each horizon branch and zero on the intersection.

#### Degenerate Killing horizon

↑ **Parent:** [Killing horizon](#killing-horizon)

A [degenerate Killing horizon](#degenerate-killing-horizon) has zero [surface gravity](#surface-gravity). In the radial quotient of an [extremal black hole](#extremal-black-hole), a double horizon root produces a pole in the [tortoise coordinate](#tortoise-coordinate) rather than the logarithm of a simple root. Such a horizon has no bifurcation surface of the usual nondegenerate type.

#### Optical scalars on a Killing horizon

↑ **Parent:** [Killing horizon](#killing-horizon)

The [null expansion](geodesic-congruence.md#null-expansion), [null shear](geodesic-congruence.md#null-shear) and [null twist](geodesic-congruence.md#null-twist) of a congruence tangent to the generators of a [Killing horizon](#killing-horizon) all vanish there. If $k$ is its Killing generator and $\ell=fk$ is an affine generator, the [screen-space projector](geodesic-congruence.md#screen-space-projector) kills every term proportional to $k$. The symmetric part of the projected derivative of $\ell$ therefore vanishes by the [Killing equation](#killing-equation). The antisymmetric part vanishes by [Frobenius theorem](differential-geometry.md#frobenius-theorem), since $\ell$ is hypersurface-orthogonal. These conclusions extend through a regular [bifurcation surface](#bifurcation-surface) by continuity.

#### Cosmological horizon

↑ **Parent:** [Killing horizon](#killing-horizon)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cosmological_horizon)

A cosmological horizon is a causal boundary beyond which events cannot communicate with a specified observer because of the global expansion or geometry of spacetime.

#### Surface gravity

↑ **Parent:** [Killing horizon](#killing-horizon)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surface_gravity)

The surface gravity $\kappa$ measures the failure of a horizon-generating [Killing vector field](#killing-vector-field) to be affinely parametrized. For a static metric $ds^2=-f(r)dt^2+f(r)^{-1}dr^2+\cdots$ with a simple horizon at $r=r_+$, $\kappa=|f'(r_+)|/2$.

##### Signed surface gravity of a de Sitter horizon

↑ **Parent:** [Surface gravity](#surface-gravity)

For the future-directed central-time [Killing vector field](#killing-vector-field) in a [static patch of de Sitter spacetime](#static-patch-of-de-sitter-spacetime), the outgoing null chart gives the future branch coefficient $+1/R$ in $\nabla_k k=\kappa k$. The ingoing chart gives $-1/R$ on the past branch. Both follow from $d(k^2)=-2\kappa k^\flat$; the cross-term sign changes the horizon covector. The physical [Hawking temperature](#hawking-temperature) uses $|\kappa|$, not a signed negative temperature.

##### Surface gravity of a static spherical horizon

↑ **Parent:** [Surface gravity](#surface-gravity)

For a [static spacetime](#static-spacetime) with $g_{tt}=-A(r)$, $g_{rr}=1/B(r)$ and simple simultaneous horizon zeros, the squared [surface gravity](#surface-gravity) of $\partial_t$ is $\lim BA'^2/(4A)=A'(r_+)B'(r_+)/4$. Choose the positive value for the outer horizon. A rescaling of the time generator rescales its [surface gravity](#surface-gravity).

###### Surface gravity of a five-dimensional charged black hole

↑ **Parent:** [Surface gravity of a static spherical horizon](#surface-gravity-of-a-static-spherical-horizon)

The time translation is normalized at infinity. For the roots $r_\pm^2=M\pm\sqrt{M^2-Q^2}$, evaluate $V'(r)/2$ to get the signed horizon values. The outer [Hawking temperature](#hawking-temperature) in natural units is $\sqrt{M^2-Q^2}/(\pi r_+^3)$. The extremal limit has zero surface gravity; the neutral case has only the outer positive-radius horizon and temperature $1/(2\pi\sqrt{2M})$.

##### Surface gravity from the Killing derivative

↑ **Parent:** [Surface gravity](#surface-gravity)

At a regular [Killing horizon](#killing-horizon), the generator is hypersurface orthogonal and satisfies $\nabla_KK=\kappa K$. The [Killing derivative contraction identity](#killing-derivative-contraction-identity) gives the displayed squared [surface gravity](#surface-gravity), including through a [bifurcation surface](#bifurcation-surface) by continuity. The generator's normalization is part of this formula.

##### Affine horizon-generator scaling

↑ **Parent:** [Surface gravity](#surface-gravity)

If a [Killing horizon](#killing-horizon) generator $\xi$ has constant nonzero [surface gravity](#surface-gravity) $\kappa$, write $\xi=F(\lambda)k$ with $k=d/d\lambda$ affine. The equation $\nabla_\xi\xi=\kappa\xi$ implies $dF/d\lambda=\kappa$, so shifting the affine origin gives $\xi=\kappa\lambda k$.

##### Schwarzschild surface gravity

↑ **Parent:** [Surface gravity](#surface-gravity)

For a [Schwarzschild black hole](#schwarzschild-spacetime) of [mass](classical-mechanics.md#mass) $M$ in [geometrized units](#geometrized-units), $f=1-2M/r$ gives $\kappa=f\prime(2M)/2=1/(4M)$. The [Killing vector field](#killing-vector-field) is normalized at infinity; changing that normalization changes $\kappa$.

#### Bifurcation surface

↑ **Parent:** [Killing horizon](#killing-horizon)

A bifurcation surface is a spacelike codimension-two cross-section on which the Killing generator of a bifurcate Killing horizon vanishes. Affine parameters on the future and past branches can be chosen to vanish there.

### Gaussian null coordinates

↑ **Parent:** [Null hypersurface](#null-hypersurface)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaussian_null_coordinates)

Gaussian null coordinates adapt one coordinate to the generators of a [null hypersurface](#null-hypersurface), one affine coordinate to transverse null geodesics, and the remaining coordinates to a spacelike cross-section. If the generator parameter is affine, the metric can locally be written

$$
ds^2=2\,dr\,d\lambda+r^2F\,d\lambda^2+2rh_i\,d\lambda\,dy^i+h_{ij}\,dy^i\,dy^j.
$$

### Trapped surface

↑ **Parent:** [Null hypersurface](#null-hypersurface)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trapped_surface)

A trapped surface is a closed spacelike codimension-two surface whose two future-directed orthogonal null congruences both have negative expansion.

#### Marginally outer trapped surface

↑ **Parent:** [Trapped surface](#trapped-surface)

A closed spacelike two-surface is marginally outer trapped when its future outgoing [null expansion](geodesic-congruence.md#null-expansion) is zero. For the future black-hole type, the ingoing [null expansion](geodesic-congruence.md#null-expansion) is negative. An [apparent horizon](#apparent-horizon) on a chosen spatial slice is ordinarily its outermost such surface under suitable regularity assumptions. The notion depends on the slice and is not the global definition of an [event horizon](#event-horizon).

#### Apparent horizon

↑ **Parent:** [Trapped surface](#trapped-surface)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apparent_horizon)

An [apparent horizon](#apparent-horizon) is the outer boundary of the future-trapped region on a chosen spatial slice, under the usual regularity conditions. Its location depends on the slice; it is not the globally defined [event horizon](#event-horizon). At a regular boundary the outward [null expansion](geodesic-congruence.md#null-expansion) vanishes while the inward [null expansion](geodesic-congruence.md#null-expansion) is negative. Its area cannot be substituted into every version of the [Penrose inequality](#penrose-inequality) without the appropriate area and initial-data hypotheses.

#### Anti-trapped surface

↑ **Parent:** [Trapped surface](#trapped-surface)

An anti-trapped surface is the time reverse of a trapped surface: both future-directed orthogonal null expansions are positive.

## Null geodesic completeness

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)

A spacetime is null-geodesically complete when every inextendible affinely parametrized null geodesic has an affine parameter extending without a finite endpoint in both directions.

A [complete manifold](riemannian-geometry.md#complete-manifold) requires completeness for every geodesic, whereas this condition only tests null ones.

### Null geodesic incompleteness

↑ **Parent:** [Null geodesic completeness](#null-geodesic-completeness)

[Null geodesic incompleteness](#null-geodesic-incompleteness) means that some [maximal geodesic](riemannian-geometry.md#maximal-geodesic) with a [null tangent](special-relativity.md#null-vector) has a finite endpoint of its [affine parameter](riemannian-geometry.md#affine-parameter). This can indicate a removable missing region or a genuine obstruction; it does not alone assert curvature divergence.

### Penrose singularity theorem

↑ **Parent:** [Null geodesic completeness](#null-geodesic-completeness)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penrose_singularity_theorem)

The Penrose singularity theorem states that a globally hyperbolic spacetime with a noncompact Cauchy hypersurface, a closed trapped surface, and the null convergence condition is future null-geodesically incomplete. Time reversal replaces the trapped surface by an anti-trapped surface and gives past incompleteness.

#### Penrose theorem at a Cauchy horizon

↑ **Parent:** [Penrose singularity theorem](#penrose-singularity-theorem)

The globally hyperbolic [maximal Cauchy development](#maximal-cauchy-development) of appropriate [Reissner-Nordstrom spacetime](#reissner-nordstrom-spacetime) data obeys the [Penrose singularity theorem](#penrose-singularity-theorem) when it contains a closed [trapped surface](#trapped-surface). Its resulting [null geodesic incompleteness](#null-geodesic-incompleteness) can include generators reaching a smoothly extendible inner [Cauchy horizon](#cauchy-horizon) in finite [affine parameter](riemannian-geometry.md#affine-parameter). The full analytic extension is not globally hyperbolic and cannot be substituted into that version of the theorem. Incompleteness alone does not establish curvature blowup at every endpoint.

#### Compactness of the future horismos of a trapped surface

↑ **Parent:** [Penrose singularity theorem](#penrose-singularity-theorem)

In a future null-complete [globally hyperbolic spacetime](#globally-hyperbolic-spacetime) obeying the [null convergence condition](#null-convergence-condition), the [future horismos](#future-horismos) of a [compact](topology.md#compact-space) [trapped surface](#trapped-surface) is [compact](topology.md#compact-space). Normalize its future null normals continuously using a timelike field. [Compactness](topology.md#compact-space) gives a uniform negative bound $\theta\leq-c<0$ on both initial [null expansions](geodesic-congruence.md#null-expansion). The [null focusing theorem](geodesic-congruence.md#null-focusing-theorem) bounds all boundary generators by affine length $2/c$. Their endpoints lie in the image under a [continuous map](topology.md#continuous-map) of the [compact](topology.md#compact-space) bundle of normalized null normals times $[0,2/c]$. The [future horismos](#future-horismos) is [closed](topology.md#closed-set), so is a [compact](topology.md#compact-space) subset of that image.

## Causal hierarchy

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)

The causal hierarchy orders restrictions on the causal structure of a [spacetime](special-relativity.md#spacetime), from exclusion of [closed timelike curves](#closed-timelike-curve) through [stable causality](#stable-causality) and [global hyperbolicity](#globally-hyperbolic-spacetime).

### Observer event horizon

↑ **Parent:** [Causal hierarchy](#causal-hierarchy)

An observer's future event horizon bounds the events that can ever signal to its future-inextendible worldline. Its past event horizon bounds the events reachable by signals from its past-inextendible worldline. A complete inertial observer in [Minkowski spacetime](special-relativity.md#minkowski-spacetime) has neither. The uniformly accelerated worldline $t=a^{-1}\sinh(a\tau)$, $x=a^{-1}\cosh(a\tau)$ has $I^-(\gamma)=\{t-x<0\}$ and $I^+(\gamma)=\{t+x>0\}$. Their boundaries are its two [Rindler horizons](special-relativity.md#rindler-horizon). This observer dependence is distinct from a black-hole horizon defined using [future null infinity](#future-null-infinity).

#### Cosmological event horizon

↑ **Parent:** [Observer event horizon](#observer-event-horizon)

For a comoving observer in a spatially flat [Friedmann-Lemaître-Robertson-Walker metric](cosmology.md#friedmann-lemaitre-robertson-walker-metric), a finite future [conformal time](cosmology.md#conformal-time) interval gives an event horizon at the displayed comoving radius. Its physical radius at time $t$ is $a(t)\chi_{\mathrm{eh}}(t)$.

##### Cosmological round-trip communication horizon

↑ **Parent:** [Cosmological event horizon](#cosmological-event-horizon)

A light signal sent from a comoving observer to a comoving reflector at separation $\chi$, and immediately returned, consumes [conformal time](cosmology.md#conformal-time) $2\chi$. The supremum of the initial physical reflector distances allowing a return is therefore half the [cosmological event horizon](#cosmological-event-horizon) radius. Equality generally requires infinite return time; finite-time reception requires a strictly smaller distance. For a [cosmological domain wall network](cosmology.md#cosmological-domain-wall-network) with $a\propto t^2$, the supremum is $t/2=1/H$.

### Time orientation

↑ **Parent:** [Causal hierarchy](#causal-hierarchy)

A [time orientation](#time-orientation) is a continuous choice of future cone throughout a time-orientable [spacetime](special-relativity.md#spacetime). A continuous nonzero [timelike vector field](#timelike-vector-field) supplies such a choice. For [metric signature](topology.md#metric-signature) $(-,+,+,+)$, a nonzero causal vector $Z$ is future-directed relative to a future timelike $W$ exactly when $g(W,Z)<0$.

### Causal curve

↑ **Parent:** [Causal hierarchy](#causal-hierarchy)

A [causal curve](#causal-curve) has an everywhere nonspacelike tangent of consistent time orientation; its smooth segments are timelike or null. More generally, curves with local [Lipschitz continuity](real-analysis.md#lipschitz-continuity) and future causal tangent almost everywhere are allowed. The [causal future](#causal-future) and [causal past](#causal-past) are defined by reachability along these curves.

### Causal past

↑ **Parent:** [Causal hierarchy](#causal-hierarchy)

The [causal past](#causal-past) is the time-reversed [causal future](#causal-future). Relative to a specified component $\mathcal I^+$ of [future null infinity](#future-null-infinity), the [black hole](#black-hole) region is the complement of its [causal past](#causal-past): $\mathcal B=\mathcal M\setminus J^-(\mathcal I^+)$.

### Causal future

↑ **Parent:** [Causal hierarchy](#causal-hierarchy)

The [causal future](#causal-future) of $S$ consists of points reached from $S$ by future-directed causal curves, including points of $S$ itself. In a [globally hyperbolic spacetime](#globally-hyperbolic-spacetime), the [causal future](#causal-future) of a [compact](topology.md#compact-space) set is [closed](topology.md#closed-set).

#### Future horismos

↑ **Parent:** [Causal future](#causal-future)

The [future horismos](#future-horismos) is the part of the [causal future](#causal-future) outside the [chronological future](#chronological-future). For a [compact](topology.md#compact-space) set in a [globally hyperbolic spacetime](#globally-hyperbolic-spacetime), it equals the [boundary](topology.md#boundary-of-a-set) of the [chronological future](#chronological-future). Its normal [null geodesic](special-relativity.md#null-geodesic) generators cannot have passed a [conjugate point to a spacelike surface](geodesic-congruence.md#conjugate-point-to-a-spacelike-surface) when the initial set is that surface.

### Chronological future

↑ **Parent:** [Causal hierarchy](#causal-hierarchy)

The chronological future $I^+(S)$ of a subset $S$ of a [spacetime](special-relativity.md#spacetime) is the set of points reachable from $S$ by future-directed [timelike curves](special-relativity.md#timelike-curve). The chronological future is an [open set](topology.md#open-set).

#### Chronologically saturated subset

↑ **Parent:** [Chronological future](#chronological-future)

A subset $U$ is chronologically saturated in both directions when $I^+(U)=U=I^-(U)$. If the ambient [spacetime](special-relativity.md#spacetime) is connected and $U$ is nonempty, then $U$ is the whole spacetime.

### Chronological past

↑ **Parent:** [Causal hierarchy](#causal-hierarchy)

The chronological past $I^-(S)$ is the set of points from which a future-directed [timelike curve](special-relativity.md#timelike-curve) reaches $S$. Equivalently, it is the time reverse of the [chronological future](#chronological-future).

### Closed timelike curve

↑ **Parent:** [Causal hierarchy](#causal-hierarchy)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Closed_timelike_curve)

A closed timelike curve is a closed curve whose tangent is everywhere timelike, so an observer following it returns to the same spacetime event.

#### Closed timelike circles in negative-radius Kerr spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Closed timelike curve](#closed-timelike-curve)

On the equatorial plane of the [Kerr metric](#kerr-metric), the displayed component is negative for sufficiently small negative $r$. A curve of constant $t,r,\theta=\pi/2$, with $\phi$ increasing through $2\pi$, is therefore a regular [closed timelike curve](#closed-timelike-curve). It avoids the ring at $r=0$. Such circles occur on the negative-radius sheet of the exact analytic extension, not in the exterior region accessible from positive-radius infinity.

### Stable causality

↑ **Parent:** [Causal hierarchy](#causal-hierarchy)

A spacetime is stably causal when its light cones can be widened slightly without creating a [closed timelike curve](#closed-timelike-curve). Equivalently, it admits a continuous time function strictly increasing along every future-directed causal curve.

### Strong causality

↑ **Parent:** [Causal hierarchy](#causal-hierarchy)

Strong causality means that every event has arbitrarily small neighborhoods which no causal curve can leave and then re-enter. [Stable causality](#stable-causality) implies strong causality.

#### Future-distinguishing spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Strong causality](#strong-causality)

A spacetime is future-distinguishing when $I^+(p)=I^+(q)$ implies $p=q$. Every [strongly causal](#strong-causality) spacetime is future-distinguishing.

### Globally hyperbolic spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Causal hierarchy](#causal-hierarchy)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globally_hyperbolic_spacetime)

A globally hyperbolic spacetime has no causal pathologies and admits a Cauchy hypersurface intersected exactly once by every inextendible causal curve.

#### Initial data in general relativity

↑ **Parent:** [Globally hyperbolic spacetime](#globally-hyperbolic-spacetime)

Gravitational [initial data](#initial-data-in-general-relativity) on a spatial manifold consist of a [Riemannian metric](differential-geometry.md#riemannian-metric) $h$ and an [extrinsic curvature](differential-geometry.md#extrinsic-curvature) $K$ satisfying the [Hamiltonian constraint](numerical-relativity.md#hamiltonian-constraint) and [momentum constraint](numerical-relativity.md#momentum-constraint). Matter theories require their own data and constraints as well. The data determine a [maximal Cauchy development](#maximal-cauchy-development).

##### Maximal Cauchy development

↑ **Parent:** [Initial data in general relativity](#initial-data-in-general-relativity)

A [maximal Cauchy development](#maximal-cauchy-development) is the largest globally hyperbolic solution, unique up to an appropriate [isometry](riemannian-geometry.md#isometry), determined by admissible [initial data](#initial-data-in-general-relativity), with the initial hypersurface a [Cauchy hypersurface](#cauchy-surface). It can sometimes be extended as a spacetime across a [Cauchy horizon](#cauchy-horizon) even though that extension is no longer globally determined by the initial hypersurface.

###### Strong cosmic censorship conjecture

↑ **Parent:** [Maximal Cauchy development](#maximal-cauchy-development)

The [strong cosmic censorship conjecture](#strong-cosmic-censorship-conjecture) asserts that the [maximal Cauchy development](#maximal-cauchy-development) of generic admissible [initial data](#initial-data-in-general-relativity) is inextendible in a specified regularity class. The matter model, topology on initial data, and extension regularity are part of the assertion. An exceptional exact solution with a smooth [Cauchy horizon](#cauchy-horizon) need not violate this generic claim.

#### Achronal set

↑ **Parent:** [Globally hyperbolic spacetime](#globally-hyperbolic-spacetime)

An achronal set contains no pair of points connected by a timelike curve.

##### Achronal boundary

↑ **Parent:** [Achronal set](#achronal-set)

An achronal boundary is the boundary of a chronological future or past. Its null generators cease to belong to the boundary after developing a conjugate point or intersecting another generator in the relevant way.

#### Cauchy surface

↑ **Parent:** [Globally hyperbolic spacetime](#globally-hyperbolic-spacetime)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cauchy_surface)

A Cauchy hypersurface is an achronal hypersurface met exactly once by every inextendible timelike curve. Its topology records the global spatial topology of a globally hyperbolic spacetime.

##### Future domain of dependence

↑ **Parent:** [Cauchy surface](#cauchy-surface)

The set $D^+(\Sigma)$ of events for which every past-inextendible [causal curve](#causal-curve) meets $\Sigma$. This causal characterization permits an initial sign condition on a [null geodesic congruence](geodesic-congruence.md#null-geodesic-congruence) to propagate throughout the regular region by integrating a focusing inequality from $\Sigma$.

##### Cauchy horizon

↑ **Parent:** [Cauchy surface](#cauchy-surface)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cauchy_horizon)

The future or past Cauchy horizon of a partial Cauchy hypersurface is the boundary of the region whose physical evolution is determined by data on that hypersurface. Crossing it can destroy uniqueness of the maximal globally hyperbolic development.

###### Dependence of a Cauchy horizon on the initial hypersurface

↑ **Parent:** [Cauchy horizon](#cauchy-horizon)

A complete [Cauchy hypersurface](#cauchy-surface) in [Minkowski spacetime](special-relativity.md#minkowski-spacetime), such as $t=0$, has full [domain of dependence](partial-differential-equation.md#domain-of-dependence) and empty [Cauchy horizons](#cauchy-horizon). This does not forbid horizons for a non-Cauchy initial surface in the same spacetime. The smooth edgeless spacelike hyperboloid $t=\sqrt{a^2+r^2}$, $a>0$, has domain of dependence $t>r$ and a past [Cauchy horizon](#cauchy-horizon) on the future light cone of the origin. Every causal curve starting inside that cone crosses the hyperboloid in the direction needed for dependence: $t^2-r^2$ passes through $a^2$, while a null generator of the cone never meets it. The time-reversed hyperboloid gives the analogous future horizon.

###### Inner-horizon blueshift coordinate

↑ **Parent:** [Cauchy horizon](#cauchy-horizon)

Near a nondegenerate outgoing inner [Cauchy horizon](#cauchy-horizon), a regular null coordinate behaves as $V_-\propto-e^{-\kappa_-v}$, where $\kappa_->0$ is the magnitude of the inner-horizon [surface gravity](#surface-gravity). Transforming stress components gives $T_{V_-V_-}\propto e^{2\kappa_-v}T_{vv}$. Even a small power-law tail can therefore cause an unbounded locally measured flux, motivating [mass inflation](#mass-inflation).

###### Mass inflation

↑ **Parent:** [Cauchy horizon](#cauchy-horizon)

[Mass inflation](#mass-inflation) is the growth of the interior effective mass and curvature near an inner [Cauchy horizon](#cauchy-horizon) under suitably perturbed black-hole data. It can obstruct regular spacetime extensions that exist for an exact [Reissner-Nordstrom spacetime](#reissner-nordstrom-spacetime). The extension regularity matters: curvature blowup alone does not rule out every continuous-metric extension.

## Black hole

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_hole)

In an asymptotically predictable spacetime, a black hole is the region that cannot send a causal signal to future null infinity. Its future boundary is the event horizon.

### Kerr-Newman metric

↑ **Parent:** [Black hole](#black-hole)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerr–Newman_metric)

The stationary axisymmetric Einstein–Maxwell family of [asymptotically flat spacetimes](#asymptotically-flat-spacetime) extends the [Kerr metric](#kerr-metric) by including [electric charge](electromagnetism.md#electric-charge). Its horizon polynomial is the displayed $\Delta$; for $M^2>Q^2+a^2$ the roots are $r_\pm=M\pm\sqrt{M^2-Q^2-a^2}$. The outer [Killing horizon](#killing-horizon) has area $4\pi(r_+^2+a^2)$, angular velocity $a/(r_+^2+a^2)$ and electric potential $Qr_+/(r_+^2+a^2)$.

#### First law for the Kerr-Newman family

↑ **Parent:** [Kerr-Newman metric](#kerr-newman-metric)

Differentiate $M=(r_+^2+a^2+Q^2)/(2r_+)$, $J=Ma$ and $A=4\pi(r_+^2+a^2)$. Eliminating $dr_+$ and $da$ gives the displayed relation, with $\Omega_H=a/(r_+^2+a^2)$ and $\Phi_H=Qr_+/(r_+^2+a^2)$. Matching [Hawking temperature](#hawking-temperature) to this [First law of black-hole mechanics](#first-law-of-black-hole-mechanics) fixes the [Bekenstein-Hawking entropy](#bekenstein-hawking-entropy) as $A/4$.

#### Kerr-Newman horizon temperature

↑ **Parent:** [Kerr-Newman metric](#kerr-newman-metric)

The outer-horizon [surface gravity](#surface-gravity) is $(r_+-r_-)/(2(r_+^2+a^2))$. [Hawking radiation](#hawking-radiation) or nondegenerate [Euclidean black-hole regularity condition](#euclidean-black-hole-regularity-condition) gives $T_H=\kappa/(2\pi)$ in units $G=\hbar=c=k_B=1$. The zero-temperature extremal value is the nonextremal limit; conical regularity alone does not fix a Euclidean period at a degenerate horizon.

### Oppenheimer-Snyder model

↑ **Parent:** [Black hole](#black-hole)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oppenheimer–Snyder_model)

The [Oppenheimer-Snyder model](#oppenheimer-snyder-model) describes a homogeneous ball of [pressureless matter](cosmology.md#pressureless-matter) collapsing from rest, with a closed [FLRW metric](cosmology.md#friedmann-lemaitre-robertson-walker-metric) inside and a [Schwarzschild metric](#schwarzschild-spacetime) outside. In geometric units its interior is $ds^2=-d\tau^2+a^2(d\chi^2+\sin^2\chi\,d\Omega^2)$, with $a=a_{\max}(1+\cos\eta)/2$ and $\tau=a_{\max}(\eta+\sin\eta)/2$. The boundary is a fixed comoving angle. [Density](fluid-mechanics.md#density) conservation gives $\rho a^3$ constant, and the [Friedmann equation](cosmology.md#friedmann-equations) at the initial turning point gives $8\pi\rho_0a_{\max}^2/3=1$.

#### Oppenheimer-Snyder cloud

↑ **Parent:** [Oppenheimer-Snyder model](#oppenheimer-snyder-model)

The comoving dust ball $0\le\chi\le\chi_0<\pi/2$ in the [Oppenheimer-Snyder model](#oppenheimer-snyder-model) is an Oppenheimer-Snyder cloud. Its [pressureless matter](cosmology.md#pressureless-matter) has uniform [density](fluid-mechanics.md#density) on each constant-[proper time](special-relativity.md#proper-time) slice and reaches a simultaneous final singularity in the ideal homogeneous model.

#### Event horizon inside an Oppenheimer-Snyder cloud

↑ **Parent:** [Oppenheimer-Snyder model](#oppenheimer-snyder-model)

Radial outgoing [null geodesics](special-relativity.md#null-geodesic) in the interior of [Oppenheimer-Snyder collapse](#oppenheimer-snyder-model) obey $d\chi/d\eta=1$. Trace the ray crossing the boundary at $(\eta_H,\chi_0)$ backward: it starts at the centre at $\eta=\eta_H-\chi_0$. This ray is the [event horizon](#event-horizon); it can exist inside the cloud before the surface crosses $R=2M$. An [event horizon](#event-horizon) is determined by global escape to [future null infinity](#future-null-infinity), rather than by a local stopwatch signal.

#### Proper-time horizon crossing in homogeneous dust collapse

↑ **Parent:** [Oppenheimer-Snyder model](#oppenheimer-snyder-model)

A boundary clock in [Oppenheimer-Snyder collapse](#oppenheimer-snyder-model) reads $\tau=a_{\max}(\eta+\sin\eta)/2$. Its [event horizon](#event-horizon) crossing occurs at $R=2M$, giving the displayed angle, and the final [curvature singularity](#curvature-singularity) occurs at $\eta=\pi$. The remaining [proper time](special-relativity.md#proper-time) is $a_{\max}(\pi-\eta_H-\sin\eta_H)/2$. For $R_0\gg2M$ this approaches $4M/3$, but it is not exactly that value for finite release radius.

#### Homogeneous dust-ball matching

↑ **Parent:** [Oppenheimer-Snyder model](#oppenheimer-snyder-model)

At the boundary of an [Oppenheimer-Snyder cloud](#oppenheimer-snyder-cloud), its [areal radius](#areal-radius) is $R=a\sin\chi_0$. Matching to a [Schwarzschild metric](#schwarzschild-spacetime) gives $M=4\pi\rho R^3/3=a_{\max}\sin^3\chi_0/2$. Thus $\sin^2\chi_0=2M/R_0$ and $a_{\max}=\sqrt{R_0^3/(2M)}$. The boundary is a radial [timelike geodesic](#timelike-geodesic) with specific [Killing energy](#killing-energy) $\sqrt{1-2M/R_0}=\cos\chi_0$. Its radial equation is $\dot R^2=2M/R-2M/R_0$. The induced metric and the angular [extrinsic curvature](differential-geometry.md#extrinsic-curvature) agree on both sides, so no surface layer is required.

### Schwarzschild-Tangherlini metric

↑ **Parent:** [Black hole](#black-hole)

For $D\geq4$, the static spherical vacuum metric $ds^2=-fdt^2+f^{-1}dr^2+r^2d\Omega_{D-2}^2$ has an [event horizon](#event-horizon) at $r_h=\mu^{1/(D-3)}$. With time normalized at infinity its [ADM mass](#arnowitt-deser-misner-mass) is $M=(D-2)\Omega_{D-2}\mu/(16\pi G_D)$, where $\Omega_{D-2}$ is the unit-sphere area. For $D=5$ and $\mu=2m$, $r_h=\sqrt{2m}$ and $M=3\pi m/(4G_5)$. The spherical orbits are three-spheres, even though the radial-time [Penrose diagram](#penrose-diagram) has the same causal pattern as its four-dimensional counterpart.

#### Kruskal extension of the five-dimensional Schwarzschild-Tangherlini metric

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild-Tangherlini metric](#schwarzschild-tangherlini-metric)

The [tortoise coordinate](#tortoise-coordinate) is $r_*=r+(r_h/2)\log|(r-r_h)/(r+r_h)|$. Set $u=t-r_*$ and $v=t+r_*$, then $U=-e^{-u/r_h}$ and $V=e^{v/r_h}$ in the right exterior. The extended radial metric is $-r_h^2(r+r_h)^2e^{-2r/r_h}dU\,dV/r^2$, regular at $r=r_h$. The horizons are $UV=0$, while $r=0$ gives $UV=1$ and is a spacelike curvature singularity. Compactification by $\arctan U,\arctan V$ displays two exteriors, a future [black hole](#black-hole) and a past [white hole](#white-hole).

### Penrose inequality

↑ **Parent:** [Black hole](#black-hole)

The [Penrose inequality](#penrose-inequality) compares asymptotic energy and an appropriate horizon or enclosing area in [geometrized units](#geometrized-units). Its physical motivation uses censorship, positive radiated energy, settling to a [Kerr black hole](#kerr-black-hole), and [Hawking's area theorem](#hawking-s-area-theorem). The area variable requires care: the area of an arbitrary [apparent horizon](#apparent-horizon) on non-time-symmetric data does not give a universally valid inequality. The [Riemannian Penrose inequality](#riemannian-penrose-inequality) uses the relevant outermost [minimal surface](second-fundamental-form.md#minimal-surface) under nonnegative [scalar curvature](second-fundamental-form.md#scalar-curvature).

#### Riemannian Penrose inequality

↑ **Parent:** [Penrose inequality](#penrose-inequality)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riemannian_Penrose_inequality)

For complete asymptotically flat three-dimensional Riemannian initial data with nonnegative [scalar curvature](second-fundamental-form.md#scalar-curvature) and an outermost compact [minimal surface](second-fundamental-form.md#minimal-surface) boundary of area $A$, the [Riemannian Penrose inequality](#riemannian-penrose-inequality) gives the displayed bound. Equality is realized by the spatial exterior of [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime). Time-symmetric gravitational initial data have this form when the [Einstein field equations](#einstein-field-equations) and energy hypothesis give the required nonnegative curvature. This theorem does not justify substituting arbitrary non-time-symmetric [apparent horizon](#apparent-horizon) area into the same formula.

#### Apparent-horizon area comparison

↑ **Parent:** [Penrose inequality](#penrose-inequality)

The heuristic derivation of the [Penrose inequality](#penrose-inequality) from [Hawking's area theorem](#hawking-s-area-theorem) needs an initial area comparison, in addition to enclosure of the [apparent horizon](#apparent-horizon) by the [event horizon](#event-horizon). Set inclusion alone is not an area inequality. An [outer area-minimizing surface](second-fundamental-form.md#outer-area-minimizing-surface) or appropriate enclosing-area hypotheses supply the needed comparison in formulations where it is valid; arbitrary slice-dependent apparent-horizon area need not do so.

### Reissner-Nordstrom spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Black hole](#black-hole)

The four-dimensional charged spherically symmetric electrovacuum solution has radial function $f=1-2M/r+Q^2/r^2$. For $0<|Q|<M$, its outer and inner horizon radii are $r_\pm=M\pm\sqrt{M^2-Q^2}$. A complete two-ended spatial bridge has a [maximal Cauchy development](#maximal-cauchy-development) ending at smoothly extendible inner [Cauchy horizons](#cauchy-horizon) in the exact solution.

#### Reissner-Nordstrom Hawking temperature

↑ **Parent:** [Reissner-Nordstrom spacetime](#reissner-nordstrom-spacetime)

For $f=(r-r_+)(r-r_-)/r^2$, near-horizon Euclidean geometry has polar form $d\rho^2+[f'(r_+)/2]^2\rho^2d\tau^2$. Smoothness gives inverse [temperature](thermodynamics.md#temperature) $4\pi/f'(r_+)$ and the displayed formula. This assumes a nondegenerate [Killing horizon](#killing-horizon) and time normalized at infinity. Its nonextremal limit is zero at $M=|Q|$.

##### Charge-preserving Reissner-Nordstrom evaporation endpoint

↑ **Parent:** [Reissner-Nordstrom Hawking temperature](#reissner-nordstrom-hawking-temperature)

In the ideal thermal-cutoff model where charged particles of [mass](classical-mechanics.md#mass) $m$ are emitted only for $T>m$, a sufficiently large initial [mass](classical-mechanics.md#mass) keeps charge fixed throughout evaporation exactly when $m$ is at least the [fixed-charge Reissner-Nordstrom temperature maximum](#fixed-charge-reissner-nordstrom-temperature-maximum). Neutral radiation then approaches an extremal charged remnant of area $4\pi Q^2$. This conclusion does not include nonthermal electric-field discharge, nor establish finite-time attainment.

##### Fixed-charge Reissner-Nordstrom temperature maximum

↑ **Parent:** [Reissner-Nordstrom Hawking temperature](#reissner-nordstrom-hawking-temperature)

Set $y=r_+/|Q|\geq1$. Then $M/|Q|=(y+y^{-1})/2$ and $|Q|T=(y^{-1}-y^{-3})/(4\pi)$. Differentiation gives a unique maximum at $y=\sqrt3$, proving the displayed [mass](classical-mechanics.md#mass) and [temperature](thermodynamics.md#temperature). The curve is zero at extremality and asymptotic to $1/(8\pi M)$ at large [mass](classical-mechanics.md#mass).

#### Extremal Reissner-Nordstrom causal crossing

↑ **Parent:** [Reissner-Nordstrom spacetime](#reissner-nordstrom-spacetime)

A neutral radial timelike geodesic of an [extremal black hole](#extremal-black-hole) has conserved specific energy $E=(1-M/r)^2\dot t$, giving the displayed radial equation. An infalling geodesic reaches $r=M$ with finite nonzero $\dot r=-E$ and finite proper time. In ingoing coordinates, outgoing radial null curves have $dr/dv=(1-M/r)^2/2$; those starting inside approach $r=M$ only at infinite $v$ and cannot return to the same exterior future null infinity. This distinguishes causal access from length on a static slice, and supplies an operational [event horizon](#event-horizon) test even without strictly trapped round spheres.

#### Extremal Reissner-Nordstrom isotropic coordinates

↑ **Parent:** [Reissner-Nordstrom spacetime](#reissner-nordstrom-spacetime)

For $M=|Q|>0$ and the exterior areal radius $r>M$, integrate $d\rho/\rho=dr/(r-M)$ and choose $\rho=r-M$. The [Reissner-Nordstrom metric](#reissner-nordstrom-spacetime) becomes the displayed form. Constant-time proper distance to $\rho=0$ diverges, but these exterior coordinates do not cover the horizon; ingoing null coordinates are regular there. Infinite spatial throat length therefore does not establish absence of an [event horizon](#event-horizon).

#### Reissner-Nordstrom horizon surface gravity

↑ **Parent:** [Reissner-Nordstrom spacetime](#reissner-nordstrom-spacetime)

For $M>0$ and $M\geq|Q|$, put $r_\pm=M\pm\sqrt{M^2-Q^2}$. The stationary [Killing vector field](#killing-vector-field) normalized at infinity generates the outer [Killing horizon](#killing-horizon). In an ingoing null chart its covector is $dr$ on that horizon; the [Killing equation](#killing-equation) then gives $\nabla_k k=\tfrac12 f'(r_+)k$, where $f=(r-r_+)(r-r_-)/r^2$. Thus the positive nonextremal [surface gravity](#surface-gravity) is $(r_+-r_-)/(2r_+^2)$. It tends to zero when $M=|Q|$, where the horizon becomes a [degenerate Killing horizon](#degenerate-killing-horizon). The past horizon branch has the opposite signed nonaffinity for the same time-translation field.

#### Global structure of a charged spherical black hole

↑ **Parent:** [Reissner-Nordstrom spacetime](#reissner-nordstrom-spacetime)

For $0<|Q|<M$, a [Reissner-Nordstrom spacetime](#reissner-nordstrom-spacetime) has an outer [event horizon](#event-horizon) and an inner [Cauchy horizon](#cauchy-horizon). Between them $r$ is timelike; inside the inner horizon $r$ is spacelike and the $r=0$ [curvature singularity](#curvature-singularity) is timelike. The exact analytic extension contains repeated exterior blocks beyond the [Cauchy horizons](#cauchy-horizon). Small perturbations produce severe inner-horizon blueshift and [mass inflation](#mass-inflation), so that exact continuation is not a robust prediction for collapse. At $|Q|=M$ the horizons merge into a [degenerate Killing horizon](#degenerate-killing-horizon); for $|Q|>M$ the spherical electrovacuum solution instead has a [naked singularity](#naked-singularity).

#### Reissner-Nordstrom trapped spheres

↑ **Parent:** [Reissner-Nordstrom spacetime](#reissner-nordstrom-spacetime)

In ingoing [Reissner-Nordstrom spacetime](#reissner-nordstrom-spacetime), orient $-\partial_r$ to the future and normalize the other radial [null vector](special-relativity.md#null-vector) to $\partial_v+(f/2)\partial_r$. The [spherical null expansion in ingoing coordinates](geodesic-congruence.md#spherical-null-expansion-in-ingoing-coordinates) gives $-2/r$ and $f/r$. Hence the strictly future [trapped surfaces](#trapped-surface) among the round spheres occur exactly between the distinct horizons. At either horizon the outgoing [null expansion](geodesic-congruence.md#null-expansion) vanishes. Inside the inner horizon the same round spheres are not future trapped.

### White hole

↑ **Parent:** [Black hole](#black-hole)

A white hole is the time reverse of a [black hole](#black-hole): causal trajectories can emerge from its past singularity, while exterior future-directed trajectories cannot enter through its past horizon. The maximal [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime) contains both a future black-hole interior and a past white-hole interior. Both have the same areal-radius range, illustrating why a global time orientation and choice of extension are essential in causal statements.

### Black-hole uniqueness theorem

↑ **Parent:** [Black hole](#black-hole)

In four-dimensional asymptotically flat [general relativity](general-relativity.md), a regular stationary vacuum black hole is a [Kerr black hole](#kerr-black-hole) under the standard connectedness and analyticity hypotheses. The electrovacuum extension is the Kerr--Newman family.

#### Black-hole rigidity theorem

↑ **Parent:** [Black-hole uniqueness theorem](#black-hole-uniqueness-theorem)

For a regular analytic stationary asymptotically flat vacuum black hole with a connected nondegenerate horizon, the stationary [Killing vector field](#killing-vector-field) either generates the horizon or the spacetime admits an additional commuting axial [Killing vector field](#killing-vector-field). In the rotating case the horizon generator is $K+\Omega_H\Phi$, where $\Phi$ has closed rotational orbits. Together with the appropriate staticity and [black-hole uniqueness theorem](#black-hole-uniqueness-theorem), this identifies an equilibrium uncharged collapse remnant with the [Kerr metric](#kerr-metric). The theorem concerns an equilibrium geometry; it does not itself prove that arbitrary dynamical collapse settles to equilibrium.

#### Black-hole no-hair theorem

↑ **Parent:** [Black-hole uniqueness theorem](#black-hole-uniqueness-theorem)

The black-hole no-hair principle says that radiation carries away the higher multipole structure of a collapsing isolated body, leaving a stationary black hole specified by conserved asymptotic charges such as [mass](classical-mechanics.md#mass), [angular momentum](classical-mechanics.md#angular-momentum), and [electric charge](electromagnetism.md#electric-charge).

### Extremal black hole

↑ **Parent:** [Black hole](#black-hole)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extremal_black_hole)

An extremal black hole lies at the parameter bound where its inner and outer horizons coincide. Its [surface gravity](#surface-gravity) and hence its semiclassical [Hawking temperature](#hawking-temperature) vanish.

#### Extremal Euclidean throat

↑ **Parent:** [Extremal black hole](#extremal-black-hole)

At a degenerate static horizon the Euclidean proper radial distance diverges logarithmically, so the horizon is an infinite end rather than a finite polar origin. Its time circle is not contracted at a conical tip, and local cone regularity imposes no finite period. The vanishing limiting [surface gravity](#surface-gravity) determines zero Hawking temperature; the simple-horizon periodicity argument must not be applied unchanged at the degenerate root.

### Naked singularity

↑ **Parent:** [Black hole](#black-hole)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naked_singularity)

A naked singularity is a spacetime singularity visible to distant observers because it is not hidden behind an [event horizon](#event-horizon).

#### Weak cosmic censorship conjecture

↑ **Parent:** [Naked singularity](#naked-singularity)

The [weak cosmic censorship conjecture](#weak-cosmic-censorship-conjecture) predicts, under appropriate generic isolated initial-data and matter assumptions, that collapse singularities are hidden from distant observers by an [event horizon](#event-horizon). It motivates exterior predictability in the physical argument for the [Penrose inequality](#penrose-inequality). It is distinct from the [strong cosmic censorship conjecture](#strong-cosmic-censorship-conjecture), which concerns inextendibility of the [maximal Cauchy development](#maximal-cauchy-development).

<h3 id="five-dimensional-reissner-nordstrom-black-hole">Five-dimensional Reissner--Nordstrom black hole</h3>

↑ **Parent:** [Black hole](#black-hole)

The static spherically symmetric electrically charged black hole in five spacetime dimensions has $f(r)=1-2M/r^2+Q^2/r^4$. When $M>Q$ it has outer and inner horizon radii $r_\pm^2=M\pm\sqrt{M^2-Q^2}$; for $M=Q$ it is an [extremal black hole](#extremal-black-hole), while $M<Q$ exposes a [naked singularity](#naked-singularity).

#### Kretschmann scalar of a five-dimensional charged black hole

↑ **Parent:** [Five-dimensional Reissner--Nordstrom black hole](#five-dimensional-reissner-nordstrom-black-hole)

For $V=1-2M/r^2+Q^2/r^4$, an orthonormal-frame curvature calculation gives $(V'')^2+6(V'/r)^2+12((1-V)/r^2)^2$. Expansion gives the displayed invariant, diverging at zero radius. It is finite at every positive root of V. Thus horizon coordinate singularities are distinct from the central [curvature singularity](#curvature-singularity).

#### Causal trapping between two spherical horizons

↑ **Parent:** [Five-dimensional Reissner--Nordstrom black hole](#five-dimensional-reissner-nordstrom-black-hole)

In the regular metric $ds^2=-f\,dv^2+2\,dv\,dr+r^2d\Omega^2$, a future causal tangent has $\dot v\geq0$. For $\dot v>0$, causality gives $\dot r\leq f\dot v/2-r^2|\dot\Omega|^2/(2\dot v)$. If $f<0$ between two horizons, the areal radius must decrease. An inner event cannot reach the original exterior [future null infinity](#future-null-infinity) without traversing that band in the forbidden direction.

#### Tortoise coordinate of a five-dimensional charged black hole

↑ **Parent:** [Five-dimensional Reissner--Nordstrom black hole](#five-dimensional-reissner-nordstrom-black-hole)

For distinct horizon radii $a>b>0$, integrating $dr_\star/dr=f^{-1}$ gives $r_\star=r+\frac{a^3}{2(a^2-b^2)}\log|(r-a)/(r+a)|-\frac{b^3}{2(a^2-b^2)}\log|(r-b)/(r+b)|+C$. The [tortoise coordinate](#tortoise-coordinate) defines constant retarded or advanced coordinates on radial [null geodesics](special-relativity.md#null-geodesic).

<h4 id="komar-integral-of-a-five-dimensional-reissner-nordstrom-metric">Komar integral of a five-dimensional Reissner--Nordstrom metric</h4>

↑ **Parent:** [Five-dimensional Reissner--Nordstrom black hole](#five-dimensional-reissner-nordstrom-black-hole)

For $f=1-s/r^2+q/r^4$ and the spacetime orientation $-dt\wedge dr\wedge d\Omega_3$, the [Hodge star operator](differential-form.md#hodge-star-operator) gives $\int_{S^3}\star dk^\flat=2\pi^2r^3f^{\prime}\to4\pi^2s$. A prefactor $1/(16\pi)$ produces $\pi s/4$, whereas the dimension-adjusted ADM mass normalization produces $3\pi s/8$. The normalization and orientation must be specified rather than imported from four dimensions.

### Event horizon

↑ **Parent:** [Black hole](#black-hole)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Event_horizon)

An event horizon is the null boundary of the causal past of future null infinity. Its global definition makes it respond teleologically to matter that falls in later.

#### Horizon-free Minkowski spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Event horizon](#event-horizon)

Every event in complete [Minkowski spacetime](special-relativity.md#minkowski-spacetime) emits a future-directed [null geodesic](special-relativity.md#null-geodesic) ending on [future null infinity](#future-null-infinity). Therefore its black-hole region $M\setminus J^-(\mathcal I^+)$ is empty and there is no intrinsic event horizon. The null edges of the [Minkowski conformal compactification](#minkowski-conformal-compactification) represent infinity, not hidden regions inside the physical spacetime. An accelerated observer can instead have an observer-dependent [Rindler horizon](special-relativity.md#rindler-horizon).

### Strong asymptotic predictability

↑ **Parent:** [Black hole](#black-hole)

Strong asymptotic predictability requires the exterior region visible from future null infinity to lie inside a globally hyperbolic neighborhood of the conformal completion. It is the cosmic-censorship-type hypothesis used in classical black-hole area and topology arguments.

### Black-hole non-splitting theorem

↑ **Parent:** [Black hole](#black-hole)

In a strongly asymptotically predictable spacetime, the causal future on a later Cauchy surface of one connected component of an earlier black-hole region lies inside one connected component of the later black-hole region. A classical black hole can merge with another component, but it cannot bifurcate into causally disconnected black holes.

### Myers-Perry black hole

↑ **Parent:** [Black hole](#black-hole)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myers-Perry_black_hole)

A Myers-Perry black hole is the higher-dimensional asymptotically flat rotating vacuum generalization of the [Kerr black hole](#kerr-black-hole). In six dimensions, a solution with one nonzero rotation parameter $a$ and horizon radius $r_+$ has mass parameter $\mu=r_+(r_+^2+a^2)$.

#### Five-dimensional equal-spin Myers-Perry black hole

↑ **Parent:** [Myers-Perry black hole](#myers-perry-black-hole)

A five-dimensional [Myers-Perry black hole](#myers-perry-black-hole) with equal rotational parameters has a metric depending on a single radial coordinate. In a fibered coordinate system, its radial functions can be written $f=1-r_0^2/r^2+\alpha^2r_0^2/r^4$, $h=1+\alpha^2r_0^2/r^4$, and $\Omega=\alpha r_0^2/(r^4h)$. For $0<\alpha<r_0/2$ there are outer and inner [Killing horizons](#killing-horizon); at equality they form a [degenerate Killing horizon](#degenerate-killing-horizon). With the standard sphere identifications and $G_5=1$, its [Komar mass](#komar-mass) is $3\pi r_0^2/8$.

#### Singly rotating six-dimensional Myers-Perry black hole

↑ **Parent:** [Myers-Perry black hole](#myers-perry-black-hole)

For the singly rotating six-dimensional Myers-Perry solution with $G=1$ and $\mu=r_+(r_+^2+a^2)$,

$$
M=\frac{2\pi}{3}\mu,
\qquad
\Omega_H=\frac{a}{r_+^2+a^2},
\qquad
\kappa=\frac{3r_+^2+a^2}{2r_+(r_+^2+a^2)},
$$

and the horizon area is

$$
A_H=\frac{8\pi^2}{3}r_+^2(r_+^2+a^2).
$$

### BTZ black hole

↑ **Parent:** [Black hole](#black-hole)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BTZ_black_hole)

The BTZ black hole is a quotient of three-dimensional [Anti-de Sitter spacetime](#anti-de-sitter-spacetime). In lapse-and-shift coordinates its outer and inner horizon radii are $r_+$ and $r_-$, and its outer-horizon surface gravity is

$$
\kappa=\frac{r_+^2-r_-^2}{L^2r_+}.
$$

#### Conical singularity of negative-mass BTZ geometry

↑ **Parent:** [BTZ black hole](#btz-black-hole)

For the nonrotating [BTZ black hole](#btz-black-hole) family with $M<0$ and angular period $2\pi$, the circumference of a small circle divided by its proper radius tends to $2\pi\sqrt{-M}$. A smooth axis requires this ratio to be $2\pi$, so only $M=-1$ gives regular [Anti-de Sitter spacetime](#anti-de-sitter-spacetime). Other values have a [conical singularity](riemannian-geometry.md#conical-singularity), with deficit angle $\delta$ or an angular excess when $\delta<0$. The lapse $r^2/\ell^2-M$ is strictly positive, so outward [radial null geodesics](special-relativity.md#radial-null-geodesic) escape from arbitrarily near the axis: the defect is a [naked singularity](#naked-singularity), despite finite local curvature away from it.

#### Ingoing BTZ coordinates

↑ **Parent:** [BTZ black hole](#btz-black-hole)

For

$$
ds^2=-fdt^2+\frac{dr^2}{f}+r^2(d\phi-\Omega dt)^2,
$$

the transformations $dv=dt+dr/f$ and $d\chi=d\phi+\Omega\,dr/f$ give

$$
ds^2=-f\,dv^2+2\,dv\,dr+r^2(d\chi-\Omega\,dv)^2,
$$

which is regular at every horizon where $f$ vanishes while $\Omega$ remains finite.

#### Trapped circle inside a nonrotating BTZ black hole

↑ **Parent:** [BTZ black hole](#btz-black-hole)

For the nonrotating BTZ metric in ingoing coordinates, future null normals to a circle of fixed $v,r$ may be chosen as $\ell=\partial_v+(f/2)\partial_r$ and $n=-\partial_r$. Their expansions are $\theta_{(\ell)}=f/(2r)$ and $\theta_{(n)}=-1/r$, so both are negative inside the outer horizon.

#### BTZ causal singularity

↑ **Parent:** [BTZ black hole](#btz-black-hole)

The BTZ black hole can be null-geodesically incomplete at $r=0$ even though every local curvature invariant is finite there. The endpoint comes from the global anti-de Sitter quotient rather than curvature blowup.

<h3 id="schwarzschild-anti-de-sitter-black-hole">Schwarzschild--anti-de Sitter black hole</h3>

↑ **Parent:** [Black hole](#black-hole)

In four dimensions the Schwarzschild--anti-de Sitter black hole has

$$
f(r)=1+\frac{r^2}{L^2}-\frac{2M}{r}.
$$

Writing the horizon radius as $r_+$ gives $M=r_+(1+r_+^2/L^2)/2$ and

$$
T_H=\frac{1+3r_+^2/L^2}{4\pi r_+}.
$$

<h4 id="five-dimensional-schwarzschild-anti-de-sitter-black-hole">Five-dimensional Schwarzschild--anti-de Sitter black hole</h4>

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild--anti-de Sitter black hole](#schwarzschild-anti-de-sitter-black-hole)

The spherical five-dimensional Schwarzschild--anti-de Sitter metric has $f(r)=1+r^2/L^2-\mu/r^2$. Its horizon satisfies $\mu=r_H^2(1+r_H^2/L^2)$ and has area $2\pi^2r_H^3$.

<h4 id="canonical-stability-of-a-schwarzschild-anti-de-sitter-black-hole">Canonical stability of a Schwarzschild--anti-de Sitter black hole</h4>

↑ **Parent:** [Schwarzschild--anti-de Sitter black hole](#schwarzschild-anti-de-sitter-black-hole)

The Schwarzschild--anti-de Sitter heat capacity has the sign of $dT_H/dr_+$. It is positive precisely for $r_+>L/\sqrt3$, so these large black holes are locally stable in a fixed-temperature reservoir.

### Black string

↑ **Parent:** [Black hole](#black-hole)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_string)

A black string is a higher-dimensional black object whose horizon extends along one spatial direction. The direct product of a $d$-dimensional static black-hole metric with a line or circle gives a uniform black string in $d+1$ spacetime dimensions.

### Penrose diagram

↑ **Parent:** [Black hole](#black-hole)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penrose_diagram)

A Penrose diagram conformally compactifies a spacetime while preserving causal directions, placing null infinity, timelike infinity, spacelike infinity, horizons, and singularities at finite coordinate locations.

#### Schwarzschild conformal compactification

↑ **Parent:** [Penrose diagram](#penrose-diagram)

For the [Kruskal–Szekeres coordinates](#kruskal-szekeres-coordinates) of a positive-mass [Schwarzschild spacetime](#schwarzschild-spacetime), $UV=(1-r/(2M))e^{r/(2M)}$. The displayed [conformal compactification](geometry-and-topology.md#conformal-compactification) makes radial [null geodesics](special-relativity.md#null-geodesic) lines of slope $\pm1$. The [Killing horizons](#killing-horizon) are $T=\pm X$, and the spacelike [curvature singularities](#curvature-singularity) $r=0$ lie at $T=\pm\pi/4$, with $|X|<\pi/4$. Two asymptotically flat exterior regions each have [past null infinity](#past-null-infinity), [null infinity](#null-infinity) to the future, [timelike infinity](#timelike-infinity) and [spacelike infinity](#spacelike-infinity). The two exterior regions cannot communicate by any [causal curve](#causal-curve); future-directed curves that cross a future horizon instead end at the black-hole singularity.
