#### Spacelike infinity

↑ **Parent:** [Penrose diagram](#penrose-diagram)

[Spacelike infinity](#spacelike-infinity) is the ideal endpoint of spatial directions in the [Minkowski conformal compactification](#minkowski-conformal-compactification), represented by $(T,R)=(0,\pi)$. Unlike [null infinity](#null-infinity), it is a vertex of the radial [Penrose diagram](#penrose-diagram).

#### Timelike infinity

↑ **Parent:** [Penrose diagram](#penrose-diagram)

Future and past [timelike infinity](#timelike-infinity) are ideal endpoints of asymptotically escaping timelike trajectories in a [conformal completion](#conformal-completion). For the [Minkowski conformal compactification](#minkowski-conformal-compactification), they are the vertices $(T,R)=(\pi,0)$ and $(-\pi,0)$; these vertices are not physical spacetime events.

##### Past timelike infinity

↑ **Parent:** [Timelike infinity](#timelike-infinity)

This is the past endpoint of complete inertial [timelike geodesics](#timelike-geodesic) in the [Minkowski conformal compactification](#minkowski-conformal-compactification), at $(T,R)=(-\pi,0)$. It is the time reversal of [future timelike infinity](#future-timelike-infinity) and is reached at infinite physical [proper time](special-relativity.md#proper-time) to the past.

##### Future timelike infinity

↑ **Parent:** [Timelike infinity](#timelike-infinity)

This is the future endpoint of complete inertial [timelike geodesics](#timelike-geodesic) in the [Minkowski conformal compactification](#minkowski-conformal-compactification), at $(T,R)=(\pi,0)$. The endpoint has finite unphysical coordinate time but infinite physical [proper time](special-relativity.md#proper-time). Accelerated timelike observers need not have this endpoint; a [Rindler horizon](special-relativity.md#rindler-horizon) can accompany an observer asymptotic to a null direction instead.

#### Null infinity

↑ **Parent:** [Penrose diagram](#penrose-diagram)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Null_infinity)

[Null infinity](#null-infinity) is the ideal boundary reached by asymptotically escaping [null geodesics](special-relativity.md#null-geodesic) in a suitable conformal completion. It has future and past components. A finite location on a [Penrose diagram](#penrose-diagram) need not represent a finite [affine parameter](riemannian-geometry.md#affine-parameter).

##### Past null infinity

↑ **Parent:** [Null infinity](#null-infinity)

[Past null infinity](#past-null-infinity) is the ideal boundary from which incoming [null geodesics](special-relativity.md#null-geodesic) arrive in an [asymptotically flat spacetime](#asymptotically-flat-spacetime). In the [Minkowski conformal compactification](#minkowski-conformal-compactification) it is $T-R=-\pi$, with the retarded coordinate tending to $-\infty$ and the advanced coordinate finite.

#### Causal projection along a spacelike circular fiber

↑ **Parent:** [Penrose diagram](#penrose-diagram)

For $g=q+C(d\psi+A)^2$ with $C>0$, projection of every causal curve is causal for $q$, since $q(\dot\gamma,\dot\gamma)\leq g(\dot\gamma,\dot\gamma)\leq0$. Conversely, the lift defined by $d\psi=-A$ along any given base curve has the same norm as that curve. Thus the orbit-space metric $q$ describes the projected causal structure exactly. A [Penrose diagram](#penrose-diagram) suppressing that circle is a quotient diagram, not the metric of a constant-$\psi$ slice.

#### Tortoise coordinate

↑ **Parent:** [Penrose diagram](#penrose-diagram)

A [tortoise coordinate](#tortoise-coordinate) straightens radial null directions. For a static radial metric $-F(r)dt^2+G(r)dr^2$ outside a horizon, choose $dr_*/dr=\sqrt{G/F}$; then $t\pm r_*$ are constant along the corresponding radial [null geodesics](special-relativity.md#null-geodesic). Continuation across horizons requires choosing branches consistently rather than replacing an analytic derivative by its absolute value.

### Black-hole thermodynamics

↑ **Parent:** [Black hole](#black-hole)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black-hole_thermodynamics)

Black-hole thermodynamics relates horizon geometry to thermodynamic laws, assigning temperature and entropy to a stationary black hole.

#### Black-hole energy extraction

↑ **Parent:** [Black-hole thermodynamics](#black-hole-thermodynamics)

Black-hole energy extraction transfers part of a black hole's mass, charge, or rotational energy to its exterior. A reversible limiting process preserves the horizon area and saturates the maximum extraction permitted by the [First law of black-hole mechanics](#first-law-of-black-hole-mechanics) and [Hawking's area theorem](#hawking-s-area-theorem).

#### Laws of black-hole mechanics

↑ **Parent:** [Black-hole thermodynamics](#black-hole-thermodynamics)

The laws of black-hole mechanics relate [surface gravity](#surface-gravity), horizon area, mass, angular momentum, and charge in close analogy with the laws of thermodynamics. Quantum theory identifies the analogy physically through [Hawking temperature](#hawking-temperature) and [Bekenstein-Hawking entropy](#bekenstein-hawking-entropy).

#### Zeroth law of black-hole mechanics

↑ **Parent:** [Black-hole thermodynamics](#black-hole-thermodynamics)

The zeroth law states that the [surface gravity](#surface-gravity) is constant over a stationary Killing horizon under the standard field-equation and energy assumptions.

#### Hawking temperature

↑ **Parent:** [Black-hole thermodynamics](#black-hole-thermodynamics)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawking_temperature)

In units $G=\hbar=c=k_B=1$, a stationary black hole with surface gravity $\kappa$ has Hawking temperature $T_H=\kappa/(2\pi)$.

##### Euclidean black-hole regularity condition

↑ **Parent:** [Hawking temperature](#hawking-temperature)

Near a nonextremal Euclidean horizon, the radial--time plane is smooth only when Euclidean time has period $\beta_H=4\pi/f'(r_H)$. Any other period produces a conical singularity at the horizon.

###### Euclidean metric near a simple static horizon

↑ **Parent:** [Euclidean black-hole regularity condition](#euclidean-black-hole-regularity-condition)

On the side where V is positive, set $r-r_0=V'(r_0)\rho^2/4$. Its radial Euclidean metric becomes the displayed plane in polar coordinates. The would-be singularity is conical unless the angular variable $|V'(r_0)|\tau/2$ has period exactly $2\pi$. This fixes $\beta=4\pi/|V'(r_0)|$; a nontrivial integer multiple still has a cone excess at the tip.

###### Euclidean temperature and signed horizon surface gravity

↑ **Parent:** [Euclidean black-hole regularity condition](#euclidean-black-hole-regularity-condition)

For a normalized static metric $ds^2=-Vdt^2+dr^2/V+r^2d\Omega^2$ with a simple outer zero at $r_0$, set $b=V'(r_0)>0$. The [Wick rotation](perturbative-quantum-field-theory.md#wick-rotation) and $r-r_0=b\rho^2/4$ give $d\rho^2+(b\rho/2)^2dt_E^2$ to leading order, so smoothness fixes period $4\pi/b$. In an ingoing null chart, $\nabla_k k=(b/2)k$ on the future horizon; the outgoing chart gives the opposite sign on the past branch. The [Hawking temperature](#hawking-temperature) is $b/(4\pi)=|\kappa|/(2\pi)$ and uses the time generator normalized at infinity.

###### Euclidean horizon period under a regular radial conformal factor

↑ **Parent:** [Euclidean black-hole regularity condition](#euclidean-black-hole-regularity-condition)

For a Euclidean radial-time metric $C(r)(f(r)dt_E^2+dr^2/f(r))$, assume $f$ has a simple horizon zero and $C$ is smooth, finite and positive there. A proper radial coordinate $\rho=2\sqrt{C(r_H)(r-r_H)/f'(r_H)}$ puts the leading metric in the form $d\rho^2+f'(r_H)^2\rho^2dt_E^2/4$. Smoothness fixes the displayed period independently of $C(r_H)$. When the Lorentzian time is normalized at infinity, the period is the inverse [Hawking temperature](#hawking-temperature) in natural units. A conformal factor singular at the horizon need not preserve this argument.

// Target: general-relativity.bigb

##### Hawking radiation

↑ **Parent:** [Hawking temperature](#hawking-temperature)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawking_radiation)

Hawking radiation is the approximately thermal flux seen at future infinity when outgoing quantum-field modes are traced backward through a collapsing black-hole geometry. The exponential near-horizon redshift produces nonzero Bogoliubov beta coefficients at temperature $T_H=\kappa/(2\pi)$.

###### Collapse vacuum for Hawking radiation

↑ **Parent:** [Hawking radiation](#hawking-radiation)

In a collapse spacetime, choose the quantum field's incoming state to have no incoming particles at [past null infinity](#past-null-infinity) and to be regular in freely falling coordinates near the forming [event horizon](#event-horizon). The late outgoing modes then have the [Hawking temperature](#hawking-temperature), while there is no incoming thermal bath. This collapse-state boundary condition differs from an equilibrium state on an eternal [black hole](#black-hole). The [Hawking exponential ray map](#hawking-exponential-ray-map) and the [thermal ratio of Hawking Bogoliubov coefficients](#thermal-ratio-of-hawking-bogoliubov-coefficients) determine the leading late-time particle spectrum; [greybody factors](#greybody-factor) describe scattering outside the [event horizon](#event-horizon).

###### Black-hole evaporation

↑ **Parent:** [Hawking radiation](#hawking-radiation)

[Black-hole evaporation](#black-hole-evaporation) is mass loss through [Hawking radiation](#hawking-radiation). For a large isolated [Schwarzschild black hole](#schwarzschild-spacetime), the leading mass dependence is $dM/dt\propto-M^{-2}$ and the evaporation timescale is proportional to $M^3$. This semiclassical scaling cannot determine the endpoint when the curvature reaches quantum-gravitational scales.

###### Semiclassical cubic mass law for Schwarzschild evaporation

↑ **Parent:** [Black-hole evaporation](#black-hole-evaporation)

For a large isolated [Schwarzschild black hole](#schwarzschild-spacetime) with a fixed effective set of radiated species, [Hawking radiation](#hawking-radiation) has $T\propto M^{-1}$ and luminosity $P=\alpha/M^2$ in natural units. Integrating $dM/dt=-\alpha/M^2$ gives the displayed law. A horizon-area [blackbody radiation](statistical-physics.md#black-body-radiation) estimate gives $\alpha=1/(15360\pi)$; [greybody factors](#greybody-factor) and species change it. With mass-dependent $\alpha$, integrate $t=\int_{M(t)}^{M_0}m^2\,dm/\alpha(m)$ instead. The [semiclassical gravity](quantum-theory.md#semiclassical-gravity) approximation fails near [Planck mass](physics.md#planck-mass), so a formal zero of the cubic law does not settle the final state.

###### Stefan-Boltzmann estimate of Schwarzschild evaporation time

↑ **Parent:** [Black-hole evaporation](#black-hole-evaporation)

Approximate an isolated [Schwarzschild black hole](#schwarzschild-spacetime) as emitting photon [blackbody radiation](statistical-physics.md#black-body-radiation) from an area $16\pi G^2M^2/c^4$ at [Hawking temperature](#hawking-temperature) $\hbar c^3/(8\pi Gk_BM)$. The [Stefan–Boltzmann law](thermodynamics.md#stefan-boltzmann-law) gives $P=\hbar c^6/(15360\pi G^2M^2)$. Integrating $c^2dM/dt=-P$ gives the displayed lifetime, about $2\times10^{67}$ years for a solar mass. [Greybody factors](#greybody-factor) and additional emitted species modify the coefficient; the robust large-mass scaling is $M_0^3$, and the semiclassical calculation does not determine the final quantum-gravity endpoint.

// Target: general-relativity.bigb

###### Hawking exponential ray map

↑ **Parent:** [Hawking radiation](#hawking-radiation)

The late outgoing rays from a nonextremal collapsing [black hole](#black-hole) have an exponential relation between a regular early affine null coordinate $U$ and late retarded time $u$. Logarithmic divergence of the [tortoise coordinate](#tortoise-coordinate) at the horizon gives $U_H-U=Ae^{-\kappa u}$ up to subleading terms. Backward propagation turns $e^{-i\omega u}$ into $(U_H-U)^{i\omega/\kappa}$; its positive- and negative-frequency Fourier parts give a thermal [Bogoliubov transformation](quantum-field-theory.md#bogoliubov-transformation).

###### Thermal ratio of Hawking Bogoliubov coefficients

↑ **Parent:** [Hawking exponential ray map](#hawking-exponential-ray-map)

For the [Hawking exponential ray map](#hawking-exponential-ray-map), regulated integrals $\int_0^\infty x^{ia}e^{-(\epsilon\pm i\omega')x}\,dx=\Gamma(1+ia)(\epsilon\pm i\omega')^{-1-ia}$ have squared-modulus ratio $e^{-2\pi a}$ as $\epsilon\downarrow0$, where $a=\omega/\kappa$. Combining this with the [canonical identities for a bosonic Bogoliubov transformation](quantum-field-theory.md#canonical-identities-for-a-bosonic-bogoliubov-transformation) gives the bosonic occupation $[e^{2\pi\omega/\kappa}-1]^{-1}$. Continuum modes require wave-packet normalization to interpret finite particle counts.

###### Greybody factor

↑ **Parent:** [Hawking radiation](#hawking-radiation)

For a [Schwarzschild black hole](#schwarzschild-spacetime), a [greybody factor](#greybody-factor) is a mode's transmission probability through the exterior scattering potential. The spectrum of [Hawking radiation](#hawking-radiation) at [future null infinity](#future-null-infinity) is the thermal horizon occupation multiplied by this factor, with a sum over angular modes.

###### Black hole information paradox

↑ **Parent:** [Hawking radiation](#hawking-radiation)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_hole_information_paradox)

The black hole information paradox is the apparent conflict between unitary quantum evolution and the semiclassical prediction that complete black-hole evaporation leaves only thermal [Hawking radiation](#hawking-radiation). A resolution must explain how information is preserved or why one of the assumptions behind the conflict fails.

#### Bekenstein-Hawking entropy

↑ **Parent:** [Black-hole thermodynamics](#black-hole-thermodynamics)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bekenstein-Hawking_entropy)

In units $G=\hbar=c=k_B=1$, a black hole of horizon area $A$ has entropy $S_{BH}=A/4$.

##### Conical-singularity derivation of black-hole entropy

↑ **Parent:** [Bekenstein-Hawking entropy](#bekenstein-hawking-entropy)

Changing the Euclidean time period away from its smooth value creates a curvature delta function at the horizon. Applying $(1-\beta\partial_\beta)$ to the Euclidean gravitational partition function isolates this term and gives $S=A/(4G_N)$.

<h4 id="gibbons-hawking-york-boundary-term">Gibbons–Hawking–York boundary term</h4>

↑ **Parent:** [Black-hole thermodynamics](#black-hole-thermodynamics)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gibbons–Hawking–York_boundary_term)

The Gibbons–Hawking–York boundary term makes the Einstein--Hilbert variational problem well posed when the induced boundary metric is fixed. It is the integral of the trace of the boundary's extrinsic curvature with the conventional normalization and sign.

#### First law of black-hole mechanics

↑ **Parent:** [Black-hole thermodynamics](#black-hole-thermodynamics)

For a nonrotating uncharged stationary black hole, the first law is $\delta M=\kappa\delta A/(8\pi)=T_H\delta S_{BH}$.

##### Physical-process first law of black-hole mechanics

↑ **Parent:** [First law of black-hole mechanics](#first-law-of-black-hole-mechanics)

The physical-process first law derives the area change caused by a small flux of matter through an initially and finally stationary horizon. Linearized [Null Raychaudhuri equation](geodesic-congruence.md#null-raychaudhuri-equation) evolution converts the Killing-energy flux into $\Delta M=\kappa\Delta A/(8\pi)$.

###### First-order horizon-area response to matter flux

↑ **Parent:** [Physical-process first law of black-hole mechanics](#physical-process-first-law-of-black-hole-mechanics)

For a small perturbation of a stationary horizon, the linearized [Null Raychaudhuri equation](geodesic-congruence.md#null-raychaudhuri-equation) gives $\delta\theta^{\prime}=-8\pi\delta T_{kk}$. With vanishing boundary term $[\lambda\delta\theta]$, integration by parts gives $\delta A=8\pi\int\lambda\delta T_{kk}\,d\lambda\,dA_0$. Combining this with [affine horizon-generator scaling](#affine-horizon-generator-scaling) converts area response to horizon Killing-energy flux.

###### Physical-process first law for a rotating black hole

↑ **Parent:** [Physical-process first law of black-hole mechanics](#physical-process-first-law-of-black-hole-mechanics)

For horizon generator $\xi=k+\Omega_Hm$, a small matter flux into an initially and finally stationary rotating black hole satisfies

$$
\Delta M-\Omega_H\Delta J=\frac{\kappa}{8\pi}\Delta A.
$$

#### Second law of black-hole mechanics

↑ **Parent:** [Black-hole thermodynamics](#black-hole-thermodynamics)

Under the [null energy condition](#null-energy-condition) and the usual global assumptions excluding naked pathologies, the area of every cross-section of a future [event horizon](#event-horizon) is nondecreasing toward the future. The [Null Raychaudhuri equation](geodesic-congruence.md#null-raychaudhuri-equation) and future completeness rule out negative expansion of its generators.

<h5 id="hawking-s-area-theorem">Hawking's area theorem</h5>

↑ **Parent:** [Second law of black-hole mechanics](#second-law-of-black-hole-mechanics)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawking's_area_theorem)

Hawking's area theorem states that the total area of classical future event horizons cannot decrease when the [null energy condition](#null-energy-condition) and the required global assumptions hold. It is the geometric content of the [second law of black-hole mechanics](#second-law-of-black-hole-mechanics).

###### Area bound on equal-mass Schwarzschild merger radiation

↑ **Parent:** [Hawking's area theorem](#hawking-s-area-theorem)

Two initially well-separated [Schwarzschild black holes](#schwarzschild-spacetime) of mass $m$ each have total horizon area $32\pi G^2m^2/c^4$. If they merge into a [Schwarzschild black hole](#schwarzschild-spacetime), [Hawking's area theorem](#hawking-s-area-theorem) gives final mass $M\ge\sqrt2m$. Conserved total energy then bounds escaped radiation by the displayed expression, or $1-1/\sqrt2$ of the initial rest energy. This is an upper bound under classical horizon and energy assumptions, not a proof that an actual merger can saturate it.

###### Future-complete horizon focusing proof of the area theorem

↑ **Parent:** [Hawking's area theorem](#hawking-s-area-theorem)

For future-complete generators of an [event horizon](#event-horizon), the [null convergence condition](#null-convergence-condition) and hypersurface orthogonality reduce the [Null Raychaudhuri equation](geodesic-congruence.md#null-raychaudhuri-equation) to $\theta'\leq-\theta^2/2$. If $\theta_0<0$, integration gives focusing within affine distance $2/|\theta_0|$. The [null focusing theorem](geodesic-congruence.md#null-focusing-theorem) identifies this with a [conjugate point to a spacelike surface](geodesic-congruence.md#conjugate-point-to-a-spacelike-surface). A null generator cannot remain on an [achronal boundary](#achronal-boundary) beyond such a focal point, contradicting future completeness on the horizon. Thus $\theta\geq0$, and $dA/d\lambda=\theta A\geq0$ gives the [second law of black-hole mechanics](#second-law-of-black-hole-mechanics). New horizon generators may join toward the future at crease sets, adding area, but generators have no future endpoints on a regular event horizon under these assumptions.

// Target: quantum-field-theory.bigb

#### Third law of black-hole mechanics

↑ **Parent:** [Black-hole thermodynamics](#black-hole-thermodynamics)

The third law of black-hole mechanics says that no finite physical process can reduce the [surface gravity](#surface-gravity) of a regular black-hole horizon to zero.

#### Generalized second law

↑ **Parent:** [Black-hole thermodynamics](#black-hole-thermodynamics)

The generalized second law states that the sum of [Bekenstein-Hawking entropy](#bekenstein-hawking-entropy) and the entropy of matter and radiation outside black holes does not decrease.

### Kerr black hole

↑ **Parent:** [Black hole](#black-hole)

The Kerr black hole is the asymptotically flat stationary axisymmetric vacuum black hole with mass $M$ and angular momentum $J=Ma$.

A Kerr black hole is the [Kerr metric](#kerr-metric) with mass $M>0$ and spin parameter satisfying $|a|\leq M$ in geometrized units; its outer horizon is $r_+=M+\sqrt{M^2-a^2}$.

#### Lense-Thirring precession

↑ **Parent:** [Kerr black hole](#kerr-black-hole)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lense–Thirring_precession)

Lense-Thirring precession is the rotation of orbital angular momentum caused by the gravitational field of a spinning mass. In the secular weak-field picture, the precession vector is parallel to the central spin. Thus an annulus has [torque](classical-mechanics.md#torque) proportional to $\mathbf J_h\times\delta\mathbf J_d$, perpendicular to $\mathbf J_h$. Summing such torques preserves this orthogonality even if their radial coefficients differ. Dissipation and the internal response of a warped [accretion disk](astrophysics.md#accretion-disk) can supplement precession with an alignment torque.

##### Frame dragging inside a slowly rotating spherical shell

↑ **Parent:** [Lense-Thirring precession](#lense-thirring-precession)

For a weakly gravitating thin spherical shell of mass $M$, radius $R$ and uniform angular velocity $\Omega$, its interior [linearized gravity](#linearized-gravity) metric has constant scalar potentials $A=-GM/R$, $C=GM/R$ and $g_{0i}=\omega(\widehat{\mathbf z}\times\mathbf x)_i$. The scalar mass shell is a monopole. Its mass current is a dipole: regular interior and decaying exterior radial solutions are proportional to $r$ and $r^{-2}$, and their derivative jump fixes the factor $4/3$. Local inertial axes co-rotate with the shell at $\omega$ relative to asymptotic nonrotating axes. This is the leading interior [Lense-Thirring precession](#lense-thirring-precession), neglecting quadratic rotation and higher gravitational orders.

###### Inertial coordinates inside a slowly rotating spherical shell

↑ **Parent:** [Frame dragging inside a slowly rotating spherical shell](#frame-dragging-inside-a-slowly-rotating-spherical-shell)

At linear order in $\epsilon=GM/R$ and the leading rotation term, this coordinate change takes the interior shell metric to [Minkowski spacetime](special-relativity.md#minkowski-spacetime). Consequently the retained interior [Riemann curvature tensor](#riemann-curvature-tensor) vanishes although inertial axes rotate relative to distant reference axes. A freely moving particle has $\mathbf X=\mathbf X_0+\mathbf V T$, while its original coordinates obey $d^2\mathbf x/dt^2=2\boldsymbol\omega\times d\mathbf x/dt$ to first rotation order. Terms of order $\omega^2$ would include centrifugal acceleration and are outside this approximation. A [gyroscope](classical-mechanics.md#gyroscope) at rest to the retained order has its parallel-transported spin precessing at $\boldsymbol\omega$ in the original coordinates.

##### Black-hole and disk alignment under internal torque

↑ **Parent:** [Lense-Thirring precession](#lense-thirring-precession)

For the opposite internal torques on a [black hole](#black-hole) and its [accretion disk](astrophysics.md#accretion-disk), the total [angular momentum](classical-mechanics.md#angular-momentum) $\mathbf J_t$ and the black-hole spin magnitude $J_h$ are constant. If $A=K_2|\mathbf J_h\times\mathbf J_d|^2$, then $d(\mathbf J_h\cdot\mathbf J_t)/dt=A$ and $dJ_d^2/dt=-2A$. The $K_1$ term produces [precession](classical-mechanics.md#precession); the positive $K_2$ term increases the alignment of the hole with the total vector while decreasing the disk angular-momentum magnitude. The exact direction equation is $\dot c=K_2J_hJ_t(1-c^2)$ for $c=\cos\angle(\mathbf J_h,\mathbf J_t)$.

###### Counteralignment of a black hole and accretion disk

↑ **Parent:** [Black-hole and disk alignment under internal torque](#black-hole-and-disk-alignment-under-internal-torque)

After persistent dissipative alignment of the hole with the conserved total [angular momentum](classical-mechanics.md#angular-momentum), the final vectors are $\mathbf J_{h,f}=J_h\widehat{\mathbf J}_t$ and $\mathbf J_{d,f}=(J_t-J_h)\widehat{\mathbf J}_t$. The disk is therefore antiparallel to the hole if $J_t<J_h$. Squaring $J_t^2=J_h^2+J_{d,0}^2+2J_hJ_{d,0}\cos\theta_0$ gives the displayed initial-angle criterion. The disk and hole need not finish aligned with each other even though the hole aligns with the total. Equality $J_t=J_h$ leaves zero final disk angular momentum rather than a defined disk spin direction.

###### Persistent-torque criterion for asymptotic spin alignment

↑ **Parent:** [Black-hole and disk alignment under internal torque](#black-hole-and-disk-alignment-under-internal-torque)

For nonzero $J_h,J_t$ and a noncollinear initial configuration, the alignment equation integrates to $\operatorname{artanh}c(t)=\operatorname{artanh}c(0)+J_hJ_t\int_0^tK_2(s)\,ds$. Hence the positive dissipative torque aligns the hole with the total vector if its integrated strength diverges. A fixed positive coefficient is sufficient. Pointwise positivity alone need not suffice for time-dependent coefficients: with $J_h=J_t=1$, $K_1=0$, $K_2=e^{-t}$ and $c(0)=0$, the limiting cosine is $\tanh1<1$. Exactly antiparallel initial spin and total angular momentum form a stationary, nongeneric exception.

#### Kerr axial analytic extension

↑ **Parent:** [Kerr black hole](#kerr-black-hole)

The two-dimensional symmetry-axis restriction of the [Kerr metric](#kerr-metric) has simple horizons at $r_\pm$ for $0<|a|<M$, but is regular at $r=0$ because the ring is off-axis. Its static region below the inner horizon continues to an asymptotically flat end at negative infinity. The horizon blocks repeat in the maximal analytic extension. This ideal continuation is distinct from a claim of physical stability at the [Cauchy horizon](#cauchy-horizon).

##### Kerr axial timelike effective potential

↑ **Parent:** [Kerr axial analytic extension](#kerr-axial-analytic-extension)

Conserved stationary energy and unit timelike normalization give this equation in the [Kerr axial analytic extension](#kerr-axial-analytic-extension). For $a>0$, the negative-sheet maximum of the potential is $1+M/a$ at $r=-a$. Energy squared above this maximum permits an inward orbit to pass through regular $r=0$ and continue to negative infinity without a turning point. At energy squared one, zero is a turning point. For negative spin the threshold is expressed with $|a|$.

#### Kerr ring singularity

↑ **Parent:** [Kerr black hole](#kerr-black-hole)

For nonzero spin, the curvature singularity of the [Kerr metric](#kerr-metric) is the ring $x^2+y^2=a^2$, $z=0$ in oblate Cartesian coordinates. The rest of the $r=0$ disk is regular and allows continuation to negative $r$. The entire disk must not be mistaken for a curvature singularity, and axial trajectories do not hit the ring.

#### Scalar wave separation in Kerr spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Kerr black hole](#kerr-black-hole)

For the massless [Klein-Gordon equation](wave-equation.md#klein-gordon-equation) on a [Kerr black hole](#kerr-black-hole), the [covariant wave operator](quantum-field-theory.md#covariant-wave-operator) separates into radial and angular [ordinary differential equations](differential-equation.md#ordinary-differential-equation). With $K=(r^2+a^2)\omega-am$ and angular [separation constant](partial-differential-equation.md#separation-constant) $\Lambda$, the radial equation is $(\Delta R')'+[K^2/\Delta-a^2\omega^2+2am\omega-\Lambda]R=0$. The angular equation is $(\sin\theta\,\Theta')'/\sin\theta+[a^2\omega^2\cos^2\theta-m^2/\sin^2\theta+\Lambda]\Theta=0$. No extra radial factor is included in the displayed ansatz.

##### Scalar spheroidal harmonic

↑ **Parent:** [Scalar wave separation in Kerr spacetime](#scalar-wave-separation-in-kerr-spacetime)

A [scalar spheroidal harmonic](#scalar-spheroidal-harmonic) is a regular angular solution of $(\sin\theta\,S')'/\sin\theta+[c^2\cos^2\theta-m^2/\sin^2\theta+\Lambda]S=0$; with $e^{im\phi}$ it is smooth at both axes. For real $c$, regularity gives discrete eigenvalues $\Lambda_{\ell m}(c)$. At $c=0$ it reduces to the angular part of a [spherical harmonic](analysis.md#spherical-harmonic), with $\Lambda=\ell(\ell+1)$. The sign of the $c^2$ term distinguishes the convention used here.

#### Kerr horizon angular velocity

↑ **Parent:** [Kerr black hole](#kerr-black-hole)

The outer [Killing horizon](#killing-horizon) of a [Kerr black hole](#kerr-black-hole) is generated by $k+\Omega_Hm$, with the stationary [Killing vector field](#killing-vector-field) $k$ normalized at infinity and the axial generator $m$ having $2\pi$-periodic orbits. In [ingoing Kerr coordinates](#ingoing-kerr-coordinates) its generators satisfy $d\chi/dv=\Omega_H$. This is the horizon's angular velocity relative to the nonrotating asymptotic stationary frame.

#### Ingoing Kerr coordinates

↑ **Parent:** [Kerr black hole](#kerr-black-hole)

[Ingoing Kerr coordinates](#ingoing-kerr-coordinates) cancel the explicit inverse-$\Delta$ singularities of the [Boyer-Lindquist coordinates](#boyer-lindquist-coordinates) metric. They give a smooth, nondegenerate [Kerr metric](#kerr-metric) at the future outer horizon when $M>|a|$. Its stationary and axial [Killing vector fields](#killing-vector-field) are $\partial_v$ and $\partial_\chi$. The advanced chart need not cover every horizon branch of the maximal extension.

#### Irreducible mass

↑ **Parent:** [Kerr black hole](#kerr-black-hole)

The [irreducible mass](#irreducible-mass) of a four-dimensional uncharged [Kerr black hole](#kerr-black-hole) is $M_{\mathrm{irr}}=\sqrt{A/(16\pi)}$. Its [mass](classical-mechanics.md#mass) satisfies $M^2=M_{\mathrm{irr}}^2+J^2/(4M_{\mathrm{irr}}^2)$. [Hawking's area theorem](#hawking-s-area-theorem) prevents a classical process from decreasing $M_{\mathrm{irr}}$ under the theorem's energy and global hypotheses.

##### Area bound for an axisymmetric Kerr merger

↑ **Parent:** [Irreducible mass](#irreducible-mass)

For initially well-separated [Kerr black holes](#kerr-black-hole) with no orbital [angular momentum](classical-mechanics.md#angular-momentum), exact [axisymmetry](calculus.md#axisymmetric-vector-field) gives $J_f=J_1+J_2$. Put $X=(A_1+A_2)/(8\pi)$. The [black-hole area theorem](#hawking-s-area-theorem) and the [irreducible mass](#irreducible-mass) identity imply

$$
M_f^2\geq\frac12\left(X+\frac{(J_1+J_2)^2}{X}\right).
$$

Indeed, $X\geq|J_1|+|J_2|\geq|J_f|$, and $\tfrac12(Y+J_f^2/Y)$ is nondecreasing for $Y\geq X$. Opposite spin signs lower $J_f^2$ at fixed initial areas and hence raise the allowed radiated-energy bound; this need not be achievable in an actual merger.

#### Boyer-Lindquist coordinates

↑ **Parent:** [Kerr black hole](#kerr-black-hole)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boyer-Lindquist_coordinates)

Boyer-Lindquist coordinates adapt the Kerr metric to its stationary and axial symmetries. The horizon radii are the roots $r_\pm=M\pm\sqrt{M^2-a^2}$ of $\Delta=r^2-2Mr+a^2$.

#### Kerr ergoregion

↑ **Parent:** [Kerr black hole](#kerr-black-hole)

The Kerr ergoregion is the region where the asymptotically timelike stationary Killing field becomes spacelike. Its outer boundary is $r=M+\sqrt{M^2-a^2\cos^2\theta}$.

The Kerr realization of an [ergosphere](#ergosphere) lies outside the outer horizon where the stationary Killing vector is spacelike; its outer boundary is $r=M+\sqrt{M^2-a^2\cos^2\theta}$.

##### Kerr stationary-limit surface is generically timelike

↑ **Parent:** [Kerr ergoregion](#kerr-ergoregion)

For subextreme [Kerr spacetime](#kerr-black-hole), the outer stationary-limit surface is $s=r-r_e(\theta)=0$, where $r_e=M+\sqrt{M^2-a^2\cos^2\theta}$. Away from the rotation axis, $r_e>r_+$ and $\Delta(r_e)=a^2\sin^2\theta>0$. Its normal is therefore spacelike and the surface timelike. Although the stationary [Killing vector field](#killing-vector-field) has zero norm on it, it is not normal to it, and the surface is not a [Killing horizon](#killing-horizon). The actual outer Killing horizon is $r=r_+$, generated by $K+\Omega_H\Phi$.

##### Superradiance

↑ **Parent:** [Kerr ergoregion](#kerr-ergoregion)

[Superradiance](#superradiance) is amplification of a scattered wave by extracting rotational or other available conserved energy from a background. For a rotating [Kerr black hole](#kerr-black-hole), a bosonic mode of frequency $\omega$ and azimuthal integer $m$ can have negative horizon Killing-energy flux when $0<\omega<m\Omega_H$. The reflected energy can then exceed the incident energy. A stationary Killing flow need not define a globally positive particle energy.

##### Causal angular velocity bounds in Kerr spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Kerr ergoregion](#kerr-ergoregion)

Outside a subextremal [Kerr black hole](#kerr-black-hole), regular [causal curves](#causal-curve) obey $(-g_{t\phi}-\sqrt D)/g_{\phi\phi}\le d\phi/dt\le(-g_{t\phi}+\sqrt D)/g_{\phi\phi}$, where $D=g_{t\phi}^2-g_{tt}g_{\phi\phi}>0$. Positive radial and polar metric terms establish this necessary bound. In the open [Kerr ergoregion](#kerr-ergoregion) both endpoints are positive for $a>0$, forcing co-rotation.

##### Penrose process

↑ **Parent:** [Kerr ergoregion](#kerr-ergoregion)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penrose_process)

The Penrose process extracts rotational energy by allowing a fragment with negative stationary Killing energy to enter the black hole while another fragment escapes with more energy than the original body carried.

###### Horizon flux inequality for causal matter

↑ **Parent:** [Penrose process](#penrose-process)

For a future-directed causal four-momentum $p$ crossing a future [Killing horizon](#killing-horizon) with generator $\xi=t+\Omega_H\varphi$, the Lorentzian inner product obeys $p\cdot\xi\le0$. With $E=-p\cdot t$ and $L=p\cdot\varphi$, this is $E\ge\Omega_HL$. A massive particle has strict inequality. The same bound follows in the nonextremal quasistationary regime from the [Physical-process first law for a rotating black hole](#physical-process-first-law-for-a-rotating-black-hole) and the [second law of black-hole mechanics](#second-law-of-black-hole-mechanics). Negative Killing energy in a [Penrose process](#penrose-process) is allowed, but its angular momentum must satisfy this bound.

#### Kerr polar tortoise coordinate

↑ **Parent:** [Kerr black hole](#kerr-black-hole)

On the Kerr symmetry axis, the radial metric has $F=\Delta/(r^2+a^2)$ and the tortoise coordinate satisfies $dr_*/dr=F^{-1}=(r^2+a^2)/\Delta$.

#### Kerr black-hole heat capacity at fixed angular momentum

↑ **Parent:** [Kerr black hole](#kerr-black-hole)

Writing $s=\sqrt{1-J^2/M^4}=\sqrt{1-a^2/M^2}$, the heat capacity of a [Kerr black hole](#kerr-black-hole) at fixed angular momentum is

$$
C_J=4\pi M^2\frac{s(1+s)}{2-2s-s^2}.
$$

It is positive for $\sqrt{2\sqrt3-3}<|a|/M<1$, diverges at the lower endpoint, and tends to zero in the extremal limit.

## Stationary spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stationary_spacetime)

A stationary spacetime admits a Killing vector field that is timelike in the region representing stationary observers, in particular near infinity for an asymptotically flat stationary black hole.

### Ergoregion of a stationary spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Stationary spacetime](#stationary-spacetime)

For a chosen stationary time-translation [Killing vector field](#killing-vector-field) $k$, this is the region where $k$ is spacelike and observers following its orbits cannot be timelike. In an asymptotically flat geometry, the usual choice is the nonrotating time translation at infinity. The stationary-limit boundary has $g(k,k)=0$, but need not be an [event horizon](#event-horizon). The [Kerr ergoregion](#kerr-ergoregion) is a particular rotating-black-hole example: its outer boundary touches the horizon only on the axis for nonzero subextremal spin.

### Nonstationary spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Stationary spacetime](#stationary-spacetime)

A [nonstationary spacetime](#nonstationary-spacetime) lacks the stationary time-translation symmetry in the domain being considered. Without a preferred suitable timelike [Killing vector field](#killing-vector-field), the classical geometry alone does not determine a unique [positive-frequency solution](quantum-field-theory.md#positive-frequency-solution) subspace for quantization.

### Strictly stationary spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Stationary spacetime](#stationary-spacetime)

A strictly stationary spacetime has a globally defined Killing vector that is timelike everywhere.

### Static spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Stationary spacetime](#stationary-spacetime)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Static_spacetime)

A static spacetime has a timelike Killing vector that is hypersurface orthogonal. Coordinates adapted to it can be chosen with no time-space cross terms.

#### Static Killing field is a Ricci eigenvector

↑ **Parent:** [Static spacetime](#static-spacetime)

A timelike [Killing vector](#killing-vector-field) with [hypersurface orthogonality](differential-geometry.md#hypersurface-orthogonality) admits local coordinates with $k=\partial_t$ and $ds^2=N(\mathbf x)^2dt^2-h_{ij}(\mathbf x)dx^idx^j$. The only connection coefficients with time indices that can be nonzero are $\Gamma^0_{0i}=\partial_i\log N$ and $\Gamma^i_{00}=Nh^{ij}\partial_jN$. Substitution into the [Ricci tensor](#ricci-tensor) gives $R_{0i}=0$. Thus $R_{ab}k^b$ has only its time component and is proportional to $k_a$. Equivalently $k_dR^d{}_{[a}k_{b]}=0$. This local statement does not require the [Vacuum Einstein equations](#vacuum-einstein-equations) and is independent of the overall curvature-sign convention.

#### Static vacuum conformal spatial metric

↑ **Parent:** [Static spacetime](#static-spacetime)

Rescaling the spatial metric by the squared static [lapse function](numerical-relativity.md#lapse-function) gives a useful three-dimensional quotient metric $\gamma$. In [Vacuum Einstein equations](#vacuum-einstein-equations), its [Ricci tensor](#ricci-tensor) obeys $R_{ij}(\gamma)=2\partial_iU\,\partial_jU$, while $U$ is [harmonic](partial-differential-equation.md#harmonic-function). The [contracted Bianchi identity](#contracted-bianchi-identity) recovers the harmonic equation without dividing at critical points.

##### Horizonless static vacuum rigidity

↑ **Parent:** [Static vacuum conformal spatial metric](#static-vacuum-conformal-spatial-metric)

On a complete nonsingular spatial quotient with no inner boundary and standard [asymptotic flatness](#asymptotically-flat-spacetime), a bounded [harmonic function](partial-differential-equation.md#harmonic-function) tending to zero on all ends is zero. The [static vacuum conformal spatial metric](#static-vacuum-conformal-spatial-metric) is then [Ricci flat](differential-geometry.md#ricci-flat-manifold); [three-dimensional curvature from the Ricci tensor](#three-dimensional-curvature-from-the-ricci-tensor) makes it flat. Completeness and an ordinary Euclidean asymptotic end exclude nontrivial flat quotients, yielding [Minkowski spacetime](special-relativity.md#minkowski-spacetime) with the standard global time coordinate.

#### Conserved scalar-field energy in a static spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Static spacetime](#static-spacetime)

The [stress-energy current from a Killing vector](#stress-energy-current-from-a-killing-vector) associated with the static time translation gives a positive conserved energy for a massive real scalar. Its hypersurface independence follows from current conservation and the divergence theorem.

### Stationary axisymmetric spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [Stationary spacetime](#stationary-spacetime)

A stationary axisymmetric spacetime has commuting stationary and axial Killing vector fields, with the axial field having closed spacelike orbits.

## Arnowitt-Deser-Misner energy

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)

The Arnowitt-Deser-Misner energy is the asymptotic surface charge generating time translations on an asymptotically flat spatial hypersurface. In asymptotically Cartesian coordinates,

$$
E_{ADM}=\frac1{16\pi}\lim_{r\to\infty}\int_{S_r^2}n^i(\partial_jh_{ij}-\partial_ih_{jj})\,dA.
$$

The Arnowitt-Deser-Misner energy is the asymptotic Hamiltonian boundary charge in the [ADM formalism](numerical-relativity.md#adm-formalism) for an asymptotically flat spacetime.

### Arnowitt-Deser-Misner mass

↑ **Parent:** [Arnowitt-Deser-Misner energy](#arnowitt-deser-misner-energy)

For an asymptotically flat stationary solution in its asymptotic rest frame, the [ADM mass](#arnowitt-deser-misner-mass) is the gravitational mass measured by the [Arnowitt-Deser-Misner energy](#arnowitt-deser-misner-energy). It is fixed by the asymptotic metric falloff, not by a coordinate parameter's name. If spatial ADM momentum is present, distinguish the asymptotic energy component from the invariant mass obtained from the energy-momentum norm. For a static [Schwarzschild-Tangherlini metric](#schwarzschild-tangherlini-metric) in $D$ dimensions with $f=1-\mu/r^{D-3}$, the mass is $(D-2)\Omega_{D-2}\mu/(16\pi G_D)$; the dimensional normalization is also reproduced by the [Komar mass normalization in higher dimensions](#komar-mass-normalization-in-higher-dimensions).

### ADM momentum

↑ **Parent:** [Arnowitt-Deser-Misner energy](#arnowitt-deser-misner-energy)

For an asymptotically flat spatial metric $h$ and $k_{ij}=-g(\nabla_i n,e_j)$, the displayed surface integral gives ADM spatial momentum. Together with [ADM energy](#arnowitt-deser-misner-energy) it forms the asymptotic energy-momentum four-vector. Reversing the sign convention for extrinsic curvature reverses the corresponding surface-integral sign.

### Positive energy theorem

↑ **Parent:** [Arnowitt-Deser-Misner energy](#arnowitt-deser-misner-energy)

Complete asymptotically flat [spin](quantum-mechanics.md#spin) initial data obeying the Einstein constraints and the [dominant energy condition](#dominant-energy-condition) have future-causal ADM four-momentum, under the appropriate decay and boundary hypotheses. A spinorial proof solves the [Witten spinor equation](#witten-spinor-equation) and integrates the [Nester two-form](#nester-two-form). Its divergence is a sum of a nonnegative spinor-gradient term and a matter-energy contraction with a future-causal [Dirac current](quantum-field-theory.md#dirac-current). Positivity for every asymptotic constant spinor yields the full inequality, rather than merely nonnegative energy in one frame.

#### Parallel spinors and vanishing ADM mass

↑ **Parent:** [Positive energy theorem](#positive-energy-theorem)

A nonzero spacetime [parallel spinor](connection-1-form.md#parallel-spinor) makes the [Nester two-form](#nester-two-form) vanish, so its ADM boundary charge vanishes. For complete regular spin initial data in an [asymptotically flat spacetime](#asymptotically-flat-spacetime) under the [dominant energy condition](#dominant-energy-condition), the ADM momentum is future causal. Its contraction with the [spinor](algebra.md#spinor)'s nonzero future causal [Dirac current](quantum-field-theory.md#dirac-current) cannot vanish if the momentum is timelike. Thus the invariant ADM [mass](classical-mechanics.md#mass) is zero; in a rest frame this gives zero energy. Local parallel-spinor existence without asymptotic/global hypotheses does not establish this [mass](classical-mechanics.md#mass) conclusion.

#### Static zero-energy parallel-spinor rigidity

↑ **Parent:** [Positive energy theorem](#positive-energy-theorem)

The vacuum [Witten-Nester identity](#witten-nester-identity) makes a zero-energy Witten spinor parallel under the [Sen spinor connection](#sen-spinor-connection) on a complete asymptotically flat slice. In an orthogonal static slicing, extrinsic curvature vanishes, so this spinor is intrinsically parallel. Contracting its spin-curvature integrability condition gives zero spatial [Ricci tensor](#ricci-tensor); in three dimensions this implies flatness. The static vacuum equation $D_iD_jN=N R_{ij}(h)$ then gives a parallel lapse gradient, which asymptotic normalization forces to vanish. The time-independent spinor consequently extends to a spacetime [parallel spinor](connection-1-form.md#parallel-spinor), with [Minkowski spacetime](special-relativity.md#minkowski-spacetime) the complete regular asymptotically flat example.

#### Nester two-form

↑ **Parent:** [Positive energy theorem](#positive-energy-theorem)

This antisymmetric spinor bilinear converts the [positive energy theorem](#positive-energy-theorem) into a boundary-charge identity. Its divergence is $2(\nabla_\nu\bar\epsilon)\gamma^{\mu\nu\rho}\nabla_\rho\epsilon+G^{\mu\nu}\bar\epsilon\gamma_\nu\epsilon$ in the mostly-plus conventions specified here. On a spacelike hypersurface the first term is twice the difference between the squared spatial [covariant derivative](#covariant-derivative) and the squared spatial Dirac operator. The [Witten spinor equation](#witten-spinor-equation) removes the negative square, while the [dominant energy condition](#dominant-energy-condition) controls the [curvature](differential-geometry.md#curvature) term through the Einstein constraints.

##### ADM boundary term of the Nester two-form

↑ **Parent:** [Nester two-form](#nester-two-form)

For asymptotically constant spinors, the leading intrinsic [spin connection](connection-1-form.md#spin-connection) term gives half the ADM metric integrand times $K^0_\infty$. The [Sen spinor connection](#sen-spinor-connection) correction gives $-(k_{ij}-kh_{ij})K^j_\infty$. The constant-spinor derivative term integrates to zero by antisymmetry of the gamma matrices, and the remaining quadratic terms vanish under the standard decay. This identifies the [Nester two-form](#nester-two-form) flux with the ADM energy-momentum contraction, including momentum rather than just energy.

##### Witten-Nester identity

↑ **Parent:** [Nester two-form](#nester-two-form)

In mostly-plus signature orient the commuting [Dirac current](quantum-field-theory.md#dirac-current) to the future by $\bar\epsilon=-\epsilon^\dagger\gamma^{\hat0}$. For the [Nester two-form](#nester-two-form), put $b^i=n_\mu B^{\mu i}$. Its covariant divergence has a spinor-gradient term and the [Einstein tensor contraction of spin curvature](connection-1-form.md#einstein-tensor-contraction-of-spin-curvature). Projecting onto the hypersurface gives the displayed identity with positive spinor norm. The [Witten spinor equation](#witten-spinor-equation) removes the negative square, and the [dominant energy condition](#dominant-energy-condition) makes the matter term nonnegative after the Einstein constraints are imposed.

##### Witten spinor equation

↑ **Parent:** [Nester two-form](#nester-two-form)

This elliptic equation on a spacelike [spin](quantum-mechanics.md#spin) hypersurface uses the restriction of the spacetime [spin connection](connection-1-form.md#spin-connection), including [extrinsic curvature](differential-geometry.md#extrinsic-curvature). The spinor tends to a prescribed constant at spatial infinity. It removes the negative derivative square in the divergence of the [Nester two-form](#nester-two-form). It is weaker than the spacetime [Killing spinor](supersymmetry.md#killing-spinor) equation; generic positive-energy initial data need not admit any [Killing spinor](supersymmetry.md#killing-spinor).

###### Elliptic existence of a Witten spinor

↑ **Parent:** [Witten spinor equation](#witten-spinor-equation)

On complete smooth asymptotically flat [spin](quantum-mechanics.md#spin) initial data without inner boundary, satisfying the Einstein constraints and the [dominant energy condition](#dominant-energy-condition), the [Witten spinor equation](#witten-spinor-equation) admits a spinor approaching each prescribed asymptotic constant, under the standard weighted decay assumptions. The integrated [Witten-Nester identity](#witten-nester-identity) gives a coercive estimate and excludes a decaying homogeneous kernel. Weighted elliptic solvability then supplies the desired correction to a spinor extended from infinity. Positivity of the boundary integral is a conditional identity until this analytic existence step is supplied; see [the rigorous analytic proof](https://users.math.msu.edu/users/parker/witten.pdf).

###### Sen spinor connection

↑ **Parent:** [Witten spinor equation](#witten-spinor-equation)

Restrict the spacetime [spin connection](connection-1-form.md#spin-connection) to a spacelike hypersurface. With future unit normal $n$ and convention $k_{ij}=-g(\nabla_i n,e_j)$, it differs from the intrinsic spin connection by the displayed [extrinsic curvature](differential-geometry.md#extrinsic-curvature) term. Its spatial [Dirac operator](riemannian-geometry.md#dirac-operator) is the operator in the [Witten spinor equation](#witten-spinor-equation); replacing it by the intrinsic operator would omit the ADM momentum contribution.

### Differentiability of the gravitational Hamiltonian at spatial infinity

↑ **Parent:** [Arnowitt-Deser-Misner energy](#arnowitt-deser-misner-energy)

For asymptotically Cartesian spatial metrics $h_{ij}=\delta_{ij}+O(r^{-1})$, unit asymptotic lapse and vanishing asymptotic shift, the curvature term in the bulk gravitational [Hamiltonian](classical-mechanics.md#hamiltonian) has variation

$$
\delta H_{\rm bulk}=\text{bulk functional derivatives}-\frac1{16\pi G}\lim_{r\to\infty}\int_{S_r}n^i(\partial_j\delta h_{ij}-\partial_i\delta h_{jj})\,dA.
$$

The surface variation is finite under the usual differentiable asymptotic falloff and is not removed by simply taking the boundary to infinity. Adding [Arnowitt-Deser-Misner energy](#arnowitt-deser-misner-energy) cancels it and makes [Hamilton's equations](classical-mechanics.md#hamilton-s-equations) well defined for allowed variations that need not fix the energy. On the constraint surface, the total Hamiltonian for that time translation equals the boundary energy.

## Numerical relativity

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)

[This section is present in another page, follow this link to view it.](numerical-relativity.md)

## Kasner metric

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kasner_metric)

The Kasner metric is the anisotropic vacuum cosmology

$$
ds^2=-dt^2+\sum_{i=1}^3t^{2p_i}(dx^i)^2,
$$

where the vacuum Einstein equations require $\sum_i p_i=\sum_i p_i^2=1$.

<h2 id="bondi-sachs-formalism">Bondi--Sachs formalism</h2>

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bondi--Sachs_formalism)

The Bondi--Sachs formalism uses outgoing null hypersurfaces to describe asymptotically flat radiating spacetimes.

### Bondi news function

↑ **Parent:** [Bondi--Sachs formalism](#bondi-sachs-formalism)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bondi_news_function)

The Bondi news is the retarded-time derivative of the leading asymptotic shear and measures outgoing gravitational radiation at null infinity.

## Asymptotically flat spacetime

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asymptotically_flat_spacetime)

An asymptotically flat spacetime approaches [Minkowski spacetime](special-relativity.md#minkowski-spacetime) sufficiently far from isolated sources. At null infinity this is stated invariantly by requiring a smooth [conformal completion](#conformal-completion) whose boundary contains future and past null infinity.

### Asymptotic simplicity

↑ **Parent:** [Asymptotically flat spacetime](#asymptotically-flat-spacetime)

[Asymptotic simplicity](#asymptotic-simplicity) requires a suitable smooth conformal completion with $\widetilde g=\Omega^2g$, $\Omega>0$ in the physical spacetime, and $\Omega=0$, $d\Omega\ne0$ on null infinity, such that every inextendible null geodesic has both past and future endpoints there. It is stronger than merely possessing an asymptotically flat exterior. Null geodesics ending on singularities or on omitted finite-affine-distance horizons prevent this global condition.

### Conformal completion

↑ **Parent:** [Asymptotically flat spacetime](#asymptotically-flat-spacetime)

A conformal completion embeds a physical spacetime $(\mathcal M,g)$ into a manifold with boundary carrying a regular metric $\bar g=\Omega^2g$. Infinity is represented by $\Omega=0$ with $d\Omega\ne0$; the causal character of each boundary component is determined by $\bar g^{ab}\bar\nabla_a\Omega\bar\nabla_b\Omega$.

This spacetime boundary construction is a [conformal compactification](geometry-and-topology.md#conformal-compactification), not an arbitrary topological compactification.

#### Minkowski conformal compactification

↑ **Parent:** [Conformal completion](#conformal-completion)

For four-dimensional [Minkowski spacetime](special-relativity.md#minkowski-spacetime), set $p=\arctan((t-r)/L)$ and $q=\arctan((t+r)/L)$, with $L>0$, then $T=p+q$, $R=q-p$. Multiplication by the square of the [conformal factor](#conformal-factor) $2\cos p\cos q/L$ yields the displayed metric on $R\geq0$, $|T|+R<\pi$. Its radial [Penrose diagram](#penrose-diagram) is a triangle: the timelike edge $R=0$ is the ordinary centre, while the two sloping edges are [future null infinity](#future-null-infinity) and [past null infinity](#past-null-infinity). A signed Cartesian spatial coordinate in two dimensions gives a full diamond instead.

##### Geodesic endpoints in Minkowski conformal compactification

↑ **Parent:** [Minkowski conformal compactification](#minkowski-conformal-compactification)

A complete inertial [timelike geodesic](#timelike-geodesic) in [Minkowski spacetime](special-relativity.md#minkowski-spacetime) has $\mathbf x=\mathbf b+\mathbf vt$ with $|\mathbf v|<1$. Under $p=\arctan(t-r)$, $q=\arctan(t+r)$, both $p,q$ tend to the same endpoint, giving [past timelike infinity](#past-timelike-infinity) and [future timelike infinity](#future-timelike-infinity). A complete [null geodesic](special-relativity.md#null-geodesic) has one finite limiting null coordinate at each end and reaches [past null infinity](#past-null-infinity) and [future null infinity](#future-null-infinity). Complete [spacelike geodesics](riemannian-geometry.md#spacelike-geodesic) have $r>|t|$ asymptotically and both ends approach [spacelike infinity](#spacelike-infinity). The endpoints have finite compactified coordinates but occur at infinite physical [affine parameter](riemannian-geometry.md#affine-parameter). A [conformal transformation](geometry-and-topology.md#conformal-map) preserves null paths as unparametrized geodesics; it does not generally preserve timelike or spacelike geodesic paths.

##### Projective Minkowski compactification

↑ **Parent:** [Minkowski conformal compactification](#minkowski-conformal-compactification)

The Lorentzian real form of the [Klein quadric](differential-geometry.md#klein-quadric) is the projectivized real null cone of signature $(2,4)$. Scaling a nonzero null vector makes its positive two-dimensional and negative four-dimensional parts both unit vectors. Real projectivization then identifies $(u,v)$ with $(-u,-v)$, yielding the displayed quotient. Its universal cover is $\mathbb R\times S^3$, and its fundamental group is infinite cyclic. An affine Minkowski chart omits a null cone at infinity. This projective compact space is distinct from an unwrapped causal cover used for a [Penrose diagram](#penrose-diagram).

#### Future null infinity

↑ **Parent:** [Conformal completion](#conformal-completion)

Future null infinity is the boundary reached by future-directed outgoing null geodesics in an [asymptotically flat spacetime](#asymptotically-flat-spacetime). In four dimensions it has topology $\mathbb R\times S^2$ under the standard asymptotic-flatness assumptions.

It is the future component of [null infinity](#null-infinity); the past component is $\mathcal I^-$.

## Gravitational lensing

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gravitational_lensing)

Gravitational lensing is the deflection and focusing of light by spacetime curvature along the line of sight.

### Mass-sheet degeneracy

↑ **Parent:** [Gravitational lensing](#gravitational-lensing)

For a single source plane, adding a uniform sheet and rescaling the original projected lens mass as shown leaves the reduced shear $g=\gamma/(1-\kappa)$ unchanged. Rescaling source coordinates by $\lambda$ also preserves the image positions and shapes. Thus image geometry alone need not determine absolute lens mass normalization. Absolute magnification, independently known source sizes, time-delay information with a fixed distance scale, or additional geometric/dynamical constraints can break or restrict this [mass-sheet degeneracy](#mass-sheet-degeneracy).

### Gravitational lens

↑ **Parent:** [Gravitational lensing](#gravitational-lensing)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gravitational_lens)

A gravitational lens is a foreground distribution of matter whose gravitational field deflects light from a background source. Its [projected surface mass density](fluid-mechanics.md#projected-surface-mass-density) determines the mapping in the thin-lens approximation. The lens is the deflecting object; [gravitational lensing](#gravitational-lensing) is the resulting optical process.

### Lensing magnification

↑ **Parent:** [Gravitational lensing](#gravitational-lensing)

The signed magnification is the reciprocal determinant of the [Jacobian matrix](calculus.md#jacobian-matrix) of the [thin gravitational lens equation](#thin-gravitational-lens-equation). Its absolute value gives the flux and solid-angle amplification; its sign gives image parity. [Gravitational lensing](#gravitational-lensing) preserves [surface brightness](astrophysics.md#surface-brightness) in the geometric-optics approximation.

### Thin gravitational lens equation

↑ **Parent:** [Gravitational lensing](#gravitational-lensing)

A thin lens maps apparent image angle $\boldsymbol\theta$ to unlensed source angle $\boldsymbol\beta$. In the usual outward reduced-deflection convention, $\boldsymbol\alpha=\nabla_\theta\psi$ for a projected lensing potential. For an axisymmetric lens its magnitude is $4GM_{\rm projected}(<D_d\theta)D_{ds}/(c^2D_dD_s\theta)$. The mass here is projected inside a cylinder, not the three-dimensional mass inside a sphere.

#### Softened isothermal lensing potential

↑ **Parent:** [Thin gravitational lens equation](#thin-gravitational-lens-equation)

This circular lensing potential has reduced deflection $\alpha(r)=\theta_Er/\sqrt{r^2+\theta_c^2}$, mean [lensing convergence](#lensing-convergence) $\bar\kappa=\theta_E/\sqrt{r^2+\theta_c^2}$ and local convergence $\kappa=\theta_E(\theta_c^2+r^2/2)/(r^2+\theta_c^2)^{3/2}$. Its central convergence is finite for $\theta_c>0$. As the core shrinks to zero, the deflection tends to that of a [singular isothermal sphere lens](galaxy.md#singular-isothermal-sphere-lens). This softening of the projected lensing potential must not be confused with replacing a three-dimensional density by a different cored profile.

##### Multiple-image caustic of a softened isothermal lensing potential

↑ **Parent:** [Softened isothermal lensing potential](#softened-isothermal-lensing-potential)

For $0<\theta_c<\theta_E$, the signed circular lens mapping $\beta=\theta[1-\theta_E/\sqrt{\theta^2+\theta_c^2}]$ has radial [stationary points](calculus-of-variations.md#stationary-point) at $\theta_r^2=(\theta_E\theta_c^2)^{2/3}-\theta_c^2$. Substituting them gives the displayed caustic radius. An offset [point-like astronomical source](astrophysics.md#point-like-astronomical-source) has three images when $0<|\beta|<\beta_c$, a merging pair plus a separate outer image on the fold, and one image outside. At alignment, the two off-centre images form a ring while a central image remains. For $\theta_c\geq\theta_E$ the mapping is monotone and there is no finite multiple-image region. The zero-core singular limit loses the central image and has a different image-count exception.

### Einstein radius

↑ **Parent:** [Gravitational lensing](#gravitational-lensing)

For a point lens of [mass](classical-mechanics.md#mass) $m$ at observer distance $D_l$ and source distance $D_s$ in a nearby Euclidean geometry, the [Einstein radius](#einstein-radius) in the lens plane is $R_E=\sqrt{4GmD_l(D_s-D_l)/(c^2D_s)}$. Its angular version is $\theta_E=R_E/D_l$. This is the ring radius for perfect alignment and the characteristic transverse scale of a [gravitational microlensing](#gravitational-microlensing) event. A crossing timescale is $t_E=R_E/v_\perp$, proportional to the square root of lens mass at fixed geometry and speed.

### Weak gravitational lensing

↑ **Parent:** [Gravitational lensing](#gravitational-lensing)

When [gravitational lensing](#gravitational-lensing) gives small angular distortions without generic multiple imaging, its deflection field changes source positions, magnification and shear. Angular derivatives of the lensing potential determine these distortions. Ensemble galaxy-shape distortions and [cosmic microwave background](cosmology.md#cosmic-microwave-background) remapping probe the foreground gravitational field.

#### Cosmic shear

↑ **Parent:** [Weak gravitational lensing](#weak-gravitational-lensing)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cosmic_shear)

Cosmic shear is the statistically correlated [lensing shear](#lensing-shear) of distant [galaxies](galaxy.md) produced by the intervening large-scale matter distribution. Correlations across angle and source redshift constrain clustering, the growth of structure and distance geometry, thereby testing dark matter and dark energy. Intrinsic galaxy alignments and measurement errors must be separated from the lensing signal.

#### Lensing shear

↑ **Parent:** [Weak gravitational lensing](#weak-gravitational-lensing)

The trace-free part of the angular lensing-potential Hessian stretches an image anisotropically. The source-image [Jacobian matrix](calculus.md#jacobian-matrix) has eigenvalues $1-\kappa\pm|\gamma|$. Thus a circular source becomes an ellipse in the local linear approximation; the orientation follows the shear axes.

#### Lensing convergence

↑ **Parent:** [Weak gravitational lensing](#weak-gravitational-lensing)

The trace of the angular derivative of the reduced deflection defines convergence in the standard [thin gravitational lens equation](#thin-gravitational-lens-equation) convention. It equals projected [surface mass density](fluid-mechanics.md#projected-surface-mass-density) divided by the critical surface density, $\Sigma_{\rm crit}=c^2D_s/(4\pi GD_dD_{ds})$. If deflection is instead defined as source minus image angle, the divergence relation has a minus sign.

#### Angular deflection kernel for cosmological lensing

↑ **Parent:** [Weak gravitational lensing](#weak-gravitational-lensing)

For a source at comoving distance $\chi_s$ in a spatially flat universe, define deflection as apparent minus unlensed source direction. The transverse [Weyl lensing potential](linear-cosmological-perturbation-theory.md#weyl-lensing-potential) gradient along the ray receives the distance weight $(\chi_s-\chi)/\chi_s$. Integrating the transverse photon acceleration twice and dividing the source displacement by $\chi_s$ gives the formula. Reversing the definition of the deflection reverses its sign. Its angular lensing potential is $2\int_0^{\chi_s}(\chi_s-\chi)\Psi/(\chi_s\chi)\,d\chi$.

### Born approximation in cosmological lensing

↑ **Parent:** [Gravitational lensing](#gravitational-lensing)

In a first-order light-deflection calculation, evaluate the [Weyl lensing potential](linear-cosmological-perturbation-theory.md#weyl-lensing-potential) and its gradients on the unperturbed [null geodesic](special-relativity.md#null-geodesic). The position correction is first order, so inserting it into an already first-order potential produces a second-order correction. This approximation omits repeated deflection and lens-lens coupling.

### Strong gravitational lensing

↑ **Parent:** [Gravitational lensing](#gravitational-lensing)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strong_gravitational_lensing)

Strong gravitational lensing produces multiple resolved images, arcs, or rings when a sufficiently massive foreground lens lies close to the source's line of sight.

#### Gravitational-lensing critical curve

↑ **Parent:** [Strong gravitational lensing](#strong-gravitational-lensing)

A critical curve is a locus in the image plane where the [Jacobian matrix](calculus.md#jacobian-matrix) of the [thin gravitational lens equation](#thin-gravitational-lens-equation) becomes singular. Its formal point-source [lensing magnification](#lensing-magnification) diverges, while finite source size regularizes the measured flux. Its mapped locus in the source plane is a [gravitational-lensing caustic](#gravitational-lensing-caustic).

##### Radial critical curve of an axisymmetric lens

↑ **Parent:** [Gravitational-lensing critical curve](#gravitational-lensing-critical-curve)

The local and mean [lensing convergence](#lensing-convergence) obey $2\kappa=2\bar\kappa+r\bar\kappa'$. Hence the radial [derivative](calculus.md#derivative) of the lens mapping is $1-\alpha'(r)=1-2\kappa+\bar\kappa$. Its zeros are radial [gravitational-lensing critical curves](#gravitational-lensing-critical-curve), where radial stretching diverges and images merge on crossing the mapped [gravitational-lensing caustic](#gravitational-lensing-caustic).

##### Tangential critical curve of an axisymmetric lens

↑ **Parent:** [Gravitational-lensing critical curve](#gravitational-lensing-critical-curve)

For circular reduced deflection $\alpha(r)=r\bar\kappa(r)$, a displacement perpendicular to the radial direction is multiplied by $1-\bar\kappa$. Vanishing of this [eigenvalue](linear-operator-theory.md#eigenvalue) gives a tangential [gravitational-lensing critical curve](#gravitational-lensing-critical-curve). Its source position is $\beta=r-\alpha(r)=0$, so its caustic is the origin in the circular model. Near it, images are stretched tangentially into arcs or an [Einstein ring](#einstein-ring).

##### Gravitational-lensing caustic

↑ **Parent:** [Gravitational-lensing critical curve](#gravitational-lensing-critical-curve)

A caustic is the source-plane image of a [gravitational-lensing critical curve](#gravitational-lensing-critical-curve). Crossing a generic fold caustic creates or destroys a pair of images of opposite parity. For a circular lens, a tangential critical circle maps to an aligned-source point, while a radial critical circle can map to a finite source-plane circle. Critical curves and caustics consequently occupy different planes and must not be identified as the same angular locus.

#### Einstein ring

↑ **Parent:** [Strong gravitational lensing](#strong-gravitational-lensing)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Einstein_ring)

An aligned source behind an axisymmetric [gravitational lens](#gravitational-lens) produces a circular image at an [Einstein radius](#einstein-radius). Its angular radius satisfies $\theta_E^2=4GM_{\rm projected}(<D_d\theta_E)D_{ds}/(c^2D_dD_s)$. Exact alignment gives a ring; displaced sources generally give arcs or distinct images rather than a complete ring.

### Gravitational microlensing

↑ **Parent:** [Gravitational lensing](#gravitational-lensing)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gravitational_microlensing)

Gravitational microlensing is lensing by compact masses whose individual image splitting is unresolved, so their motion is observed through time-dependent magnification.

#### Microlensing optical depth

↑ **Parent:** [Gravitational microlensing](#gravitational-microlensing)

The [microlensing optical depth](#microlensing-optical-depth) is the probability, when small, that a source lies within one [Einstein radius](#einstein-radius) of a foreground lens. For point lenses and compact-object [mass density](fluid-mechanics.md#density) $\rho_l$ in nearby Euclidean geometry,

$$
\tau=\frac{4\pi G}{c^2}\int_0^{D_s}\rho_l(D_l)\frac{D_l(D_s-D_l)}{D_s}\,dD_l.
$$

The individual lens mass cancels because $n_l=\rho_l/m$ and $R_E^2\propto m$. Detection efficiencies and event durations still depend on individual masses and survey cadence.

## Perfect fluid

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perfect_fluid)

A perfect fluid has no viscosity or heat flux in its local rest frame; its stress is specified by energy density and isotropic pressure. Its relativistic realization is the [perfect fluid in general relativity](#perfect-fluid-in-general-relativity).

## Eddington-Finkelstein coordinates

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eddington–Finkelstein_coordinates)

The advanced time $v=t+r_*$ or retarded time $u=t-r_*$ combines Schwarzschild time with the [Schwarzschild tortoise coordinate](#schwarzschild-tortoise-coordinate) to remove one horizon-coordinate singularity. The advanced choice gives [Ingoing Eddington-Finkelstein coordinates](#ingoing-eddington-finkelstein-coordinates); the retarded choice is outgoing.

## Conformally flat manifold

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conformally_flat_manifold)

A (pseudo-)[Riemannian manifold](riemannian-geometry.md#riemannian-manifold) is conformally flat if every point has a neighborhood on which the metric is $g=\Omega^2\eta$ for a flat metric $\eta$ and a positive function $\Omega$. This is a local property and does not imply global flatness.

## Kerr metric

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerr_metric)

The Kerr metric is the stationary axisymmetric vacuum metric of a rotating mass. Parameters $M$ and $a$ determine whether its geometry has the horizons of a [Kerr black hole](#kerr-black-hole); the metric also admits over-extremal parameters with no horizon.

## Ergosphere

↑ **Parent:** [General relativity](general-relativity.md)  
ⓦ [Wiki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ergosphere)

An ergosphere is the region outside a rotating black hole horizon in which the stationary time-translation [Killing vector](#killing-vector-field) is spacelike. Observers cannot remain stationary relative to infinity there. The [Kerr ergoregion](#kerr-ergoregion) is its Kerr-metric instance.
